# Data Hub design principles

Status: first version, 22 October 2026. Built in a design session with Max.

This file is the source of truth for **the rules** of the design. The **values** (colours, sizes, durations of transitions) live only in [`tokens.css`](tokens.css). The **screens** live in [`screens/`](screens/). Confluence describes this and links to it, but does not duplicate it.

Working agreement: when a rule changes, change this file first, then the tokens and the code.

Its decisions and open points carry IDs with the code `UX`, numbered and linked like the requirements ([requirements conventions](../requirements/README.md)). The web app does not follow this design yet; [OP-UX-07](#op-ux-07) to [OP-UX-13](#op-ux-13) are where it differs from what is decided or built today.

## Sources of truth

| What | Where |
| --- | --- |
| Rules, navigation behaviour, decisions | this file |
| Style values | [`tokens.css`](tokens.css) |
| Components: their groups, behaviour and dependencies | [`components.md`](components.md); their look in [`base.css`](base.css) (prototype), later the components in `packages/ui` |
| Screens | [`screens/*.html`](screens/), each with the requirements it fulfils |

Data Hub is an internal tool and has no Figma. The design lives in code ([DEC-UX-01](#dec-ux-01)).

## General

- The interface is in **Dutch** by default, with English available; names stay English and are never translated ([DEC-DH-65](../overview/data-hub.md#dec-dh-65), [localisation](../requirements/non-functional/localisation.md)). *Note of 2026-10-25: this replaces "the interface is in English". The prototypes still show the interface in English.*
- **Easy to do right, hard to do wrong** ([DEC-UX-207](#dec-ux-207)): good software is like good governance. The right step is the obvious one, offered where it is taken, to whoever can take it, with why; the wrong one takes an effort. Every requirement says which behaviour it makes easy and which it makes hard ([Adding a requirement](../requirements/README.md#adding-a-requirement)).
- **Fields keep the order of the Resource Card** wherever they are listed, unless a clearly stated reason asks for another ([DEC-UX-323](#dec-ux-323)), so that a User finds a field where they found it before.
- **Calm and air:** plenty of negative space. Rather less on a screen than a full screen.
- **Full screen.** The content uses the full width, divided into blocks, never long rows or lines across the full width.
- **No password manager** offers itself on a field ([DEC-UX-211](#dec-ux-211)): Data Hub asks for no password, as Google signs Users in, so every field where one types tells 1Password, LastPass, Bitwarden and Dashlane that it is not theirs. A check box or a radio button needs nothing.
- **Loading:** while a page, a panel, a tab, a sheet or a tile reads what it shows, three dots go back and forth where it will appear, in the muted colour; after a moment only, so that a quick answer shows none, and standing still with Reduce motion. A small space, such as a count, takes small dots in its line. What has a sentence of its own keeps it next to the dots, such as the lineage being read. Never a spinner over the whole app ([DEC-UX-165](#dec-ux-165)).

## Layers and focus

There are three layers:

1. **Background:** a slow transition between three warm colours (sand, blush, mist), or a picture painted as a watercolour ([DEC-UX-34](#dec-ux-34)).
2. **Frames:** the tab of the active navigation icon, the panel and the main area. Slightly transparent, they blend into the background.
3. **Reading surfaces:** everything with text on it that you actually read. Fully opaque (white, or warm grey within a white surface).

**Focus follows the mouse.** The window the mouse is in gets fully opaque reading surfaces. The other windows become slightly softer, their text and all: the whole window fades a little ([DEC-UX-146](#dec-ux-146)).

- By default the focus is on the main area, because that is where the actual work happens.
- The transition is slow and sweeps; it does not switch on and off (`--motion-focus`).
- A short delay (`--delay-focus`) makes sure that a mouse that only passes by sets nothing in motion.
- On touch, hover does not exist: there the window tapped last gets the focus.

## Colour

- **Colour only on content** (charts, Resources), **never on navigation**.
- Hover and active are **shades of grey**, not colour.
- The palette is **warm grey**. A deliberate deviation from the cool greys in the brandbook, because Data Hub is an internal tool.
- Text is **warm dark grey, never black**.
- **Collections have no colour:** no stripe, no line at the head of their page, no colour on their chip. Their grey icon sets them apart ([DEC-UX-105](#dec-ux-105)).
- **Signal colour** (ochre) only for a state that calls for action, such as a Level that has dropped.
- **The values in the picture of a monitor** are three shades of olive (`--seg-1` to `--seg-3`): they stand for several values, never one each, and judge nothing; a judgement has the colour of its Tone ([DEC-UX-197](#dec-ux-197)).
- **The ribbon of an environment** is teal (`--env-ribbon`), on every screen of an environment that is not prod; teal means nothing else in Data Hub, so it never reads as a state of the data ([DEC-UX-203](#dec-ux-203)).
- **Urgent colour** (red, `--signal-urgent`) only for "Without a collection": Resources that wait for the Data Team ([DEC-UX-16](#dec-ux-16)); and for the button of a run that reads above the approval threshold ([DEC-UX-28](#dec-ux-28)); and for what is Broken: its red dot, chip and callout ([DEC-UX-81](#dec-ux-81)).

## Themes

- Light and dark. Dark is **warm dark grey** ([OP-UX-09](#op-ux-09)).
- The theme always follows the system, also while Data Hub is open: when the system turns to night, so does Data Hub ([DEC-UX-172](#dec-ux-172)). The switch at the foot of the rail turns it for the session only; nothing is remembered, and the next change of the system, or opening Data Hub again, follows the system once more.
- The switch shows where it goes: the sun by night, the moon by day ([DEC-UX-172](#dec-ux-172)).
- A switch happens at once: for that moment every transition is off, so nothing slides from day into night ([DEC-UX-149](#dec-ux-149)).

## Typography

- **Poppins**, with its weights for hierarchy: regular for text, medium for titles and names, semibold sparingly.
- One large page title, the rest small and calm.
- Technical names (Keys, columns, Log IDs) in a monospace font.

## Long names

*A proposal of 2026-11-23, at the request of Max, in CSS alone; its open points are [OP-UX-120](#op-ux-120) to [OP-UX-122](#op-ux-122).* Screen: [long-names](screens/long-names.html), with real names from uat in four columns that can be dragged narrower or wider.

- **What matters is often at the end:** "Credit leads, current (flat)", "…, monthly", the date of a query, the table after its dataset and the part after the last `__`. A browser cuts a name at its end, which cuts exactly that.
- **In CSS alone:** the browser fits a name; no code measures it. Four ways, side by side:
  1. **Cut the end**, "…" at the end: the way of today.
  2. **Two lines, then cut:** a name wraps once, a Key breaks anywhere; only what passes two lines is cut, and rows differ in height.
  3. **Cut the start**, "…" at the start: the box runs right to left, so the end stays in view; the name itself still reads left to right.
  4. **Keep the end whole:** the name comes in two parts, split once at its last comma, or a Key at its last `__`; the first part shrinks with "…", the last does not.
- **Whatever is chosen:** the whole name shows on hover, search finds any part of it, and a Key keeps its copy button where it has one.

## Shape

- **Rounding only, never shadow.** Shadow does not work with transparent surfaces.
- Concave corners where two surfaces flow into each other: the active navigation icon into the panel (`--flare-nav`), the active tab into its content (`--flare-tab`).

## Icons

- One family: **Phosphor**.
- **Filled icons** for everything of the interface.
- **Line icons** for what the User chooses, such as the icon of a Collection, in the **light** weight; for now Data Hub uses no other weight ([OP-CO-17](../requirements/functional/03-collections.md#op-co-17), [DEC-CO-19](../requirements/functional/03-collections.md#dec-co-19)). The icon of a Collection is **grey, without a circle** ([DEC-UX-15](#dec-ux-15)), and the only thing that sets it apart: a Collection has no colour ([DEC-UX-105](#dec-ux-105)).

*The web app rebuilt in Svelte no longer shows the icons of Tabler; it starts from zero with Phosphor, so no icon is moved ([OP-CO-16](../requirements/functional/03-collections.md#op-co-16)). The prototypes still draw those icons in the regular weight.*

- A Resource has **no icon of its own**: icons are for the interface and for Collections ([DEC-UX-06](#dec-ux-06)).
- In a list, an item has the **icon of its kind**: a table for a Resource, the same for every Resource. That icon is part of the interface ([DEC-UX-11](#dec-ux-11)).

## Navigation

- **On the left, floating:** the icons have no bar or background of their own. Five main items on top: Collections, Query, Charts, the gallery of semgraph, Messages, with the number unread ([DEC-UX-29](#dec-ux-29)), and the learning centre ([DEC-UX-89](#dec-ux-89), [DEC-UX-17](#dec-ux-17)). No Lenses and no Reports: they live in their Collections ([DEC-UX-66](#dec-ux-66)). For the Data Team, Admin follows them, a little apart ([DEC-UX-130](#dec-ux-130)). At the bottom, the switch of the theme, the sun by night and the moon by day ([DEC-UX-172](#dec-ux-172)), and the initials that open My page ([DEC-UX-84](#dec-ux-84)). There is no button for settings ([DEC-UX-53](#dec-ux-53)).
- **Collapsed,** each icon shows a label after a short delay. The active icon keeps its transparent tile.
- **A slide-out panel per main item.** The active icon and the panel form one shape: the icon has the same background as the panel and flows into it with concave corners.

### Navigation rules

1. **Click on another main item:** you go to that section and its panel slides open. The panel always belongs to the active section.
2. **Click on the main item you are already in:** the panel slides in or out. The button in the panel does the same.
3. **Click on an item in the panel:** the detail opens on the right; the panel stays.
4. **Click on a Collection in the panel:** its page opens on the right, and it unfolds in the panel ([DEC-UX-13](#dec-ux-13)). **The arrow** next to it only folds or unfolds it. Several can be unfolded at once.
5. Whether the panel is open or closed is **remembered per User** ([OP-UX-13](#op-ux-13)).

### What is in the panel

The panel is **the index of its section**: one panel, never a second one for what is inside an item ([DEC-UX-10](#dec-ux-10)). Screens: every screen of Collections; [resource-detail](screens/resource-detail.html) shows a Collection unfolded.

For Collections:

- **On top:** the title, a plus button for a new Collection, and search, with the filter of the panel next to it: Resources, Lenses or both, both by default. With one left out, a Collection without the other leaves the panel; with only Lenses, each Collection unfolds to them ([DEC-UX-151](#dec-ux-151), [DEC-UX-150](#dec-ux-150)).
- **Every Collection the User sees,** **All** by default, or **Yours**, the Collections of the User's Owner Units, chosen in the filter next to the search ([DEC-UX-87](#dec-ux-87), [DEC-UX-161](#dec-ux-161)). Each with its icon, name, number of Resources and an arrow that turns when it unfolds; a Collection of the User also says whether it is public or private. The Collection whose page is open has an outline.
- **An unfolded Collection** shows what it holds in its own card, as a **plain list**: no box or rounded corners per item, little space between them.
  - Each item: the icon of its kind, under the centre of the icon of the Collection; its name; a small maturity icon on the right, in grey.
  - The item you are on: its name in medium weight and the darkest colour. Opening a Resource unfolds its Collection.
  - A Resource that is not published: its name in a lighter grey, without an icon. Hover says "not published" ([DEC-UX-12](#dec-ux-12)).
  - The first eight items, then "Show all" with the number.
  - **Its folders first** ([DEC-UX-156](#dec-ux-156)): each with a folder icon, its name and how many it holds, folded, and an arrow that unfolds it to what it holds, indented under it with a thin line: its own folders first, then its Resources and Lenses. Then what sits at the top of the Collection. The folder whose page is open is in medium weight. The Owner Unit and the Data Team drag a Resource, a Lens or a folder onto another folder, or onto the Collection, to move it there ([DEC-UX-157](#dec-ux-157)).
  - **Then its Lenses,** right after the Resources, without a label of their own: the icon tells them apart. The Lenses of the Collection that the User sees, each with the lens icon, its name and its Level. Each group shows its first eight, then "Show all". Reports join later as a group of their own ([DEC-UX-65](#dec-ux-65), [FR-CO-36](../requirements/functional/03-collections.md#fr-co-36)).
- **For the Data Team,** on top: "Without a collection", a card with the Resources that wait for a Collection. It is no Collection, so it has no colour bar: it is in the urgent red instead, with a red tint, a red name and icon, and the number waiting in a red badge before its name, and under it "waiting · Data Admins only", naming the role that sees it ([DEC-UX-16](#dec-ux-16), [DEC-UX-335](#dec-ux-335)). It is folded until the Data Admin unfolds it; opening one of its Resources, or a search that finds one, unfolds it ([DEC-UX-133](#dec-ux-133)).
- **For the Data Team,** under it: **"To complete"**, a card with the Resources whose model type misses a field it asks for, or that have no model type yet ([FR-CO-45](../requirements/functional/03-collections.md#fr-co-45), [DEC-UX-265](#dec-ux-265)). It is in the warning colour, calmer than the red of "Without a collection": a to-do, not an emergency. The number in a badge before its name, "model types to complete · Data Admins only" under it ([DEC-UX-335](#dec-ux-335)), and per Resource under its name what it misses, such as "no model type" or "misses when it happened". Opening one goes to its page, where a warning says what is missing and "Complete it" reopens the steps from the model type on. The card is not shown when nothing is left to complete.
- **One line per name:** a name that is too long ends in an ellipsis and shows in full on hover. This holds for Collections and Resources alike.
- **Search** filters Collections, Resources and Lenses together. A Collection whose name matches stays as it was; a Collection with a matching Resource or Lens unfolds and shows only those.

## Start screen per section

Each main item opens on a **start screen**, not on an overview that repeats the panel. The panel is the index; the start screen shows what the list cannot show.

For Collections:

- **Intro** at the top, with a graphic element, "What are collections?", a short explanation, the button "New collection" and a link to more explanation.
  - It can be dismissed. That is saved **per User and per section** in the settings.
  - The intro has a **version number**: after a big change it can appear again.
  - Dismissed: a calm title bar with "Show intro" and the fixed button "New collection".
- **A search for a question,** "What do you want to know?", over Collections, Resources and Lenses, as a new query finds Lenses ([FR-CO-37](../requirements/functional/03-collections.md#fr-co-37), [FR-SR-48](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#fr-sr-48)); what it finds replaces the sections, Lenses, Resources and Collections, the shared words marked ([DEC-UX-127](#dec-ux-127)).
- **Yours:** the Collections of the User's Owner Units, then the Lenses of the User and their Owner Unit, with when they last ran them. A Lens that waits for the Data Team says so; only its author sees it.
- **Recently**, switchable between **Updated** and **Added**: what changed, by whom and when.
- **New lenses:** the Lenses made public lately, each as a tile with its name, the question it answers, its Level, and when it ran. Nothing popular for now ([DEC-UX-86](#dec-ux-86)). New means published in the last 30 days in a public Collection, at most six, without those of the User's own Owner Units ([DEC-UX-126](#dec-ux-126)).

*Lenses had a start screen of their own; it is withdrawn on 2026-10-26, as Lenses have no section any more. Its search, "Yours" and Discover moved to the start screen of Collections ([DEC-UX-66](#dec-ux-66)).*

For Learning ([learning-start](screens/learning-start.html), [DEC-UX-38](#dec-ux-38)): an intro, "What is the learning centre?", with "Start with the compass"; a search; "Continue" with the card the User was reading; "Suggested for you" ([DEC-LR-18](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#dec-lr-18)); the path from easy to advanced; and "New and changed". See [Learning](#learning).

## Grid

- The grid is **always 100% wide**.
- Tiles have a **minimum width**; they stretch until there is room for an extra column. That way there is never an empty strip on the right.
- The minimum width depends on the content and is chosen per screen.

## Tabs

- Real tabs: **rounded corners at the top, concave corners at the bottom** that flow into the content surface, and a **fine line** under the row of tabs.
- The content surface under the tabs is a reading surface. Tiles in it get a light warm-grey tint instead of a border.

## Maturity

- The **maturity icon**: six bars, filled up to the current Level (0 Beta, 1 Available, 2 Described, 3 Confirmed, 4 Explored, 5 Monitored).
- When the Level has **dropped**, the previous Level shows as an outlined bar in the signal colour.
- The icon is the fixed symbol for maturity, wherever a Resource appears. On hover it shows the name of the Level.
- In the panel it is smaller and grey, so the names lead; on the page of a Resource it is full size and dark.

## Detail page of a Resource

Screens: [resource-overview](screens/resource-overview.html), [resource-detail](screens/resource-detail.html), [resource-edit](screens/resource-edit.html), [resource-columns-edit](screens/resource-columns-edit.html), [resource-unassigned](screens/resource-unassigned.html), [resource-publish](screens/resource-publish.html) and [resource-deprecate](screens/resource-deprecate.html).

- **Header:** the name, the Key with a copy button, the button "Query", which starts a new query on the Resource in [Query](#query) ([DEC-UX-21](#dec-ux-21)), "Messages" with their number, which opens the Messages on the Resource in a slide-in ([DEC-UX-29](#dec-ux-29)), and the button "Ask a question", which opens the same slide-in with Question chosen ([DEC-UX-08](#dec-ux-08)). For its Owner, while it is not published, "Publish" takes the place of "Ask a question" ([DEC-UX-31](#dec-ux-31)). No icon: a Resource has none of its own.
- **Status row:** maturity, Publication, Owner, open Signals. No freshness ([DEC-UX-07](#dec-ux-07)).
- **Warning on a change** since the last Confirmation ([DEC-MR-08](../requirements/functional/06-maturity-and-release.md#dec-mr-08), [DEC-UX-64](#dec-ux-64)): what changed and when, in the signal colour, with the Level that stays. For the Owner and the Data Team, "Mark as seen" takes it away; the change stays in the History. There is no Reconfirm.
- **Warning on a field of its model type that is gone** ([FR-RC-29](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#fr-rc-29), [DEC-UX-264](#dec-ux-264)): for everyone, in the signal colour, as the warning on a change: which role lost its field, the field and when it went, such as "`week_start`, its period, was removed in dbt yesterday", and that the Level stays and the Data Team has been told. The Data Team has "Choose another field", which opens Edit relations at the step of the fields. It goes once a field holds the role again; there is nothing to mark seen.
- **Warning that its model type is incomplete** ([FR-CO-45](../requirements/functional/03-collections.md#fr-co-45), [DEC-UX-265](#dec-ux-265)): for the Data Team only, in the warning colour: what is missing, such as "no model type yet" or "its model type misses when it happened", that its EDA cannot start until it is complete, and "Complete it", which reopens the steps from the model type on.
- **Tabs:** Overview, Columns, Lineage, History, Preview, Monitor ([DEC-UX-90](#dec-ux-90), [DEC-UX-205](#dec-ux-205)). No tab EDA, which is not in v1, and no tab Conversations: the Messages open from "Messages" in the header ([DEC-UX-67](#dec-ux-67)).
- **Lineage** ([resource-lineage](screens/resource-lineage.html)): walked one step at a time, never shown whole. This Resource stays in the middle, always. Its direct upstream models stand in a column on the left, its direct downstream models in a column on the right, each joined to it by a smooth curved line, without an arrowhead: what it is built on stands left, what is built on it right ([DEC-UX-215](#dec-ux-215)). A click on a model adds its own next models beside it, a column further out, and marks the model clicked; a click on another model, anywhere in the open flow, opens that branch and closes the one that was open, and a click on an open model closes it. No titles above the columns; the columns align at the top. Every card has the same height, with room for a name on two lines; a longer name ends in an ellipsis and shows in full on hover. It is drawn on the canvas of Svelte Flow, with the same curves: the canvas pans when dragged and zooms with its buttons at the bottom left, a pinch, or Cmd or Ctrl and scrolling; a zoom or a pan stays until the next step. The canvas takes all the room left in the frame below the tabs, across its whole width, so that the walk opens on the whole screen without scrolling ([DEC-UX-217](#dec-ux-217)). Each step brings what was walked into view, shrunk to half at most; when even that is too wide, the Resource stays in view with the columns nearest it, on the side that was walked ([DEC-UX-152](#dec-ux-152)). A model that is a Resource shows its name and Level; "Back to its direct models" returns ([FR-SR-54](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#fr-sr-54), [DEC-UX-100](#dec-ux-100)).
- **History** ([resource-history](screens/resource-history.html)): every query that used the Resource, one row per query, a Lens, a query or its previews, with who made it, "Claude" for one made through Claude ([DEC-CL-15](../requirements/functional/17-claude.md#dec-cl-15)), how many runs, its last run and what that read; a row opens it ([FR-SR-44](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#fr-sr-44)). Only its Owner Unit and the Data Team see the tab ([DEC-SR-41](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#dec-sr-41)).
- **Overview** ([resource-overview](screens/resource-overview.html)), in parts, each a page of its own, with a navigation on the left under the tabs ([DEC-UX-168](#dec-ux-168)). It opens on the Summary; then come Description, Known Behaviour, Known Gaps, Querying, Entities, Maturity and Changes, and, apart at the end, for the Owner Unit and the Data Team, the Danger zone ([DEC-UX-176](#dec-ux-176)). The Messages are no part of their own: "Messages" in the header opens them. A part can show a count, such as its Known Gaps, or a dot in the signal colour when something in it calls for a look, such as a change since the last Confirmation. *resource-detail and resource-edit still show the Overview in tiles; resource-overview takes their place for the Overview.*
  - **Summary:** everything Data Hub knows about the Resource on one page, in the order to read it, each part numbered, with its weight and a way to the part itself ([DEC-UX-169](#dec-ux-169), [FR-RC-19](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#fr-rc-19)). The weights: Read first, Before you query, When interpreting results and Context, each with a label of its own, explained above the parts. The order and the weights are the same for every Resource ([DEC-RC-17](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#dec-rc-17)). A deprecation comes first, in the signal colour; a part with nothing in it is left out. Reliable since stands in the part of the Known Gaps, its first line, with its Date Field and why not earlier ([DEC-UX-296](#dec-ux-296)). Claude reads the same Summary ([FR-CL-25](../requirements/functional/17-claude.md#fr-cl-25)). From the Messages on the Resource it takes the Q&A of the Owner and the Data Team, every thread its Owner Unit or the Data Team started or answered in, which stays for good, with the open Signals; and what colleagues wrote that they have not touched yet, as Context ([FR-RC-22](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#fr-rc-22), [DEC-RC-18](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#dec-rc-18), [DEC-UX-171](#dec-ux-171)). The role of the Owner and the Data Team shows next to their names; a way to them opens the Messages.
  - **Description:** the Business Description, written in Markdown and shown rendered, with the description from dbt under it, and the model type and Grain from the Data Team ([FR-RC-20](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#fr-rc-20)). Without a Business Description, the description from dbt stands alone, and the Owner and the Data Team see "Add a business description". Under them, **what one row is** ([FR-RC-28](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#fr-rc-28), [DEC-UX-263](#dec-ux-263)): the model type with its definition in one line and its Learning Card marker; the Grain with its fields and its sentence; then each field its model type asks for, by its role, such as "Happened at `eventDate`" for an `event`, or "Grouped by `lead_source`", "Period `week_start`" and "Counts and sums: `lead_count` adds up" for an `aggregate`. A role whose field is gone shows so, in the signal colour. All of it marked "From the Data Team". For a Data Admin, "Change model type" in its head opens the sheet of adding a Resource from the model type on, without its first step; the step of the model type then says the model type it has now, and what changing drops: the fields of the old model type, and that its EDA is revalidated ([DEC-RC-22](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#dec-rc-22)). Next to the description from dbt, "Seems wrong?" tells the Data Team it seems wrong ([DEC-UX-338](#dec-ux-338)). What one row is has, for a Data Admin, the button for the Grain next to "Change model type" ([DEC-UX-337](#dec-ux-337)).
  - **Known Behaviour:** intended use and pitfalls, written in Markdown and shown rendered, as the Business Description ([DEC-RC-19](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#dec-rc-19)). Since when the data is reliable moved to Known Gaps ([DEC-UX-296](#dec-ux-296)). Stability is not in v1 ([FR-RC-06](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#fr-rc-06)).
  - **Known Gaps:** a list, one gap per line, each with who added it and when ([FR-RC-21](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#fr-rc-21), [DEC-UX-170](#dec-ux-170)). The Owner and the Data Team add one in the line at the bottom, and change or remove one on its own line. Each gap keeps its why in plain text and may be narrowed down, with what applies, combined ([DEC-UX-177](#dec-ux-177), [FR-RC-24](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#fr-rc-24)): a period, from and to, a side left open, with no field of its own: which fields it concerns, Fields says ([DEC-UX-178](#dec-ux-178)); fields, empty or unreliable; and rows, a field with one of some values, typed, or empty. Each shows as a chip under its text: "Period until 28 Feb 2025", "Fields visitorId", "Rows lead_source is partner". Adding opens a form: the text first, then Period, Fields and Rows to add, each a block of its own with its ×. A gap without one stays free text. The Summary writes each scope on a line under its gap. A period is **one day or a period** ([DEC-RC-30](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#dec-rc-30), [OP-UX-110](#op-ux-110)): two choices on top of its block, its dates right behind the choice: One day with one date, A period with From until, a side left empty for from the start or until now. Under them **On the Date Field**, optional ([DEC-RC-31](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#dec-rc-31)): "The Date Field of the Resource · `week_start`" first, which names none and follows the Date Field, then its other date fields. A gap that names one says so on its chip: "Period on 21 Oct 2026 │ on `loaded_at`".
  - **Reliable since, on top of Known Gaps** ([DEC-UX-296](#dec-ux-296), [FR-RC-31](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#fr-rc-31)): a tile of its own above the gaps, its title with the ⓘ of its definition and Learning Card K5.17, as every term of the dictionary ([DEC-UX-279](#dec-ux-279)). It shows the day, large, or "From its first row" with the day of that row; then "on `week_start`, its period": the Date Field and what it is for its table type ([FR-RC-32](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#fr-rc-32)); "Why not earlier" with its text; who set it and when. Under it the **time of its data as a line**: from its first row to today, the time before Reliable since shaded with fine stripes and its day marked, with a key for the shading: "Before Reliable since: rows that are there, but not to be read as reliable". The line is about the reference date alone: it draws no Known Gaps. **Not set** is empty, never one of the two answers: "Not set yet", required to publish, and the question "From which day can its data be relied on? Choose one." with From its first row and From a later day, neither chosen; Save stays off, "Nothing is chosen yet", until one is, and the choice opens the rest of the form. **Change** opens the form in place: From its first row, or From a later day, with the day, the Date Field, proposed from its table type, and Why not earlier, required. Another field than its table type gives shows a warning under it, in the signal colour, with what it changes ([OP-RC-30](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#op-rc-30)). The gaps follow under "The gaps, one per line".
  - **Querying:** the partition column and its unit, the number of partitions, the cluster columns in their order, the size, and a sentence on how to query cheaply, from dbt and BigQuery, read-only; the Key Fields; and "Query". A Resource has no Parameters of its own ([DEC-RC-34](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#dec-rc-34)).
  - **Entities:** each Entity with its identifier, the identity written in full, such as `user.userId`, and the Resources it joins to, also as a picture, whose labels name the Entity alone ([DEC-UX-216](#dec-ux-216)). An Entity without an identifier at this Grain is shown, but joins nothing. A Data Admin has the button for the Grain and the mappings in its head ([DEC-UX-337](#dec-ux-337)).
  - **Maturity:** the ladder, with the previous Level after a drop, and beside it every Level from 1 · Available to 5 · Monitored, explained ([DEC-UX-175](#dec-ux-175), [FR-MR-16](../requirements/functional/06-maturity-and-release.md#fr-mr-16)): a Level passed says how, with who and when: registered through which exposure, described in dbt, published and confirmed by whom and when, and what changed since; the Level it is at stands out; a Level still to reach says what it takes and who does it, such as its Owner publishing it, with the way to do so. 0 · Beta is named under them, for concepts. The Publication has no tile of its own; the monitoring is Level 5.
  - **Changes:** the recent history with Log IDs; the queries on it stay under History.
  - **Danger zone:** what changes how everyone may rely on the Resource, each explained before its form: what it does, when to do it, and that it goes into the history ([DEC-UX-176](#dec-ux-176)). Deprecate is there: why, required, and what to use instead, optional; once deprecated, who did it, when and why, and "Take it away" ([FR-SR-50](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#fr-sr-50)). Only a published Resource can be deprecated; before that, the part says so. "Deprecate" is no longer in the header; the line of a deprecation under it leads its Owner Unit and the Data Team to the Danger zone. **Remove from the Collection** is there too, for the Data Team ([DEC-UX-212](#dec-ux-212)), and for its Owner Unit while it is not published ([DEC-UX-214](#dec-ux-214)): what it does first, then one button; published, the Owner Unit reads instead that only the Data Team takes it out now. It takes the Resource out of its Collection and its folder, back to Without a Collection, where only the Data Team sees it; its descriptions and history stay with it, and a withdrawn one then shows in no list. The Danger zone shows for the Data Team whenever the Resource is in a Collection.
- **Columns:** each column with its type, its Business Description and its description from dbt side by side, its Entity with its identifier, such as `user.userId` ([DEC-UX-216](#dec-ux-216)), and what it serves as: a Key Field, marked with a key, first ([DEC-UX-266](#dec-ux-266)), the Partition, a Cluster column with its place, a Parameter. A column without a Business Description shows the one from dbt alone. A column that a Known Gap names under Fields carries "Known Gap", with the gap on hover and a way to Known Gaps ([FR-RC-26](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#fr-rc-26)). "Edit" above the table, for the Owner and the Data Team, opens the Business Descriptions of the fields. A Data Admin has "Edit relations" there, which opens step 2 of adding Resources for this one Resource ([DEC-UX-107](#dec-ux-107)).

### Editing a Resource

- The Owner and the Data Team edit what Data Hub keeps about a Resource **in place,** on the page of its part of the Overview: the Business Description on Description; intended use, pitfalls and since when the data is reliable on Known Behaviour; the Known Gaps one by one on their own page; the Key Fields on Querying ([DEC-UX-09](#dec-ux-09), [DEC-UX-168](#dec-ux-168)).
- Each page they can edit has its own "Edit". Editing turns that page into fields, with a bar on top that says every saved change goes into the history, and "Cancel" and "Save".
- What comes from dbt (the description, the columns) and what the Data Team gives in Data Hub (the model type and Grain, the Entities and relationships, [DEC-RC-09](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#dec-rc-09)) is shown next to the fields, marked as such, and not edited here.
- **The Business Description** is written next to the description from dbt, never over it: the Markdown editor of Data Hub on the left, as for the intro of a Collection ([DEC-UX-158](#dec-ux-158)), the description from dbt on the right, read-only ([DEC-UX-61](#dec-ux-61)). Left empty, the description from dbt stands alone. Every other text about a Resource and its fields is plain text ([DEC-RC-16](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#dec-rc-16)). One line above the editor says what each is for: dbt says how the data is made; the Business Description says what it represents and what it is used for ([DEC-UX-275](#dec-ux-275)).
- **The fields** are edited on the Columns tab, the same way: "Edit" turns the table into a list, one row per field with its name and type, its Business Description as a field, and its description from dbt. A description from dbt that changed in the last 30 days says so, with its date and the text before, so the Owner sees what moved under their own; a filter shows only those fields. One quiet line above the fields says what each description is for ([DEC-UX-275](#dec-ux-275)). Screen: [resource-columns-edit](screens/resource-columns-edit.html).

### Messages on a Resource

- **No tab of their own** ([DEC-UX-67](#dec-ux-67)): the Messages on a Resource open in the slide-in from "Messages" in its header, as on every object ([DEC-UX-29](#dec-ux-29)); "Ask a question" opens it with Question chosen. See [Messages](#messages).
- On a Signal, the Owner and the Data Team can make it an Anomaly. Not in version 1: the prototypes show the link, the build leaves it out ([FR-SR-30](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#fr-sr-30)).
- Every Message knows its Subject, the object it is about: a Resource here, a Lens or a query elsewhere, the same way ([DEC-CV-01](../requirements/functional/14-conversations.md#dec-cv-01), [DEC-CV-03](../requirements/functional/14-conversations.md#dec-cv-03)). Outside the page of its object, in the Message Centre or a notification, a Message shows that object with its icon and name and links to it, opening at the Message ([FR-CV-02](../requirements/functional/14-conversations.md#fr-cv-02)).

### Preview

Screen: [resource-preview](screens/resource-preview.html). Designed on 2026-10-23, to review.

- **The first rows** of the Resource, newest first, in the grid of Query ([DEC-UX-98](#dec-ux-98)): the row number, each column with its type, the values as they are, `null` shown as such (empty is not zero).
- **Its choices** choose which rows, above the table under "Filter", such as the weeks and the lead source: its window, its first cluster column and its Key Fields of text or yes/no, the last marked "Key Field" ([DEC-RC-34](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#dec-rc-34)). Nothing to type: SQL is for [Query](#query). The partition column is always one of them, marked "Partition", and so is the first cluster column, marked "Cluster": a preview reads only what it shows. In BigQuery a `LIMIT` alone lowers nothing that is read. The partition column offers its newest 1, 4 or 12 partitions that hold rows ([DEC-SR-39](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#dec-sr-39)); the other choices list at most 100 values each ([DEC-SR-37](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#dec-sr-37)).
- **The Pre-flight** sits next to the choices: what the preview scans, and how many partitions it reads ([FR-SR-36](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#fr-sr-36), [FR-SR-40](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#fr-sr-40)). Above the approval threshold it asks first ([FR-SR-37](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#fr-sr-37)).
- **Under the table:** the rows 100 at a time, with how many of how many and a way to the next and previous page; a page is read from the same run, never a new query ([FR-SR-51](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#fr-sr-51)). Then what was scanned, and the **Query ID** with a copy button and a link to the Query Log ([FR-LN-06](../requirements/functional/07-log-and-notifications.md#fr-ln-06)).
- **A way further,** as a tile under the table: the own question goes to Query, with "Query this resource", or, in version 2, to Claude ([FR-SR-26](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#fr-sr-26), [FR-CH-03](../requirements/functional/13-channels.md#fr-ch-03)), with a prompt to copy. No tile for an export and no download button ([DEC-UX-120](#dec-ux-120)).

### Queries

Screen: [resource-history](screens/resource-history.html). Designed on 2026-10-23, to review ([DEC-UX-19](#dec-ux-19)). *Note of 2026-10-28: this is the tab History, one row per query or Lens, without a Query ID; the screen leads where the lines below differ ([DEC-UX-90](#dec-ux-90), [OP-SR-18](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#op-sr-18)).*

- Every query that ran on the Resource, newest first: when, who, through what (Query, a preview, Claude, a Lens), what it was, what it read, and its Query ID. A blocked query shows its estimate.
- **Only the Owner Unit of the Resource and the Data Team** see the list, and they see the SQL of its queries ([DEC-SR-41](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#dec-sr-41)); for everyone else the tab is not there.
- **On top,** a few numbers: queries in the last 30 days, what they read, how many colleagues, how many filter on the partition column. And the button "Query this resource", which opens [Query](#query) with it.

### A Resource without a Collection

- Only the Data Team sees it. In the panel of Collections, the Data Team has "Without a collection" on top, folded, and unfolded like a Collection when this Resource is open ([DEC-UX-133](#dec-ux-133)).
- Its page says that it is in no Collection and that only the Data Team sees it, where it came from, and has the button "Add to a collection" for the Data Team. It opens the sheet that adds Resources, with as first step the choice of the Collection and this Resource ticked; next its Grain and relations, as from the page of a Collection ([DEC-UX-131](#dec-ux-131)). Screen: [resource-unassigned](screens/resource-unassigned.html), `?addto=open`.
- Its Overview shows what exists already: the description from dbt and the maturity. The rest follows once it has an Owner.

### Publishing and confirming

Screen: [resource-publish](screens/resource-publish.html). Designed on 2026-10-24, to review ([DEC-UX-31](#dec-ux-31)). *The sheet to confirm again after a drop is withdrawn on 2026-10-26: a change to the schema no longer lowers the Level ([DEC-UX-64](#dec-ux-64)).*

- **Not published yet:** the status row says so, and a notice says who sees it now, its Owner Unit and the Data Team, and who will once it is published. In the panel it is grey, as every Resource that is not published. For its Owner, "Publish" is the main button of the header.
- **"Publish"** opens a sheet in three parts ([FR-SR-49](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#fr-sr-49)):
  - **Who will see it:** everyone who sees its Collection, the whole company when the Collection is public ([FR-SR-31](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#fr-sr-31)).
  - **What Data Hub checked:** that the User may publish it, and that it is Described. When dbt does not describe the table, or none of its fields ([DEC-MR-12](../requirements/functional/06-maturity-and-release.md#dec-mr-12)), the sheet names what is missing, says that the Data Team describes them in dbt and that the Resource stays at 1 · Available until then, and offers to ask the Data Team. "Publish and confirm" stays off ([DEC-MR-10](../requirements/functional/06-maturity-and-release.md#dec-mr-10)). Then the one list of what makes it ready to publish, the same in Claude and in the Owner Journey ([DEC-MR-15](../requirements/functional/06-maturity-and-release.md#dec-mr-15), [DEC-MR-16](../requirements/functional/06-maturity-and-release.md#dec-mr-16)): the steps required to publish, all done in one line, or each open one with what it misses and a link to its part of the Overview; then what is advised and what is needed later for the EDA, which hold nothing up. While a required step is open, "Publish and confirm" stays off, and the sheet says that Claude takes the Owner through the steps, one at a time ([DEC-CL-17](../requirements/functional/17-claude.md#dec-cl-17)). *Note of 2026-11-20: while a step required to publish is open, "Publish" no longer opens the sheet: it warns in a pop-out and offers to guide the Owner through the steps in the [Flow Box](#flow-box) ([DEC-MR-20](../requirements/functional/06-maturity-and-release.md#dec-mr-20)). The sheet opens once every step is done, so its list holds no open step.*
  - **What you confirm,** as publishing is the first confirmation: what the Resource is and what it stands for: the whole card, read back as a colleague will see it: its Business Description and its description from dbt, its intended use, pitfalls, Reliable since with its Date Field and why not earlier, on a line of its own, its Known Gaps, its Key Fields, and its fields. The declaration accepts every text on it, also what was written before ([DEC-MR-17](../requirements/functional/06-maturity-and-release.md#dec-mr-17)). No figures and no question about its data: a confirmation makes no claim about the data inside it, which the EDA explores ([DEC-MR-07](../requirements/functional/06-maturity-and-release.md#dec-mr-07)).
- **Nothing to add** ([DEC-MR-16](../requirements/functional/06-maturity-and-release.md#dec-mr-16)): where none is an answer, the pitfalls, the empty part says it is required to publish and offers "Nothing to add" to who may describe the Resource; once pressed, it reads "None, according to" whom and when, until someone writes one. On the Maturity of a Resource not published yet, 3 · Confirmed lists the steps required to publish, each done or open with a link to its part; Reliable since is a line of its own, a done one with its value, such as "1 Jan 2025, on `week_start`" ([DEC-MR-18](../requirements/functional/06-maturity-and-release.md#dec-mr-18)). The Known Gaps are advised, not required: their part has no "Nothing to add", and without a gap it says none are known ([DEC-UX-333](#dec-ux-333), [DEC-MR-22](../requirements/functional/06-maturity-and-release.md#dec-mr-22)).
- **The declaration and its button are one** ([DEC-UX-63](#dec-ux-63)): at the foot of the sheet, the checkbox with the declaration and the button stand together in one bar, the button at its right end. Until the box is ticked, the button is off; ticking it switches the button on and marks the bar. Cancel stands apart, outside the bar.
- At the bottom, both say that Data Hub keeps the confirmation with the fingerprint of that moment, and that it does not expire.

## Page of a Collection

Screens: [collection-overview](screens/collection-overview.html), [collection-edit](screens/collection-edit.html), [collection-add-resources](screens/collection-add-resources.html), [collection-add-type](screens/collection-add-type.html), [collection-add-grain](screens/collection-add-grain.html) and [collection-add-relations](screens/collection-add-relations.html). Designed and approved on 2026-10-23, the relations designed on 2026-10-25 ([DEC-UX-58](#dec-ux-58)); the switch at the bottom right of the overview, for the prototype only, shows it as a Data Admin, the Owner or a colleague. Made simpler on 2026-11-20, at the request of Max: one table, one New, editing from the title, the history in the quick window ([DEC-UX-309](#dec-ux-309) to [DEC-UX-312](#dec-ux-312)), its open points [OP-UX-115](#op-ux-115) to [OP-UX-118](#op-ux-118) decided the same day, with the page of a folder laid out the same way ([DEC-UX-313](#dec-ux-313) to [DEC-UX-315](#dec-ux-315)).

- **Opens** when a User clicks a Collection in the panel ([DEC-UX-13](#dec-ux-13)). The page holds what the panel cannot: what the Collection is, what needs attention, and the lists in full ([DEC-UX-14](#dec-ux-14)).
- **Head:** the icon, the name and the description of the Collection; a row with the Owner Unit and private or public ([DEC-UX-145](#dec-ux-145)). For the Owner Unit and the Data Team, a click on the name or the description edits both in place, as on a query ([DEC-UX-162](#dec-ux-162)), with "Edit more" beside Save and Cancel for every other field; there is no Edit button ([DEC-UX-309](#dec-ux-309)).
- **Intro:** under the head, "About this Collection": a text its Owner Unit writes in Markdown, with headings, bold, lists and links, shown as written; it is changed with Edit more, or from the quick window, with no link of its own on the page ([OP-UX-116](#op-ux-116)). Without an intro, nothing shows ([DEC-UX-158](#dec-ux-158)).
- **Only what sits at its top** ([DEC-UX-156](#dec-ux-156)), in **one table** without a heading and without a line of explanation under it: its folders first, then its Resources and Lenses in no folder together, A to Z, each with the icon of its kind ([DEC-UX-311](#dec-ux-311)). Its columns are Name, Level, Status and Last change: a folder says what it holds under Status, a Resource whether it is published, a Lens what it is built on ([OP-UX-115](#op-ux-115)). A Resource that is not published is in a lighter grey; the Data Admins have the button to remove a Resource at the end of its row, and its Owner Unit too while it is not published ([DEC-UX-212](#dec-ux-212), [DEC-UX-214](#dec-ux-214)). A whole row opens what it is with a click and, for the Owner Unit and the Data Team, moves with a drag onto a folder, in the table, in the panel or in the path; once dropped, a line on screen says where it went ([DEC-UX-157](#dec-ux-157)).
- **The page of a folder** ([collection-folder](screens/collection-folder.html)) is laid out as the page of its Collection ([DEC-UX-315](#dec-ux-315), [OP-UX-118](#op-ux-118)): the path above it ("Collections / Credit leads / Leads in"); its name and description, which the Owner Unit and the Data Team change with a click on them ([DEC-UX-162](#dec-ux-162)), seeing "Add a description" where there is none; on the right of its head the same "New", with Folder and Lens, and "Delete"; and what it holds in **the same table**, with the same columns, the same order and the same buttons at the end of a row, without a heading and without a line of explanation. Delete stays on: on a folder that holds something it says so and deletes nothing, as only an empty folder goes.
- **Actions, on the right of the head** ([DEC-UX-310](#dec-ux-310)): "Messages" with how many are open, as before, and one **"New"** with a menu, per role: Folder, a new row on top of the table with its name in focus, and Lens, which starts with a query, for the Owner Unit and the Data Team ([DEC-CO-15](../requirements/functional/03-collections.md#dec-co-15), [DEC-CO-18](../requirements/functional/03-collections.md#dec-co-18)); Resources, for the Data Admins, which opens the sheet that adds them. A colleague without either sees no New. A Message is written under Messages, not with New ([DEC-UX-313](#dec-ux-313)). No "Edit all" and no "Add resources" in the head, and no actions next to headings.
- **Needs attention,** for the Owner Unit and the Data Team, only when there is something: a change since the last Confirmation not marked seen yet, a Level that dropped, open Signals, Resources not published yet, each of these on a line of its own ([DEC-UX-314](#dec-ux-314)). Each line links to where it is solved.
- **The history** of the Collection is no part of the page: "Show the history" in the quick window opens it in a sheet, the newest first ([DEC-UX-312](#dec-ux-312), [OP-UX-117](#op-ux-117)). A colleague does not see the entries about Resources that are not published.
- **The quick window** holds every action of the page: change the name and description, Edit more, Edit the intro, New folder, Add Resources, New Lens and Show the history, each for who may ([DEC-UX-240](#dec-ux-240)).
- **Editing** happens in place. A click on the name or the description turns both into fields, with Save and Cancel, as on a query, and "Edit more", which turns the whole head into fields with the edit bar on top ([DEC-UX-162](#dec-ux-162), [DEC-UX-309](#dec-ux-309)). Name, description, Owner Unit (only groups of whoever edits; the Data Team chooses from every group of the domain, [FR-CO-35](../requirements/functional/03-collections.md#fr-co-35)), who sees it (private or public, with what that means) and icon; a Collection has no colour ([DEC-UX-105](#dec-ux-105)). Dissolving is at the bottom, and only possible for an empty Collection.
- **Adding a Resource** opens a **sheet** from the right over the page, one Resource at a time ([DEC-UX-132](#dec-ux-132)), in four steps, named on top with an arrow between them ([DEC-UX-58](#dec-ux-58), [DEC-UX-262](#dec-ux-262)):
  1. **Choose a Resource:** the Resources without a Collection, searchable, each with its description from dbt, how it came in, and its Level, to choose one with a radio button. A Resource is in one Collection at most, so only those without a Collection appear. The page stays visible behind it. At the foot, the name of the Resource chosen, and "Next: model type" leads on.
  2. **Model type** ([FR-CO-44](../requirements/functional/03-collections.md#fr-co-44)): what one row of the Resource is, chosen explicitly, as the rest of the Resource is read through it. One line on top says why: the model type decides what may be added up, which fields the next step asks for, and which EDA-Probes its EDA runs. Then a card per model type, `event`, `state`, `fact`, `dimension`, `aggregate` and `other` ([DEC-RC-21](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#dec-rc-21)), chosen with a radio button, two side by side. Each card holds its name in monospace with its marker for the Learning Card on the model types ([FR-LR-39](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#fr-lr-39)); "One row is", its definition; "Why it exists"; "Counting", what may be added up; "Asks for", the fields of the next step; and an example at Immoscoop. Where the Domain Model says the model type ([FR-AR-20](../requirements/functional/01-artifact-registry.md#fr-ar-20)), that card says "Suggested" and where it came from, and is chosen to start with; the Data Admin still confirms it. Data Hub suggests none from the strategy or the names of a Colleciónes model, which change too often ([DEC-RC-25](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#dec-rc-25)); without a suggestion, no card is chosen to start with. `other` says what it gives up: no fields of its own, only the EDA-Probes for every Resource, and no guidance on counting; its sentence is "One row per" of the next step. At the foot "Back", the model type chosen with what it asks for, and "Next: Grain and storage".
  3. **Grain and storage** ([FR-CO-33](../requirements/functional/03-collections.md#fr-co-33), [FR-CO-34](../requirements/functional/03-collections.md#fr-co-34)): what one row is and how the Resource is stored, on a step of their own, named after the Resource, with its name and Level, the model it is built on, and its model type as a chip with "Change". On a quiet surface, what one row is: "Grain" with its fields as chips and "Column" to add one, and "One row per" with the sentence; Data Hub does not check the Grain against the data ([DEC-RC-15](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#dec-rc-15)). Below a thin line on the same surface, how it is stored: "Partitioned by" with the column and its unit, "Clustered by" with the columns as chips in their order, and "Partition filter: Required in every query" as a check box; a filter BigQuery requires is checked and locked ("Required by BigQuery"). Each says where it came from ("From dbt", "From the Domain Model", "From a dbt test", "Written here"). At the foot "Back", what one row is, and "Next: fields and relations".
  4. **Fields and relations** ([FR-CO-33](../requirements/functional/03-collections.md#fr-co-33), [FR-CO-44](../requirements/functional/03-collections.md#fr-co-44)): the sheet widens, named after the Resource. One line on top says the relation types and identities come from the Domain Model, with its version and a link to its overview. Per Resource its name and Level, the model it is built on, and how many fields are mapped. Under it, first, **what its model type asks for**: the model type as a chip with "Change", back to step 2, and its definition; then a line per field it asks for, with the field chosen and where its suggestion came from, or "Not chosen" with a warning when it is required. An `event`: when it happened, a date or timestamp. A `state`: what it is about, the field that identifies the thing; and, optional, when it was created and when it was last updated, two dates or timestamps, and as of when, for a state that keeps its history. A `fact`: when it occurred, and one or more measures to add up. A `dimension`: what it describes, the field that identifies each thing. An `aggregate`: what it is grouped by, its period when it is grouped by time, and its counts and sums, every number field as "adds up" or "a unique count, never added up". `other`: one row is, the sentence of "One row per" from the step before. **The fields are chosen in the field table**, as part of the definition of each field: a column "For the event" (or the state, the fact…) right after the field, where each field gets what it is for its model type, offered only the roles its type fits, such as "When it happened" for a date or timestamp. A role that one field holds, such as when it happened, moves when it is chosen on another field. "Add Resource" stays greyed until every required field is chosen, and says which one is missing. Then one line says where the mappings come from: the Domain Model for a Colleciónes model, or suggestions from the names of the fields. Then a table of every field: its name and type, a small "grain" mark on the fields of the Grain, its description; the mapping, chosen from "No mapping", `is`, the relation types grouped as "one per subject" and "many per subject", and "performed by" ([OP-RC-22](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#op-rc-22)); and, unless there is none, the identity as `entity.identity`, in monospace. Under it, quietly: where it came from ("From the domain model", "Suggested from its name", "Chosen here"); for a relation the subject it starts from, a choice when the Resource has more than one `is` field; and the cardinality in words with a badge ("1 row", "n rows", "N:1", "M:N"), never a choice ([DEC-RC-08](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#dec-rc-08)). An identity whose Entity is not in the Domain Model yet says so; under the table, the columns left out because they are a list, a struct or JSON, with why ([DEC-SR-33](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#dec-sr-33)). A field without a mapping shows only the choice, greyed. "Show" switches between all fields and only the mapped ones. At the foot "Back", what is about to happen, and "Add Resource".

## Query

Screen: [query](screens/query.html). Designed on 2026-10-23, to review. *Redesigned on 2026-10-27: the editor at the full width, folding its CTEs, the Parameters under it, and the Pre-flight with Run in a bar at its foot ([DEC-UX-70](#dec-ux-70), [DEC-UX-71](#dec-ux-71)); the result as a grid like that of BigQuery ([DEC-UX-72](#dec-ux-72)); a query bounded by its Resources, every run a variation in its history ([DEC-UX-73](#dec-ux-73), [DEC-UX-74](#dec-ux-74)).* *Projects designed on 2026-11-19, to review: [Projects](#projects); and adding a Resource changes the query you are in ([DEC-UX-287](#dec-ux-287)).*

- **A main section of its own,** next to Collections ([DEC-UX-17](#dec-ux-17)): a query can use every Resource the User may see, and later Lenses, not just one.
- **The panel** has "Refresh" next to "New query" in its head, which reads the queries again, such as those Claude made meanwhile ([DEC-UX-163](#dec-ux-163)). Above them, the Projects the User is in ([Projects](#projects), [DEC-UX-285](#dec-ux-285)). Under "History", it lists the queries that ran, not the Collections ([DEC-UX-20](#dec-ux-20)): **your own**, one item per query and not per run ([DEC-UX-74](#dec-ux-74)), grouped by when they last ran and searchable: per day in the current week, per week back to the first day of last month, then per month, each period with how many queries it holds. Every period starts folded, today too, and its queries are read only when it is opened ([DEC-UX-139](#dec-ux-139)). A search looks through all of them, and through the Projects, and opens the periods where something matches ([DEC-UX-139](#dec-ux-139), [DEC-UX-285](#dec-ux-285)). Previews, the queries of Lenses and hidden queries are left out, until the User ticks them in the filter next to the search, Previews, Lenses or Hidden, each with a line on what it is and how many there are ([DEC-UX-137](#dec-ux-137)). The filter is a button with the number of kinds it adds; it folds open a small form under the search, with "Reset" and the reminder that only their own panel changes. Folded, one line under the search says what it also shows, each with a cross that takes it away again ([DEC-UX-150](#dec-ux-150)). For the Data Team the same form starts with whose queries it lists, Yours by default or All ([DEC-UX-161](#dec-ux-161)). A User hides a query of theirs with the eye that shows when the pointer is on it, or with "Hide this query" in the quick window, as its page has no button for it ([DEC-UX-246](#dec-ux-246)); it is hidden for them alone, and shows again the same way ([DEC-UX-138](#dec-ux-138)). **The Data Team** also has "All": everyone's queries, with who ran them and their SQL ([DEC-LN-01](../requirements/functional/07-log-and-notifications.md#dec-ln-01)). Nobody else sees the queries of others.
- **Each query** in the panel: the icon of what it ran on, a table for Resources and an aperture for a Lens ([DEC-UX-11](#dec-ux-11)); its name; then what it ran on (for a Lens just "Lens", as the name is that of the Lens; for Resources the first, with "+1" for more), how many runs it has and when it last ran; and a label for how it ran (a preview, Claude) or whether it was approved or blocked. A click opens it. The query that is open has an arrow that unfolds its last runs, folded until the User unfolds them ([DEC-UX-140](#dec-ux-140)); a click on one opens it in its history. The previews of a Resource are one item, and so is a query of Claude, each with its history; the runs of a Lens are in the history of the Lens.
- **A query is bounded by its Resources** ([DEC-UX-73](#dec-ux-73)): a query is its name, its question and its Resources, and it reads only those. Its Resources are chosen when it is made, in a new query or with "Query" on the page of a Resource, and changed in it: adding or taking out a Resource changes this query, and a new query is no longer the required route ([DEC-UX-287](#dec-ux-287)). Several queries may share the same Resources, each with its own question and its own history: "Leads per source name" and "Partner share per week" can both read Weekly lead volume and Lead sources.
- **A power user also reads tables outside Data Hub** ([DEC-UX-220](#dec-ux-220), [FR-SR-56](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#fr-sr-56)): whose Permission group has "Query tables outside Data Hub" may read, besides the Resources of the query, every table Data Hub knows that is no Resource: a model, seed, snapshot or source of a dbt project it follows through its Source Jobs. Such a table shows next to the Resources as a quiet chip with a dashed outline and only its table name, with no Level, Owner or Publication; a hover says its layer and that it is no Resource ([DEC-UX-243](#dec-ux-243)); the query runs, and the Pre-flight measures it as any other. A table that is a Resource is read as that Resource: the editor suggests the Resource, and one that is not of the query is added to it, under its own rules ([DEC-UX-287](#dec-ux-287)). A table Data Hub does not know stays an error. "New query" offers a power user "Or start from SQL, without Resources". A query that reads a table outside Data Hub cannot become a Lens: a line under its question says so, and "Make it a lens" is greyed ([DEC-UX-218](#dec-ux-218)).
- **Find a table** ([DEC-UX-224](#dec-ux-224), [FR-SR-58](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#fr-sr-58)): next to "Add a resource", for a power user only, a quiet button that opens a sheet over the query. On top a search over the tables Data Hub knows from the dbt projects it follows ([OP-UX-81](#op-ux-81)), by their name and the words of their description, with chips for their layer, All, Sources, Staging, Intermediate and Marts, and a choice of project when Data Hub follows more than one. Each table is a row: its name, its layer, its project and dataset, and its description from dbt on one line; a table that is a Resource says so, with its Collection and Level. A click unfolds a row to its columns, each with its type and description from dbt, partition and cluster columns first and marked. A table that is no Resource is ticked, one or more at once; "Add 2 tables" closes the sheet and adds **a CTE for each, at the start of the SQL** of this query ([DEC-UX-225](#dec-ux-225)), and each shows next to the Resources as "not a Resource" ([DEC-UX-220](#dec-ux-220)). A Resource cannot be ticked here: it has "Add it to this query", which opens the sheet that adds a Resource, with it ticked, under its own rules ([DEC-UX-287](#dec-ux-287)). Before anything is typed, the sheet lists the tables of the chosen layer by name. In the prototype "Find a table", seen as the Data Team, or `?find=open`.
- **A new query** ([query-new](screens/query-new.html)) is a screen in two halves ([DEC-UX-22](#dec-ux-22)):
  - **Left, your query:** its name, required and suggested from its Resources and the date ([DEC-UX-21](#dec-ux-21)); "What do you want to know?", the question of the query in a sentence; and its Resources, each with its Collection, its partition column and its Level, to remove or add.
  - **Right, what is already made:** the Lenses that use these Resources, only those whose Resources the User may all use ([DEC-CO-16](../requirements/functional/03-collections.md#dec-co-16)). First those on **exactly** these Resources, then those on **these and more**, where the other Resources are shown as dashed chips. Within each group, the Lenses that share most words with the question come first ([DEC-UX-23](#dec-ux-23)); the first is marked "Best match". Each with its name and a sentence on what it shows, the shared words marked in both; its Resources; its tags, the shared ones marked, and how many words it shares; its type, Collection and maker, without how often it is used ([OP-SR-21](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#op-sr-21)); its Level; and "Use this lens" as the primary button. When no Lens uses these Resources, the half says so in one quiet line under its heading ([DEC-UX-114](#dec-ux-114)).
  - **At the bottom right,** "None of these fits?" with "Start your own" as a quiet button: reusing a Lens is the expected way, writing your own the alternative. Your own opens the editor with a template that filters on the partition column.
  - The new query is in the panel at once, as "not run yet".
  - **Its halves keep to the page** ([DEC-UX-336](#dec-ux-336)): a Resource whose name is long is cut with "…" inside its half and never pushes its row out of the card; the halves stack, the Lenses under the query, when the main area is narrow, whatever the window, as the panel and a Flow Box take room too.
- **Above the editor:** the name of the query, on one line, ending in "…" when it is longer, the whole name on a hover ([DEC-UX-251](#dec-ux-251)), and its question under it; a click on the name or the question edits it in place, with Save and Cancel, for who made the query ([DEC-UX-119](#dec-ux-119)). Where a query started from another is in its History, not under its question ([DEC-UX-250](#dec-ux-250)). On the right "Make it a lens", the primary button; "New query" is in the head of the panel and in the quick window, not here ([DEC-UX-252](#dec-ux-252)): a query that answers a question that comes back becomes a Lens ([DEC-UX-25](#dec-ux-25)).
- **On top of its frame,** on one line, "Reads only" and its Resources, each with its Collection and its Level; when they do not fit, they scroll sideways ([DEC-UX-249](#dec-ux-249)). A click on a Resource opens what a double-click on its name in the SQL opens: its Summary in a floating window over the query, with "Open the Resource" to go to its page; a click on a table outside Data Hub opens its window, as a double-click on it does ([DEC-UX-259](#dec-ux-259), [DEC-UX-221](#dec-ux-221)). After them, always in view, "Add a resource" as a small round + and, for a power user, "Find a table" as a small round magnifier, each with its name on a hover. "Add a resource", and the × on a Resource, change this query (below, [DEC-UX-287](#dec-ux-287)). How many Lenses already use them, as "2 Lenses" ("1 Lens", "0 Lenses"), with "Lenses on these Resources" on a hover, opens those Lenses in a sheet, as for a new query: reusing a Lens stays the first choice while writing ([DEC-UX-22](#dec-ux-22), [DEC-UX-260](#dec-ux-260)). While they are still looked up, it says "Lenses", greyed.
- **Two layouts,** chosen with two icons in the head of the page and kept per User: the query above its result, the default, or the query next to it, with its Parameters and its Pre-flight bar under it on the left and the result on the right ([DEC-UX-143](#dec-ux-143)). Either way the page fills the height of the screen: the query and the result each scroll on their own, and the divider between them is dragged, or moved with the arrow keys, to give one more room; where it stands is kept in the browser, per layout ([DEC-UX-144](#dec-ux-144)).
- **The row of the active Project** ([DEC-UX-316](#dec-ux-316), [DEC-UX-317](#dec-ux-317), [DEC-UX-319](#dec-ux-319)): there are no flagged queries; a query is kept on top by being in a Project ([Projects](#projects)). While a Project is active, the row on top of every screen of Query, above the breadcrumb, holds it: first **a tab for its details**, its icon and its name, without a key, then one tab per query, in the order of the Project, each with its name and, for the first ten, its number; one ⌘ before the numbers says which key goes with them ([OP-UX-84](#op-ux-84)). With no Project active, there is no row. The row holds more than ten: any further query has no key, and the tabs scroll sideways behind "All 14", which lists them all ([DEC-UX-283](#dec-ux-283), [OP-UX-96](#op-ux-96)). **⌘1 to ⌘9 and ⌘0** (Ctrl on Windows and Linux) open the first to the tenth query, from any screen of Query, also while typing in the SQL, by the key's place on the keyboard, so that a keyboard that needs Shift for its digits, such as AZERTY, works too ([OP-UX-86](#op-ux-86)). The open query, or the details, stands out. The quick window also goes to the next and the previous query of the row, round it ([DEC-UX-241](#dec-ux-241)). **A query of the row stays as the User left it** while they work in another: its SQL, its result and its tab, and a run still going, which finishes meanwhile ([OP-UX-85](#op-ux-85)). Its tab marks what happened there: a run going (a turning circle), a result not seen yet (a dot), a run that failed (a red dot), and Broken (the red mark of the panel). A hover shows its whole name and the × that takes it out of the Project ([DEC-UX-291](#dec-ux-291)); dragging a tab changes its place, and its number with it, for everyone in the Project ([OP-UX-95](#op-ux-95)). The row has no button for the quick window: ⌘⇧P and ⌘K open it ([DEC-UX-284](#dec-ux-284)).
- **The editor** writes BigQuery SQL, at the full width of the page, in a box of its own ([DEC-UX-70](#dec-ux-70)). It **formats** the SQL on request (Shift+Alt+F, or Format the SQL in the quick window; no button on the page, [DEC-UX-248](#dec-ux-248)), **runs** it with Cmd+Enter (Ctrl+Enter on Windows) as its button does ([DEC-UX-142](#dec-ux-142)), offers **templates** among its suggestions: "select … from … where true", which ends on `where true` for the conditions to follow ([DEC-UX-244](#dec-ux-244)), "where true and", "where false or", "with … as" and "final as … order by" ([DEC-UX-141](#dec-ux-141)), and on `count(*` the template `count(*) as …Count`, which writes `count(*) as rowCount` with `row` selected, for the User to name the count ([DEC-UX-235](#dec-ux-235)); **Enter** starts the new line at the indent of the line above, never more or less ([DEC-UX-234](#dec-ux-234)); takes all the height the query has left and is the only part of it that scrolls, with its Parameters to its right, or under it next to the result, folded at first ([DEC-UX-147](#dec-ux-147)), suggests after `from` and `join` the Resources of the query by their Display Name, written as their full name, and "New Resource…", which adds another Resource to this query ([DEC-UX-148](#dec-ux-148), [DEC-UX-287](#dec-ux-287)); after them every other Resource the User may use, marked "adds it", which, once picked, is written too and opens the sheet that adds it to this query ([DEC-UX-155](#dec-ux-155)), and **suggests** as you type: the columns of its Resources and the Parameters, the partition and cluster columns first and marked as such. Beside the list, the suggestion that is selected shows the type of its field and its descriptions, the Business Description next to the one from dbt, each with where it comes from ([OP-SR-20](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#op-sr-20), [DEC-UX-115](#dec-ux-115)). **A table that is not one of its Resources** is an error: it stands out in the SQL and next to the Resources, with "Add it to this query", and the query cannot run until it is ([DEC-UX-73](#dec-ux-73), [DEC-UX-287](#dec-ux-287)); a folded CTE with an error in it shows it on its "⋯ n lines". It **folds every CTE** to its first line, as a code editor does: an arrow in the gutter folds or unfolds one, "⋯ n lines" in its place unfolds it, and Fold all, in the quick window, folds them together ([DEC-UX-248](#dec-ux-248)); the line numbers keep their count.
- **The Parameters** are a second box under the editor, which folds open and shut: folded, a chip per Parameter with its value; open, a tile per Parameter with its kind, its value and the line of the SQL it is on ([DEC-UX-71](#dec-ux-71)). A Parameter has a name, a kind and a value:
  - **The kinds** form a standard list, each with its BigQuery type: Date (`DATE`), Period (two `DATE`s, `@name_from` and `@name_to`), Whole number (`INT64`), Decimal number (`NUMERIC`), Text (`STRING`), Choice (`STRING`, one value from a list), Several choices (`ARRAY<STRING>`) and Yes or no (`BOOL`). The list of a Choice is typed, one value a line; a list that comes from a column comes later ([DEC-UX-117](#dec-ux-117)).
  - **The value** is a value, such as `2026-10-25`, or an SQL function, such as `DATE_TRUNC(CURRENT_DATE(), YEAR)`, worked out at every run. The field suggests the common functions, and next to one of those says what it gives today, which Data Hub works out itself; next to any other function it says "worked out at every run". A function goes into the SQL of the run as it is written, so the Pre-flight checks it with the rest ([FR-LE-13](../requirements/functional/09-lenses.md#fr-le-13), [DEC-LE-14](../requirements/functional/09-lenses.md#dec-le-14), [DEC-UX-118](#dec-ux-118)).
  - **A Parameter is made or changed** in a floating window, one at a time, that moves by its head and folds to it, as a Message does ([DEC-UX-30](#dec-ux-30)): "Add parameter", or a click on a Parameter. Add or Save keeps the Parameter and closes the window; when it cannot be kept, the window stays open and says why ([DEC-UX-219](#dec-ux-219)).
  - **A Parameter that is not in the SQL** is grey: it is not sent with the query. **An `@name` in the SQL that is not a Parameter** stands out, in the SQL and under it, and the query cannot run, as BigQuery would refuse it; a click on it defines it, its kind suggested from the column it is compared with.
- **A double-click on a word of the SQL** selects it, as in any editor, and opens what it names ([DEC-UX-221](#dec-ux-221), [FR-SR-57](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#fr-sr-57)): on a Parameter, its floating window, as a click on its tile; on an `@name` that is no Parameter, the window that defines it; on the name of a Resource the User sees, its Summary in a floating window over the query, the same parts in the same order as on its page, each with its weight, with whether it is a Resource of this query and "Open the Resource" ([OP-UX-79](#op-ux-79)); on a field of its Resources, its type, its role as partition or cluster column, and its descriptions, the Business Description next to the one from dbt, each with where it comes from, in a pop-out at the word, as the suggestions show them ([DEC-UX-115](#dec-ux-115)), once for each Resource of the query that holds it, and "No Business Description yet" where the Owner wrote none. The pop-out goes with a click elsewhere, Escape or typing; the window stays until it is closed, and the query stays as it is. For a power user, the name of a table outside Data Hub opens what Data Hub knows of it in a floating window over the query, as the Summary of a Resource does, and a field of it its type and its description from dbt in the pop-out at the word, without "No Business Description yet", as such a table has no Owner ([DEC-UX-247](#dec-ux-247), [FR-SR-62](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#fr-sr-62)): under its name, "Not a Resource", its layer and its dbt project; its description from dbt; what BigQuery says of it at that moment, its kind (a table, a view, a materialized view or an external table, with how dbt makes it), how it is partitioned (the column, per what unit, and whether BigQuery asks a filter on it), its cluster columns, its size, rows and partitions, when it last changed and its full name; then its columns, partition and cluster columns first and marked, the fields of a STRUCT under it, each with its type and description, and a filter over them. "Copy the full name" stands at its foot; it has no page in Data Hub to open ([OP-UX-90](#op-ux-90)). A view says it stores nothing: a query on it reads the tables beneath it, as the Pre-flight measures. When BigQuery does not answer, the window shows what dbt knew at its last run and says so. Any other word is only selected. **A word a double-click explains has a dotted line under it**, softer than the word, and a hover says what a double-click opens; a wavy line stays the mark of an error ([DEC-UX-222](#dec-ux-222)). In the prototype `?dbl=param`, `resource` and `field`, and for a table outside Data Hub the switch Double-click, "A table outside", "A view outside" and "Its field".
- **The colours of the SQL** follow the kind of word, as BigQuery colours it ([DEC-UX-223](#dec-ux-223)): keywords, functions and types blue, text in quotes green, numbers teal, Parameters magenta, comments grey; the names of tables, CTEs and columns keep the colour of the text, so that a Resource stands out by its dotted line, not by a colour. The colours are tokens of their own, `--code-*`, for both themes.
- **Format** writes the SQL in the house layout of the Data Team for dbt (`docs/sql-formatting.md` in immoscoop-dataplatform-data), as far as a formatter goes ([DEC-UX-228](#dec-ux-228)): keywords, functions, types and date parts such as `day` in lower case; an indent of 4 spaces; each clause keyword on a line of its own with its operands under it, one per line; a function call on one line up to 100 characters; `with` on a line of its own, a blank line, and the CTEs not indented; `interval (…)` keeping its space. `where true` stays for the templates ([DEC-UX-141](#dec-ux-141)): adding it would change what the User wrote. A query opens formatted ([DEC-UX-233](#dec-ux-233)): the SQL of their own query is written so as it opens, as a click on Format does, and kept through its Pre-flight, as any change; SQL Format cannot read stays as it is. The query of someone else shows formatted, read only, unless it is in a Project the User is in, where it is theirs to change as well ([DEC-UX-286](#dec-ux-286)); a past run shows as it ran.
- **Adding or taking out a Resource** changes this query, in a sheet ([DEC-UX-287](#dec-ux-287)): to add, a search over the Resources the User may see, where one or more are ticked at once, up to ten Resources in all ([DEC-UX-225](#dec-ux-225)); to take one out, a warning when the SQL still reads it, as that table is then an error until the SQL no longer reads it. Then its Resources as they will be, the Lenses on them, and **a CTE for each Resource added, at the start of the SQL** ([DEC-UX-225](#dec-ux-225)): named after its table, selecting its fields, one a line, and filtering on its recent partitions, as the SQL of a new query does; the sheet shows which CTEs come.
