At its foot, **two buttons side by side** ([DEC-UX-289](#dec-ux-289)): **"Just add"**, the primary one, or "Just take it out", changes this query and keeps its name, its question, its result and its history: its next run reads the new set, and its history keeps what each run read ([OP-UX-102](#op-ux-102)). **"New query"** makes the new set another query ([DEC-UX-73](#dec-ux-73)), named after its Resources and the date ([DEC-UX-21](#dec-ux-21)) and renamed in place, with the question carried along; it takes the SQL and its Parameters along, with the CTEs. The old query stays as it was, with its history; the new one is on top of the panel, not run yet, and its History says where it started from, after which run ([DEC-UX-250](#dec-ux-250)). The sheet says in one line what each button does, and shows the Lenses on the new set, which can be a very different list. "New query from this one", in the quick window, opens the sheet with only "Start the new query".
- **The Pre-flight and Run** are in a bar at the foot of the query, which stays in view while the SQL scrolls ([DEC-UX-70](#dec-ux-70)): a gauge with the two thresholds, 100 MB to approve and 1 GB to the Data Team, as start values ([DEC-SR-29](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#dec-sr-29); [FR-SR-37](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#fr-sr-37)), and the partitions it reads and through which column; then "Run" with what the query reads, "at most" as clustering can make the real read lower, in the button, red with "Approve and run" above the approval threshold, as on the page of a Lens ([DEC-UX-28](#dec-ux-28)). A pop-out on the button says how to read less. **Above 1 GB,** Run becomes "Send to the Data Team", with what the query reads, and opens a pop-out over the bar: "Very large: one run reads more than 1 GB; someone of the Data Team accepts it first", an optional word for the Data Team, and Send. Once sent, the bar says "Waiting for the Data Team since 10:12" and the query in the panel is labelled "To the Data Team"; their answer comes back in the Message Centre, and once accepted the query runs above 1 GB ([DEC-SR-36](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#dec-sr-36), [DEC-UX-116](#dec-ux-116); in the prototype `?pf=request`). A query without a filter on the partition column of a partitioned Resource gets a warning there ([FR-SR-40](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#fr-sr-40)); on a Resource that requires the filter, the bar says so in place of the Pre-flight, names the column with "Add the filter", and Run is off ([DEC-SR-35](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#dec-sr-35); in the prototype `?pf=nofilter`). **The bar keeps one line of a fixed height:** the Pre-flight when the query can run, with at most one warning under it; what stops it when it cannot, such as an error of BigQuery, on at most two lines, with "+1" for any more. The two lie in the same place and cross-fade, the old one a little quicker than the new one, so that the query never changes height while it is typed. Typing is checked once it pauses two seconds; meanwhile the line keeps what it said, softer. A query that opens, a change of its Parameters, "Add the filter" and a run taken over are checked at once ([DEC-UX-154](#dec-ux-154)). **Run never waits for the Pre-flight:** while the Pre-flight of what is written has not come back, or could not be read, the button says "Run" and stays on. A click, or Cmd+Enter, checks the query at once and then does what the Pre-flight says: up to 100 MB it runs and shows the result; above it the button turns red to "Approve and run" with the focus, and a second click runs it; above 1 GB it becomes "Send to the Data Team"; what keeps it from running shows in the bar, and it does not run. A Pre-flight that could not be read says so in the bar, and Run checks again ([DEC-UX-280](#dec-ux-280)).
- **Without a limit, with a warning.** For a User who approves any run themselves, such as a Data Admin ([DEC-PL-12](../requirements/functional/12-permissions-and-library-cards.md#dec-pl-12)), Run stays "Approve and run" in red above 1 GB too, never "Send to the Data Team". The Pre-flight line then carries a warning in the warning colour, "Reads more than 1 GB: check that it has to", and the explanation of Run says it reads more than 1 GB and that they approve it themselves, without a limit. *Added on 2026-11-26, at the request of Max, built without a prototype.*
- **The result** under it is a grid like that of BigQuery, in the style of Data Hub ([DEC-UX-72](#dec-ux-72)), and takes all the height of its pane, its tabs above it and its count and pages under it, however tall the pane is dragged ([DEC-UX-226](#dec-ux-226)):
  - **The columns:** first the row number, then a column per field, its type under its name. A column starts as wide as its values; the edge of its head makes it narrower or wider, and a double click fits it to its values again. A value that does not fit ends in "…" and shows whole on hover.
  - **The rows:** one line each, alternate rows lightly shaded; values as BigQuery gives them, numbers to the right, `null` in grey. The head and the row numbers stay in view while the grid scrolls. A click selects a cell, and Ctrl+C copies it.
  - **The menu of a column** sorts the rows of this page, fits the column, or copies its name. It sorts this page only, and says so: every row in order is a matter of `order by`.
  - **Under it:** how many rows, what was read and how many partitions, the Query ID with a copy button, and the pages: 100 rows a page, read from the same run, with the first, previous, next and last page ([FR-SR-51](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#fr-sr-51)).
  - **In its upper right corner,** in the line of its tabs, while a result shows, of the last run or of a run previewed from the history: a **menu button**, the copy icon with a small caret, named "Copy the result" on hover, which opens a menu under it with **Copy as JSON** and **Copy as CSV**, under a line that says what is copied: "The first 100 of 210 rows, with the names of the fields" ([DEC-UX-292](#dec-ux-292), [DEC-UX-294](#dec-ux-294)). A choice copies and closes the menu. Each copies the first 100 rows of that result, whichever page shows, with the names of its fields, and a toast says how many: "Copied the first 100 of 210 rows as CSV", or "Copied 12 rows as JSON" when it holds fewer. Their hover says the same before the click. **JSON** is a list with an object a row, its fields by name, in their order: an integer or a float as a number, a BOOL as true or false, a list or a record as JSON, `null` as null, anything else as BigQuery writes it, in quotes; a NUMERIC stays text, so that no digit is lost. **CSV** has a header row with the names of the fields, a comma between values and a line break as Windows writes it; a value with a comma, a quote or a line break is put between quotes, a quote in it doubled; `null` is empty; a list or a record is its JSON. Nothing more leaves Data Hub this way than the first 100 rows: the rest is read in Data Hub, page by page, and a structural export goes to the Data Team ([FR-CH-01](../requirements/functional/13-channels.md#fr-ch-01), [FR-CH-02](../requirements/functional/13-channels.md#fr-ch-02)). The Preview of a Resource and the result of a Lens keep no copy ([DEC-UX-120](#dec-ux-120), [DEC-UX-122](#dec-ux-122)).
- **The history,** a tab next to the result, with how many runs ([DEC-UX-74](#dec-ux-74)): every run is a variation of the query, of its values, its SQL, its Resources or more, newest first; a run that read another set of Resources than the one before says what changed, and in a Project each run says who ran it ([OP-UX-102](#op-ux-102), [DEC-UX-286](#dec-ux-286)). Each run is one row: its number and when it ran, with **Preview** and **Apply**; it does not open to its details ([DEC-UX-88](#dec-ux-88)). Preview shows the run read only, its SQL and values, and its result while it is kept ([OP-UX-27](#op-ux-27)), in a bar over the result with Close and Apply, and "Run again" once its result is no longer kept ([DEC-UX-99](#dec-ux-99)); Apply takes its SQL and values into the query, and its next run is a new variation. The run on screen says so instead. At the bottom, when the query was started, and from which query.
- **To build it,** in Svelte ([DEC-PF-28](../requirements/technical/00-platform.md#dec-pf-28)): the editor CodeMirror 6 with its SQL language, a BigQuery dialect on top (backtick names, its functions) and suggestions from the columns Data Hub keeps; formatting with sql-formatter, which knows BigQuery. Both MIT. The Pre-flight as a dry run of BigQuery while typing pauses: it costs nothing and returns the bytes.
- **The box of the SQL folds** open and shut, as the Parameters do; folded, it shows how many lines and CTEs it has ([DEC-UX-76](#dec-ux-76)).
- **A query used as a Lens** says so under its question, with a link to the Lens. "Update the lens" replaces "Make it a lens" and opens a sheet that overwrites the Lens with this query, after its size check; "Lenses on these resources" goes, as the query already is one ([DEC-UX-77](#dec-ux-77), [FR-LE-16](../requirements/functional/09-lenses.md#fr-le-16)).
- **A query on a Resource that is not published** cannot become a Lens: "Make it a lens" is greyed, and a line under its question says which Resource is not published yet, with a link to it, and that a Lens is made only on published Resources. Its Owner publishes it from its page; then the button works ([DEC-UX-218](#dec-ux-218), [DEC-LE-22](../requirements/functional/09-lenses.md#dec-le-22)). On a hover or on keyboard focus, the greyed button says why in a pop-out: that a Lens is made only on published Resources, then each Resource not published yet with its Level, or each table outside Data Hub the query reads, and what makes the button work ([DEC-UX-232](#dec-ux-232)). "Update the lens" does the same when it is greyed for that reason.

### Projects

Screens: [query](screens/query.html), the row on top, the panel and the Project button, with the switch "Row"; and [query-project](screens/query-project.html), the details of a Project, Share and Delete. *Designed on 2026-11-19, at the request of Max ([DEC-UX-282](#dec-ux-282) to [DEC-UX-291](#dec-ux-291); [OP-UX-92](#op-ux-92) to [OP-UX-106](#op-ux-106), all decided).* *Approved on 2026-11-19, by Max: "once registered, start your engines (i mean building :))".* *Made simpler on 2026-11-20, at the request of Max: no flagged queries, the active Project follows what is open, its details as the first tab of the row, and a Project button on every query ([DEC-UX-316](#dec-ux-316) to [DEC-UX-319](#dec-ux-319)).* *Changed on 2026-11-26, at the request of Max: the Danger zone in a sheet behind a bin in the head, and archiving a Project out of your panel, with [query-projects](screens/query-projects.html), All projects ([DEC-UX-354](#dec-ux-354) to [DEC-UX-357](#dec-ux-357)).*

- **A Project** is a set of queries a User works on together, alone or with colleagues, with a name and a sentence on what it is for ([DEC-UX-282](#dec-ux-282)). Its queries are the row on top of every screen of Query while it is active ([DEC-UX-317](#dec-ux-317)). A query is in one Project at most ([OP-UX-93](#op-ux-93)). Its icon is a pair of folders, set apart from the folder of a Collection.
- **The active Project follows what is open** ([DEC-UX-317](#dec-ux-317)): a click on a Project, in the panel or the quick window, makes it active and opens its details, the first tab of its row; opening a query of a Project, from the row, the panel, the history or a link, makes that Project active. Opening a query in no Project, starting a new query that is not made in the Project, or taking the open query out of it, leaves no Project active, and the row goes. There is no button to activate or deactivate a Project, and nothing is kept for it: it is read from what is open.
- **More than ten** ([DEC-UX-283](#dec-ux-283)): the first ten keep ⌘1 to ⌘9 and ⌘0; any further query has no key. A Project holds any number of queries, all of them in its row ([DEC-SR-49](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#dec-sr-49)). The tabs share the width down to a minimum, then scroll sideways, their edges fading, the open one scrolled into view; at the end, "All 14" lists them all, with their key, their state and, in a Project, who made each ([OP-UX-96](#op-ux-96)).
- **No button for the quick window in the row** ([DEC-UX-284](#dec-ux-284)): ⌘⇧P and ⌘K open it from anywhere.
- **The Project button** on every query, in its head before "Make it a lens" ([DEC-UX-318](#dec-ux-318)): a menu button "Project" with what fits the query. In no Project, **Add to project**; in one, **Change project** and **Remove from project**. Add and Change open a sheet with the Projects the User is in, each with how many queries and who is in it, the one it is in left out, and New project with its name: "Add" or "Move" puts the query at the end of the row of that Project, which then is active, and a query leaves the Project it was in, as a query is in one Project at most ([OP-UX-93](#op-ux-93)). Remove takes it out at once, without asking, and it stays with whoever made it, in their history ([DEC-UX-291](#dec-ux-291)); no Project is active then. Add and Change are for who writes the query; Remove for anyone in the Project. The × on a tab of the row removes too.
- **A new query is in no Project** unless it is made in one: by New query on the details of a Project or New query in it in the quick window ([DEC-UX-290](#dec-ux-290), [DEC-UX-293](#dec-ux-293)). Made anywhere else, from the + of the panel, the screen of a new query or New query from this one, it is in no Project, and no Project is active while it is open ([DEC-UX-317](#dec-ux-317)); it joins one with the Project button ([DEC-UX-318](#dec-ux-318)).
- **One order for everyone in a Project,** and so one key for each query: dragging a tab, or a row on its page, changes it for all of them ([OP-UX-95](#op-ux-95)). A query added comes at the end.
- **The breadcrumb** of a query of a Project names it, as a link to its details.
- **In the panel, above the history** ([DEC-UX-285](#dec-ux-285)): "Projects", with a + for New project, then each Project the User is in, as an item: its icon, its name, how many queries and who is in it, "only you", "with Karel Leys and An Claes" or "with 4 colleagues"; the active one comes first and stands out ([DEC-UX-317](#dec-ux-317)). A click makes it active and opens its details; the arrow unfolds its first queries with their keys and "All 14, on its page". The first three, then "Show all 5", which shows the others in the panel itself, so that the history stays in view. A Project the User archived is not among them; under them, a quiet line "All projects · 2 archived" opens All projects, and on hover a Project shows Archive, as a query shows its eye ([DEC-UX-354](#dec-ux-354)). Under them, "History": the queries the User ran, as before, those of a Project among them ([OP-UX-103](#op-ux-103)).
- **The search of the panel looks through Projects and queries together** ([DEC-UX-285](#dec-ux-285)): a Project whose name or description matches stays; one with a matching query unfolds to it; the history opens the periods where something matches. The quick window finds the Projects too.
- **New project,** from the + in the panel, from the Project button of a query or from the quick window, is a sheet: its name, required; what it is for, optional; from a query, that query goes in. "Make it" makes it and opens its details, so that it is active; only the User is in it until they share it.
- **The page of a Project** ([query-project](screens/query-project.html), [DEC-UX-285](#dec-ux-285)), as the page of a folder of a Collection is laid out ([DEC-UX-156](#dec-ux-156)):
  - **Its head:** its icon, its name and what it is for, changed in place by anyone in it ([DEC-UX-162](#dec-ux-162)); **Share**, with the faces of who is in it; **New query**, which makes an empty query in it and opens it ([DEC-UX-290](#dec-ux-290), [OP-UX-105](#op-ux-105)). It is the first tab of the row of the Project ([DEC-UX-319](#dec-ux-319)); there is no button to work in it, as it is active while it is open ([DEC-UX-317](#dec-ux-317)). Then two quiet buttons with only an icon ([DEC-UX-357](#dec-ux-357)): **Archive**, the box, or Back in your panel once archived, and **the bin**, which opens its Danger zone in a sheet.
  - **A line of facts:** how many queries, how many people, who made it and when, and its last run, by whom.
  - **Its queries,** in the order of the row: its key, for the first ten; the query, its name and question; what it reads; who made it; and its last run, by whom, marked as on its tab: running, a result not seen yet, failed, Broken. A row is dragged by its handle to change its place, or moved with Alt and the arrow keys on it. At its end, always in view, **"Remove from project"** ([DEC-UX-291](#dec-ux-291)): the query leaves the Project at once, without asking, and stays with whoever made it, found again in the history of Query; a line under the list says so, with Undo ([OP-UX-106](#op-ux-106)).
  - **Who is in it,** each with who added them and how many of its queries they made, Leave for oneself and × for the others.
  - **Its Danger zone** ([DEC-UX-176](#dec-ux-176), [DEC-UX-357](#dec-ux-357)): not a part of the page, but a sheet from the bin in its head: Leave it, as the others keep it, with the queries you made in it; and Delete it, which says what follows, with an optional reason and "Delete for good" in the sheet itself ([OP-UX-100](#op-ux-100)); on top a line points to Archive for who only wants it out of the panel. Leave next to your name in "In it" and in Share opens it too.
  - A Project just made says it holds nothing yet, and how to add a query; a deleted one says by whom, when and why, and that its queries stay with whoever made them.
- **Archive** ([DEC-UX-354](#dec-ux-354) to [DEC-UX-356](#dec-ux-356); screen [query-projects](screens/query-projects.html)): a Project the User no longer works on leaves their panel, and nobody else's; nothing else changes, its queries work and run as before. It is done with the box in the head of its page, on hover in the panel or in the quick window, at once and without asking, as Back in your panel undoes it. **All projects**, from the quiet line under the Projects in the panel or the quick window, lists every Project the User is in, with a search and three chips: In your panel, Archived and All; an archived one is quieter, says when it was archived, and has "Back in your panel" at its end. **An archived Project opened**, from All projects, a link or a query of it in the history, says on top that you archived it, with "Back in your panel"; it is active while it is open, its row on top and in the panel then, and leaves the panel again after.
- **Share** ([DEC-UX-286](#dec-ux-286)) is a sheet: on top, what being in a Project means; a search over the colleagues of Immoscoop who signed in, each with their address and their Permission group; then everyone in it, with who added them, × to take them out and Leave for oneself. **A colleague whose Permission group does not let them make or run queries can be added all the same,** and Data Hub says so: "cannot run queries" in the warning colour, at the search and in the list, with what it means for them ([OP-UX-98](#op-ux-98)). Whoever is added or taken out gets a Message ([OP-UX-101](#op-ux-101)).
- **Everyone in a Project has every right on it and on its queries** ([DEC-UX-286](#dec-ux-286)): open, change and run them, add and take out queries, change their order, rename and describe it, share it and delete it. **A query runs with the rights of whoever runs it:** their Pre-flight, their approval above 100 MB and their Request above 1 GB. A query that reads a Resource someone in it may not see shows locked to them: its name, who made it, its last run and which Resource they may not see, never its SQL or its rows ([OP-UX-97](#op-ux-97)). Two in one query at once see each other, and a change of the other never replaces what you type ([OP-UX-99](#op-ux-99)).
- **The quick window holds every command of Projects** ([DEC-UX-288](#dec-ux-288), [Quick window](#quick-window)): on a query, Add to project, or Change project and Remove from project, as its Project button; New project on every screen of Query; each Project the User is in, as a place to go to, which makes it active; and, on every screen, **New query in** each Project the User is in, the active one first ([DEC-UX-293](#dec-ux-293)). On the details of a Project: Share it, New query in it, an empty one, Rename it, Change the order of its queries, Leave it and Delete it, which puts the focus on its button. Archive it, or Back in your panel; Leave it and Delete it open the sheet of its Danger zone ([DEC-UX-357](#dec-ux-357)); and All projects on every screen of Query ([DEC-UX-354](#dec-ux-354)).

## Making a Lens

Screen: [lens-new](screens/lens-new.html). Designed on 2026-10-23, to review. The [walkthrough on Confluence](https://immoscoop.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/DATA/pages/1380253717) shows where it sits in the flow from exposure to Lens.

- **From a query.** "Make it a lens" on a query in Query opens this screen, still in the section Query ([DEC-UX-25](#dec-ux-25), [FR-LE-07](../requirements/functional/09-lenses.md#fr-le-07)). The SQL, the Parameters and the Resources of the query come along; "Cancel" goes back to it.
- **Left, the lens:** its name, from the query; the question it answers, from the query; what it shows in a sentence and its tags ([FR-LE-06](../requirements/functional/09-lenses.md#fr-le-06)); its Parameters, taken from the query, each with the name Users see, its type and the default it came with ([DEC-DH-11](../overview/data-hub.md#dec-dh-11)); none is added here, and Users can always change them ([DEC-UX-75](#dec-ux-75)). what it does, step by step, which its author writes, a step a line ([DEC-UX-121](#dec-ux-121), [FR-LE-03](../requirements/functional/09-lenses.md#fr-le-03)); the Collection it goes into, with its colour bar, chosen from the Collections its author owns: a Lens always goes into one of them, even when its Resources are in other Collections ([DEC-CO-20](../requirements/functional/03-collections.md#dec-co-20)); its Resources and its Level, the lowest of theirs ([DEC-DH-10](../overview/data-hub.md#dec-dh-10)).
- **A flow of four steps** ([DEC-UX-229](#dec-ux-229)): the left shows one step at a time, with Back and Next: 1, What it is, its name, question, what it shows, what it does step by step, its Collection and the Resources it uses; 2, Tags; 3, Parameters; 4, Its fields. On the right, above the size check, the four steps by title, each with how far it is: its name and Collection, how many tags, how many Parameters, how many fields and how many of them lack a description, in the signal colour. A click on a step opens it, in any order; nothing but a name keeps the Lens from being published, from any step. In the prototype `?step=2`, `3` or `4`.
- **Its fields, as Users see them** ([DEC-UX-227](#dec-ux-227), [FR-LE-19](../requirements/functional/09-lenses.md#fr-le-19)): under the Parameters, a row for each field of its result, with its type and its description. A field named as a field of its Resources takes that description, the Business Description first, else the one from dbt, and says where it comes from; when more Resources hold that name, that of the first Resource of the query, with a note which others hold it too. Nothing more is worked out from the SQL. For a field no Resource names, "Take one from its Resources" searches the fields of its Resources by name and words, grouped by Resource, each with its type and description, and a click takes that description; "Write one" lets its author write it. "Change" does the same for any field. A field without a description does not keep the Lens from being published. Users see the descriptions on the head of each column and under About ([DEC-UX-230](#dec-ux-230)). In the prototype `?lf=pick`.
- **Its two columns in proportion** ([DEC-UX-336](#dec-ux-336)): the step about 70% of the main area, its steps and the size check about 30%, never wider than their column, so that the panel or a Flow Box beside it never pushes them off the page; one column, the side under the step, when the main area is narrow, whatever the window.
- **Right, the size check** ([FR-LE-08](../requirements/functional/09-lenses.md#fr-le-08)): what one run reads, measured now; a gauge with the two thresholds, 100 MB and 1 GB, as for every query ([DEC-SR-29](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#dec-sr-29)); per Resource the partitions it reads; then the outcome:
  - **Within range:** "Publish lens".
  - **Large:** a warning in the warning colour, what would make it read less, "Publish anyway" and "Back to the query".
  - **Very large:** it waits for the Data Team ([FR-LE-09](../requirements/functional/09-lenses.md#fr-le-09)): an optional word for the Data Team and "Send to the data team".
- **Under it,** that a Lens that later reads more than its range is checked again ([FR-LE-10](../requirements/functional/09-lenses.md#fr-le-10)).
- **An author who owns no Collection yet** ([DEC-UX-111](#dec-ux-111)): in place of "Goes into a Collection you own", a tile "Goes into a new Collection" says so in a sentence and asks for its name, one of their groups as its owner, and its icon; it is private, to share later.
- The prototype shows the three outcomes of the size check as scenarios, with a switch at the bottom, and above it a switch for an author who owns a Collection or none yet (`?owns=none`).

## Page of a Lens

Screen: [lens](screens/lens.html). Designed on 2026-10-23, to review. Where a Lens runs: its page, under Collections ([DEC-UX-26](#dec-ux-26), [FR-LE-11](../requirements/functional/09-lenses.md#fr-le-11), [DEC-UX-66](#dec-ux-66)). It shows the basic flavour of a Lens, a query with Parameters and a table as its result; other flavours, such as filtered or aggregated, come later ([OP-LE-07](../requirements/functional/09-lenses.md#op-le-07)).

- **The panel** is that of Collections: the Collection of the Lens unfolds, and the Lens is marked as the one that is open, as a Resource is ([FR-CO-19](../requirements/functional/03-collections.md#fr-co-19)). The breadcrumb reads Collections / its Collection.
- **Head:** the icon of a Lens, its name with the icon of its Level next to it, without a chip or the name of the Level, which shows on hover, and what it shows in a sentence. No chip of its Collection: a Lens is always seen in its Collection, in the breadcrumb and the panel; who made it and its tags are under About ([DEC-UX-106](#dec-ux-106)). For its Owner Unit only: its name changes with a click on it, as on a query, except while it waits for the Data Team; "Edit more" for what it shows, its steps, tags and the names of its Parameters ([DEC-UX-162](#dec-ux-162)); and "Open in Query", which opens its query for its author, and for anyone else a query of their own from its SQL, used as the Lens from then on ([DEC-UX-27](#dec-ux-27), [DEC-LE-18](../requirements/functional/09-lenses.md#dec-le-18)).
- **Two tabs:** Run, where it opens, and About.
- **Run, on top, the request:** its Parameters in a panel that folds open and shut. Folded, it is one line with each Parameter and its value; open, the Parameters stand side by side, each with the name Users see ([DEC-DH-11](../overview/data-hub.md#dec-dh-11)) and a dropdown, filled in with its default. The dropdown of a date offers a date, or a function with the function sign, worked out at every run: today, today minus a number of days, the start of this week, month or year, the end of the last complete week; each with the date it gives today ([FR-LE-13](../requirements/functional/09-lenses.md#fr-le-13)). A list offers its values. A Parameter that is changed is marked, shows its default under it, and "Default" puts that back. In the lower right corner of the panel, folded or not, "Run" with the Pre-flight in the button itself: "Run · 5.1 GB". Above the approval threshold it turns red and reads "Approve and run · 17.9 GB" ([FR-SR-37](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#fr-sr-37), [DEC-UX-28](#dec-ux-28)); when the table is partitioned, a pop-out on the button then says how many partitions the run reads and which Parameter reads less ([FR-SR-40](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#fr-sr-40)). The Pre-flight takes no room of its own. Parameters are not added here: that is editing, for the Owner Unit.
- **Run, below it, the result:** the grid of Query, with the row number, a column per field with its type and 100 rows a page; a field with a description has a dotted line under its name and shows the description on a hover ([DEC-UX-230](#dec-ux-230)) ([DEC-UX-83](#dec-ux-83), [DEC-LE-08](../requirements/functional/09-lenses.md#dec-le-08)); no chart in v1. Above it when and with which Parameters it ran, and no download in v1 ([DEC-UX-122](#dec-ux-122)); under it what was read and the Query ID with a copy button ([FR-LN-06](../requirements/functional/07-log-and-notifications.md#fr-ln-06)).
- **When a Parameter changes,** the grid fades and says it is still for the old values, until the Lens runs again ([FR-LE-12](../requirements/functional/09-lenses.md#fr-le-12)).
- **Before the first run,** the result says so. A Lens does not run by itself when it is opened: every run is a query, with its Pre-flight ([DEC-LE-12](../requirements/functional/09-lenses.md#dec-le-12)).
- **About:** tiles with the question it answers, what it shows, who made it and when, and its tags; what it does step by step, as its author wrote it ([DEC-UX-121](#dec-ux-121), [FR-LE-03](../requirements/functional/09-lenses.md#fr-le-03)); what it is built on, each Resource with its Collection and Level, and why the Lens has its Level; its Parameters with their type and default; its fields, each with its type, its description and where that comes from, a field of its Resources or its author ([DEC-UX-230](#dec-ux-230)); and its history: who made it from which query; how often it runs is not shown in v1 ([OP-SR-21](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#op-sr-21)).
- **No SQL** on this page, on either tab: a colleague never sees the SQL of a Lens; its Owner Unit sees it when editing ([DEC-UX-27](#dec-ux-27), [FR-LE-14](../requirements/functional/09-lenses.md#fr-le-14)).
- **Edit more** ([lens-edit](screens/lens-edit.html)) opens a page of its own, for its Owner Unit and the Data Team, as the same four steps as making it ([DEC-UX-231](#dec-ux-231)): What it is, which stays in its Collection; Tags; Parameters, the names users see; and Its fields, each with its description, taken or written as when it was made. On the right the steps with how far each is, then what one run of its version reads, that its SQL changes through its query, and Save ([DEC-UX-162](#dec-ux-162)). A Lens made before its fields had descriptions, and a field a new version added, get the description of a field of its Resources of the same name, to save; the others wait for one.
- **Delete, under Save on Edit** ([DEC-UX-185](#dec-ux-185), [FR-LE-18](../requirements/functional/09-lenses.md#fr-le-18)): a small button, "Delete this lens…", in the signal colour, opens a sheet ([DEC-UX-174](#dec-ux-174)). First what deleting does: gone for everyone, from every list, search, Claude and Excel; how many colleagues used it in the last 30 days, also from Excel, who are told; and what is kept, its runs, its history and the Messages on it. Then why, optional, and in its foot "Keep it" and "Delete for good". Deleted, the User lands on its Collection.
- **A deleted Lens** ([lens-deleted](screens/lens-deleted.html)): its address shows, to whoever saw it, its name and that it was deleted, by whom, when and why, that it no longer runs, also not in Excel, and that its runs and the Messages on it are kept, with the way to its Collection and to those Messages. Anyone else finds nothing, as for a Lens that does not exist.
- **When the Lens stopped,** because a run with its defaults grew past 1 GB: a chip "Stopped" next to its Level, a callout in the warning colour with what one run reads now and since when, that it waits for the Data Team, and for its Owner Unit "Open its query"; Run off, and in place of the result that it waits ([DEC-UX-125](#dec-ux-125), [DEC-LE-11](../requirements/functional/09-lenses.md#dec-le-11)).
- **When the Lens is Broken,** because it reads a Resource or a column that no longer exists: a red chip "Broken" next to its Level, an urgent callout above the tabs with what is missing and since which dbt run, "Open its query" for its Owner Unit, Run off, and in place of the result why it cannot run ([DEC-UX-81](#dec-ux-81), [FR-LE-17](../requirements/functional/09-lenses.md#fr-le-17)).
- The prototype shows the result up to date, after a change, before the first run, while choosing the value of To, Broken (`?state=broken`) and stopped (`?state=stopped`), with a switch at the bottom, and the page as the Owner Unit or a colleague. In the lists, the Lens "Response time and conversion" is Broken.

## Charts

Screen: [charts](screens/charts.html). Charts is the gallery of semgraph, the Chart Server ([DEC-UX-89](#dec-ux-89), [DEC-PF-23](../requirements/technical/00-platform.md#dec-pf-23)): the charts it draws, each for one kind of question, so that a User sees which one answers theirs. Drawn on 2026-11-04 and approved by Max the same day, as drawn ([DEC-UX-182](#dec-ux-182), [FR-CH-06](../requirements/functional/13-channels.md#fr-ch-06)).

- **What semgraph says, in the language of the User:** the charts, with their names, icons and the question each answers, the questions they are for, what to use instead, what they need, and their examples, drawn as semgraph draws them. Data Hub adds the groups and the words around them, and draws nothing itself ([DEC-PF-23](../requirements/technical/00-platform.md#dec-pf-23)).
- **The panel** says in one line what the charts are, then lists them by the question they answer: Over time, Comparing, Now and the target, and Spread, each with its icon and name; the one that is open is marked. Charts opens on the first, as Admin opens on its first tool. A chart semgraph adds is placed in a group of this design before Charts lists it.
- **A chart opened** shows on the right the breadcrumb Charts · its group, its icon, its name with its id under it, and the question it answers in a sentence.
- **Its examples,** to switch between, on a card: each drawn by semgraph in the theme of the page and placed in the page as the SVG it drew, so that its hover, its metadata panels and its links work as semgraph made them ([DEC-UX-184](#dec-ux-184)); at its own size, centred on the card: never stretched wider, only made smaller where the card is narrower ([DEC-UX-183](#dec-ux-183)). Under the picture, a chip when semgraph drew it with a warning, a deviation from the chart language, or with a caution in red; in the place of the picture, when semgraph refuses it, that it does and the code of why. An example that semgraph keeps only to show a theme or a size is not listed, as every example is drawn in the theme of the page. What an example shows, in words, is still open ([OP-UX-54](#op-ux-54)).
- **Beside it:** For questions such as, its example questions; Not for, each with what to use instead, a link to that chart, or its id and "not in semgraph yet" when semgraph only plans it; What it needs, the blocks computed in BigQuery and at least how many; and Where you meet it: in Claude, later on a Lens or a Report, and at `POST /render` by its id. Below 1100 pixels wide it comes under the examples.

## EDA

*Decided on 2026-11-16, by Max, as a concept, and drawn on 2026-11-21 for the start of an EDA and its page ([OP-UX-91](#op-ux-91)). It follows [EDA](../requirements/functional/15-eda.md): its EDA-Steps and EDA-Probes ([DEC-ED-11](../requirements/functional/15-eda.md#dec-ed-11)), how an EDA-Probe is configured ([DEC-ED-13](../requirements/functional/15-eda.md#dec-ed-13)), the section ([DEC-ED-15](../requirements/functional/15-eda.md#dec-ed-15)) and the health of an EDA ([DEC-ED-16](../requirements/functional/15-eda.md#dec-ed-16)). Screen: [eda-probe-sketches](screens/eda-probe-sketches.html), the drawings.*

- **A section of its own,** after Charts in the rail and before Monitoring, with the binoculars icon, built like Monitoring ([DEC-ED-15](../requirements/functional/15-eda.md#dec-ed-15)). Its panel is the index: the Resources the User may explore, by Collection, each with the dot of where its EDA stands, then those at 3 · Confirmed without an EDA, under Ready for an EDA. A Resource opens its EDA on the right.
- **Every EDA-Probe is drawn on one schematic table** ([DEC-UX-268](#dec-ux-268)): fine lines, rows and columns, and in the header the kind of each field as an icon: a fingerprint for what identifies a row, a calendar for a date, a tag for a category or text, a hash for a number, a link for a field that refers to another Resource. A key stays the mark of a Key Field ([DEC-UX-266](#dec-ux-266)). The EDA-Probe draws in the cells what it looks at and what a finding looks like, such as two rows of the same colour where the Grain does not hold. The drawing teaches the idea and is the same on every Resource.
- **Starting an EDA** ([DEC-UX-320](#dec-ux-320)): mostly from the Resource itself, on the maturity ladder of its Overview, where 4 · Explored is the next step. Who may explore Resources (`eda.use`) sees Start the EDA; anyone else reads that a Data Admin takes it through its EDA ([DEC-ED-25](../requirements/functional/15-eda.md#dec-ed-25)). Starting creates the EDA, In progress, and opens it in the section EDA. While it is in progress, the ladder says how many needed EDA-Probes are complete, with a bar, and Open its EDA. Screens: [resource-overview](screens/resource-overview.html) with `?ov=maturity&lvl=three` and `&lvl=eda`.
- **The page of the EDA of a Resource** ([DEC-UX-321](#dec-ux-321)): a guiding page, whose picture is the progress of the EDA-Setup and the quality of the EDA. Its status beside the title: In progress until the EDA-Setup is complete, then Explored ([DEC-ED-20](../requirements/functional/15-eda.md#dec-ed-20)). Tabs, as on a Resource: **Overview**, filled in later; **Steps**; and **Profile**, greyed until the EDA-Setup is complete, saying what will come there, the runs and their EDA-Profiles among it. Screen: [eda](screens/eda.html).
- **Steps** ([DEC-UX-322](#dec-ux-322)), in two columns:
  - **on the left, 70%,** a row per EDA-Step, in the order of [DEC-ED-11](../requirements/functional/15-eda.md#dec-ed-11), each saying how many of its needed EDA-Probes are complete. Its EDA-Probes set up are cards: the drawing, its columns named by kind, such as id, date or count, never by the fields of this Resource ([DEC-UX-330](#dec-ux-330)), and its result on it, the result in a sentence, how Data Hub reads it, as a chip above its name, which always takes the room of two lines, and the fields it is set on. After them, while the EDA-Step has EDA-Probes left to add, **one empty tile** of the same size, with a dotted grey edge and a grey background, Add probe in the middle and what an EDA-Probe is, at half opacity until it is hovered or focused ([DEC-UX-331](#dec-ux-331)). It opens the list to add an EDA-Probe ([DEC-UX-328](#dec-ux-328));
  - **on the right, 30%,** the Resource and the state of its EDA-Setup: how many needed EDA-Probes are complete, as a number and a bar; what the EDA covers in the four places ([DEC-ED-16](../requirements/functional/15-eda.md#dec-ed-16)); the best next step, with Add this EDA-Probe. Under it, the EDA-Probes on **the table as a whole**, each added, needed, suggested or on request; and **per field**, in the order of the Resource Card, what covers it per EDA-Step, the Key Fields marked with a key and what they still need dashed, the other fields quieter.
- **The detail of an EDA-Probe** ([DEC-UX-324](#dec-ux-324)) opens in the tab Steps, in place of its rows; the state of the EDA-Setup belongs to those rows and is not repeated. Two columns. **On the left, 40%:** what it does, with the drawing of its card, smaller, animated once with Replay; why it matters; its parameters, the fields it is set on among them; and **Run**, which adds it to the EDA and runs it, then Run again. An EDA-Probe without parameters of its own, such as The Grain holds, has Run alone. The left column folds to a strip, giving the result the whole width. **On the right, 60%:** before it has run, where its result will come; after, **the conclusion on top**: Data Hub's reading and a sentence with the key numbers; under it **the result drawn**, with what is wrong shaded on the chart itself, so that it needs no separate picture of good and wrong; then the numbers, up to 5 examples, and the Owner's reading: Expected, Known or A problem. A chart never grows beyond the size it is drawn at, so that its words stay the size of the text around it on a wide screen; it shrinks on a narrow one ([DEC-UX-332](#dec-ux-332)). For The Grain holds the chart is how many Grain values are on one row, two, three or more. Screen: [eda-probe](screens/eda-probe.html).
- **A result on several fields has two forms** ([DEC-UX-326](#dec-ux-326)): **a row per field** when its fields need no chart of their own, such as Nulls per Key Field: a bar of filled and empty, the numbers, Data Hub's reading with why, and the Owner's where there is something to read ([eda-probe-nulls](screens/eda-probe-nulls.html)); **a block per field** when each needs its own chart, such as Impossible values. Both in the order of the Resource Card.
- **The list to add an EDA-Probe** ([DEC-UX-328](#dec-ux-328)): Add probe opens a sheet with a search, which has the focus, at the EDA-Step of its tile, every EDA-Step in its order below and above it. Each EDA-Probe its model type is offered: its name, the question it asks of this Resource, Needed, Suggested or On request, and on the right its state: added, with Data Hub's reading; set on part of what it needs; Open, to add it; or Coming, quieter, while Data Hub does not run it, and still counted ([DEC-ED-27](../requirements/functional/15-eda.md#dec-ed-27)). Filters: All, Needed, Data Hub runs it, Added. A click opens its detail, where Run adds it. Screen: [eda](screens/eda.html) with `?list=open`.
- **The guide of an EDA** ([DEC-UX-327](#dec-ux-327)): the [Flow Box](#flow-box) with the flow **Set up its EDA**, its icon the binoculars of the section. One step per needed EDA-Probe, in the order of the EDA-Steps and grouped under their names; done with Data Hub's reading and the result in a few words, open with what it misses, or Coming with a dashed number. The step to do now is the best next step of the EDA, the same as its page proposes; it unfolds What, Why and How, its Learning Card where one is written, and Open the EDA-Probe. Starting the EDA opens it. On the tab Steps of its EDA it is folded to its strip, as the page shows the same; on the detail of an EDA-Probe, where its step says it is open on the page, and on every other page it is open. Close takes it away; Guide me, beside the title of the page of the EDA, brings it back. Screens: [eda](screens/eda.html) (`?guide=min`, `open`, `closed`) and [eda-probe](screens/eda-probe.html).
- **The EDA in Claude** ([DEC-UX-329](#dec-ux-329)): an MCP App, as a Resource is: what needs attention first, then per EDA-Step the EDA-Probes added in words and numbers, without charts; a run from Claude shows the result of that EDA-Probe with the buttons of the User's reading, and asks the User to approve what reads more than runs freely. Screen: [mcp-eda](screens/mcp-eda.html).
- **The charts of the EDA are illustrations** ([DEC-UX-325](#dec-ux-325)), drawn for the EDA and its EDA-Probes, not charts of semgraph: they teach what a result means as much as they show it. Where one later needs to be a real chart, it is revisited then, once semgraph is further.

## Monitoring

*A proposal of 2026-11-07, drawn from the requirements of [Checks and monitoring](../requirements/functional/08-checks-and-monitoring.md); its open points are [OP-UX-63](#op-ux-63) to [OP-UX-68](#op-ux-68). Redrawn the same day for the chain Monitor, Breakdown, Metric, Rule, Action ([DEC-CM-03](../requirements/functional/08-checks-and-monitoring.md#dec-cm-03)), and the build flow again for business Users, in seven steps over a picture ([DEC-CM-23](../requirements/functional/08-checks-and-monitoring.md#dec-cm-23), [DEC-UX-195](#dec-ux-195)). Screens: [monitoring-start](screens/monitoring-start.html), [monitor](screens/monitor.html), [monitor-build](screens/monitor-build.html), and the part Monitoring of [resource-overview](screens/resource-overview.html).*

- **A section of its own,** after EDA in the rail, with the pulse icon and the number of Findings of today, for a User who may manage Monitors ([OP-UX-63](#op-ux-63), [FR-CM-29](../requirements/functional/08-checks-and-monitoring.md#fr-cm-29)). Its panel is the index: the Monitors of the Resources the User may manage, by Collection, each with the dot of its Operational Status, then the Resources of their Owner Units without one. A Monitor opens on the right, as everything else does.
- **The start screen** shows what the panel cannot: an intro that can be dismissed; Today, the Findings of today, each in its Tone, with whom its Actions told, its Rule and its Resource, to mark useful or noise ([FR-CM-17](../requirements/functional/08-checks-and-monitoring.md#fr-cm-17)); Lists, the Rules that give a list, read rather than solved ([FR-CM-14](../requirements/functional/08-checks-and-monitoring.md#fr-cm-14)); To tune, what the Health Overviews propose, and the values in warm-up ([FR-CM-08](../requirements/functional/08-checks-and-monitoring.md#fr-cm-08)); and Suggestions, in the words of the Owner's creed: "people rely on this", never "you forgot this" ([FR-CM-30](../requirements/functional/08-checks-and-monitoring.md#fr-cm-30)).
- **The Tone colours a judgement** ([OP-UX-64](#op-ux-64)): opportunity in teal with a sparkle, informative in slate with an i, attention in the ochre of the signal colour with a circled exclamation mark, problem in the urgent red with an octagon. It colours a Finding, its dot, chip, square and band, and the zone of a boundary on the gauge of a picture, never the navigation. Healthy is an empty ring; warm-up a dashed one. The values in a picture are three shades of olive, which judge nothing ([DEC-UX-197](#dec-ux-197)); nothing else in monitoring has colour.
- **A Monitor** has a head that says whether its Resource can be trusted now: its Operational Status, its Level, its last run, how many ways it is watched and how many boundaries it has, and what they read a month; and three tabs: Findings, a list with the one opened beside it, as in the Message Centre, its Metric drawn with the band of its Rule; What it watches, a picture per way it is watched ([OP-UX-68](#op-ux-68)); and Health ([FR-CM-18](../requirements/functional/08-checks-and-monitoring.md#fr-cm-18)).
- **What it watches is pictures** ([OP-UX-68](#op-ux-68), [FR-CM-03](../requirements/functional/08-checks-and-monitoring.md#fr-cm-03)): per way the Resource is watched, a Breakdown never called so, named by when it checks and what it is per, such as "every weekday, per partner", with its time column, how far its data is complete, what a run reads and its Aggregate Table in one line. Under it, its picture, the same as in the seven steps: the strip, a row per measure, a Metric, with its boundaries, its Rules, on a gauge, each with its reactions, its Actions, and how often it fired in 30 days; and the sentence that reads it back. Every part changes by clicking it ([DEC-UX-196](#dec-ux-196)). "Another measure" adds a row; a way of watching at another time or for other values starts in the seven steps.
- **Building a Monitor takes the main area,** in seven steps of one question each, with the panel away, not a sheet ([OP-UX-65](#op-ux-65), [FR-CM-20](../requirements/functional/08-checks-and-monitoring.md#fr-cm-20), [DEC-CM-23](../requirements/functional/08-checks-and-monitoring.md#dec-cm-23)): Resource, How often, Period, Per, Measure, Boundaries and Reactions. It starts from a proposal of Data Hub or a draft of Claude, said in a callout on top. The Owner never chooses a time column or sees a Breakdown: the first step says which time Data Hub counts by, as the Data Team set it. **The first step is a search** over the Resources the User may manage, by name, Collection or field, with All, Without a monitor and With monitors, and a row per Resource with its Collection, its Level and its monitors; it has no picture, which shows from the second step on ([DEC-UX-204](#dec-ux-204)). **The times to check at** are those of one list for every monitor, kept in Terraform, a scheduled job each ([DEC-CM-28](../requirements/functional/08-checks-and-monitoring.md#dec-cm-28)). Started from the tab Monitor of a Resource, the steps open on the second, the Resource chosen; Edit opens a monitor with every step done.
- **The picture of a monitor** ([DEC-UX-195](#dec-ux-195)) stands above the steps from the second on, gaining one layer per step, and on the page of a Monitor, whole; one drawing, `screens/monitor-picture.js`, serves both. It is abstract and not the data. **The strip** is equal tiles that end with today. In days: M T W T F S S, Saturday and Sunday greyer, and under the letters the calendar weeks, w44, w45; in weeks: a tile per calendar week, its number under it, and under those the months, a week counting in the month of its Thursday. Today, or this week, and what is not complete yet are dotted and empty, never judged. **The marks:** a check judges one value ([DEC-CM-29](../requirements/functional/08-checks-and-monitoring.md#dec-cm-29)): a dot for one day or week, and for a stretch the same dot on its last tile with a tail back over the others, a line with round ends, lower than the dot ([DEC-UX-206](#dec-ux-206)). What is judged is solid; what it is compared with is hollow, a white fill in a border, in the same shape, shifted back by as many tiles as it is earlier ([DEC-CM-30](../requirements/functional/08-checks-and-monitoring.md#dec-cm-30)); everything before opens to the left with a gradient, and so does a stretch that starts before the strip; a fixed number is a dashed line across it; all values together a ring around the last tile. **The values:** three shades of olive stand for however many there are, never one each ([DEC-UX-197](#dec-ux-197)); left out by a filter, only or all except, they turn dotted, and so do their dots at the end of the strip. **Under the strip,** a row per measure: its sign, what it measures and what it is compared with, and its boundaries on a gauge, a short bar with the reference as a hollow ring in the middle, each boundary a line with an arrow, rises up and drops down, higher than and lower than as dashed lines from the top, its zone hatched in its Tone, and beside it an icon per reaction. A layer of a later step that was set already shows faded. Under it all, the sentence that reads the monitor back.
- **A monitor is changed by clicking its picture** ([DEC-UX-196](#dec-ux-196)): each part, the letters, the window, the values, what it is compared with, a boundary, its reactions, a measure, says on a hover what it is and that a click changes it, with a dashed outline around it. A click opens the floating window of a Parameter ([Floating window](#floating-window)), one at a time, with the choices of that part as options; the picture changes at once, Save keeps it, × or Esc undoes it. Nothing is dragged. In the seven steps a click opens the step of that part instead; so does each part of the sentence.
- **A boundary is a sentence** ([OP-UX-66](#op-ux-66), [FR-CM-12](../requirements/functional/08-checks-and-monitoring.md#fr-cm-12), [DEC-CM-24](../requirements/functional/08-checks-and-monitoring.md#dec-cm-24)): "When it drops 30% compared with 7 days earlier", with a choice from a few values for each part, and a minimum to be judged above them. Under them, how often they would have warned in the past 12 weeks, as a sentence and as the chart of one value with its band and the days it would have fired ([FR-CM-16](../requirements/functional/08-checks-and-monitoring.md#fr-cm-16)); "unusual" is explained, with the Learning Card ([FR-CM-15](../requirements/functional/08-checks-and-monitoring.md#fr-cm-15)). Reactions are tiles to switch on, several per boundary, Block it greyed as later, and the tone under them.
- **The tab Monitor of a Resource** ([resource-monitor](screens/resource-monitor.html), [DEC-UX-205](#dec-ux-205)): on the Resource itself, in its Collection, every monitor that watches it, each read back as a sentence, with its Operational Status, its last run, its boundaries, what they found in 30 days and what a run reads; Edit opens the steps on it, Open in Monitoring its page; New monitor opens the steps with the Resource chosen. A monitor that stopped stays listed, greyed, with why. Without a monitor, the tab says what one does and what Data Hub proposes ([OP-UX-73](#op-ux-73), [OP-UX-74](#op-ux-74)).
- **On the page of a Resource,** its status, not its setup ([OP-UX-67](#op-ux-67), [FR-CM-28](../requirements/functional/08-checks-and-monitoring.md#fr-cm-28)): a part Monitoring in the Overview, after Maturity, with its Operational Status, a strip of its last 30 buckets and its last run, and "Open monitoring". A Finding of a Rule that tells the Consumers also shows in the header, in its Tone, as a callout under it, and first in the Summary, as Read first.

## Learning

The Learning Centre ([DEC-LR-04](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#dec-lr-04)). Its philosophy and guiding principles are on [Learning-centre: filosofie en guiding principles](https://immoscoop.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/DATA/pages/1381138494) (Confluence, Dutch); this design applies them ([DEC-LR-09](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#dec-lr-09)). Screens:

- [learning-start](screens/learning-start.html): the start screen with the panel.
- [learning-card](screens/learning-card.html): a Learning Card, in five steps.
- [learning-see](screens/learning-see.html): See it, opened on its own from the context in Data Hub.
- [learning-message](screens/learning-message.html): a Message that carries a Learning Card.
- [learning-float](screens/learning-float.html): a card opened from a pop-out, in a floating window over the page.
- [learning-mcp](screens/learning-mcp.html): the steps of a card in Claude, as an MCP App.
- [learning-hover](screens/learning-hover.html): the marker and its pop-out, on a label, a chip and a button.
- [learning-family](screens/learning-family.html): the family of a card under Where to next, and cards coming soon (proposal).
- [learning-attention](screens/learning-attention.html) and [learning-attention-resource](screens/learning-attention-resource.html): the attention box for the Owner, on a Collection and on a Resource not published yet (proposal).

Designed on 2026-10-24 and 2026-10-25, to review ([DEC-UX-38](#dec-ux-38) to [DEC-UX-57](#dec-ux-57), [Learning](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md)).

- **In the rail,** the learning centre is the fifth main item on top, after Messages, with a graduation cap ([DEC-UX-89](#dec-ux-89), [OP-UX-37](#op-ux-37)).
- **The panel is the index of every Learning Card** ([FR-LR-03](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#fr-lr-03)):
  - On top the title, search, and one silent count: "You have seen 14 of 113 cards" ([DEC-UX-51](#dec-ux-51), [FR-LR-32](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#fr-lr-32)).
  - Then the Compass, then the four Learning Levels from easy to advanced, each a card in the tree, as a Collection is: its name with its step, the icon of its audience ([DEC-UX-62](#dec-ux-62)), how many of its cards the User has seen, and an arrow to unfold it.
  - Unfolded, it lists its sections, each with its number, name and how many of its cards the User has seen, and under each its cards: the icon of their kind (a book for a Learning Card, a compass for a card of the Compass, a chart for a Reading Card), the number and the name on one line, and a check when the User said "I understand it".
  - The card that is open is in medium weight and the darkest colour; opening a card unfolds its Learning Level.
  - A Learning Level gets no colour: colour stays on content. Its icon tells the audience: a person for 1 · User (`user`), a person in focus for 2 · Data-aware user (`user-focus`), a scatter chart for 3 · Data analyst (`chart-scatter`), a person with a gear for 4 · Data owner and expert (`user-gear`), from Phosphor, as everywhere. The step is in the name.
- **The page of a Learning Card, in five steps** ([DEC-UX-45](#dec-ux-45), [DEC-UX-48](#dec-ux-48), [FR-LR-27](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#fr-lr-27)): light, one thing at a time, over the whole width of the page.
  - On top, the way to it (learning centre, Learning Level, section); the number and title; and "I understand it", the only button that is always there ([FR-LR-28](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#fr-lr-28)). Marked, it becomes a quiet "Understood" with a check; a click undoes it. There are no labels.
  - Under the title, the five steps in a row, an arrow between each: a numbered circle and a name. The current step has a filled circle and medium weight; a step before it has a check. A click opens a step.
  - Next to the steps, the depth: "Quick" or "Full". On the quick path See it and Check your understanding are muted in the row, and can still be opened.
  - **Why now:** the sentence of a colleague, large, then the situation, and "In this card: …".
  - **Core idea:** the decision rule, large; the metaphor; the one example. A card whose topic the reader sees on a screen of Data Hub, such as the red dot of Broken, may go without the metaphor: the screen is its physical part, and the example then stands alone under the rule ([DEC-LR-36](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#dec-lr-36)).
  - **See it:** the example as a chart, its relevant part highlighted and the rest faded, with "Look at …" and "You see …" beside it. A quiet link plays the video, if the card has one; not in version 1: the prototypes show the link, the build leaves it out ([OP-LR-20](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#op-lr-20)). It is the same page as See it opened from context.
  - **Check your understanding:** one question per screen ([FR-LR-30](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#fr-lr-30)). The answers are buttons; a click reveals the answer and why. "Another question" follows, and nothing is counted. A try-it question names what to open, with a link to it, and asks what the User saw.
  - **You can now** ([DEC-UX-49](#dec-ux-49), [FR-LR-31](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#fr-lr-31)): what the User can now do, large; "What it brings you"; and "Where to next", two or three choices as tiles: deeper, a neighbouring concept, and "Just apply it". At the foot, quiet: "Share in a message", "Messages" with their number, and the source. A small drawing of two overlapping circles stands beside the first field, while it is open ([OP-LR-21](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#op-lr-21)).
  - **"Next", always in the same place:** a bar at the foot of the page, held to the bottom of the window: "Next: " with the name of the next step and one sentence on what it brings; the button on the right. It never waits for an answer. On the quick path, "Next" on Core idea leads to You can now. You can now has no "Next", only its choices.
- **See it on its own** ([DEC-UX-50](#dec-ux-50), [FR-LR-29](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#fr-lr-29)): "See it" on an item of a pop-out opens See it as a page of its own in the learning centre: the way to it, "See it" with the title of the card, the example with "Look at …" and "You see …", and two ways on: back to where the User came from, and "Open the whole card".
- **In a Message** ([DEC-UX-41](#dec-ux-41), [FR-LR-07](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#fr-lr-07)): "Add a learning card" next to the field of a new Message finds a card by its words. In the Message it shows as a small card: the book, its number and title, its Learning Level, and "Understood" when the reader marked it ([OP-LR-04](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#op-lr-04)). A click opens the card.
- **Messages on a card** ([FR-LR-06](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#fr-lr-06)): "Messages" opens the slide-in, as on every object; a card takes a Question or a Remark, which go to the Data Team.
- **A card wherever it helps** ([DEC-UX-56](#dec-ux-56), [DEC-UX-57](#dec-ux-57), [FR-LR-35](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#fr-lr-35), [FR-LR-36](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#fr-lr-36)):
  - **Only system text:** a button, a label, a chip or a status Data Hub writes itself can carry the marker; text that Users provide never does. On the page of a Resource, for example, its Level, its Grain and "Mark as seen". A name, such as `creditLead`, always carries one, with at least its own card ([DEC-UX-59](#dec-ux-59), [FR-LR-37](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#fr-lr-37)). A term of the dictionary carries one wherever it shows, on a button or a link too, where the ⓘ follows the text, outside it ([DEC-UX-340](#dec-ux-340)).
  - **The marker:** ⓘ (U+24D8, circled small i) right after the text ([OP-UX-22](#op-ux-22)), a Unicode character in the colour of its text, at about three quarters of its size and slightly raised, from a symbol font, as Poppins has none. No underline and no colour. Hovering the text tints it lightly, so its extent shows.
  - **A one-second hold:** the pop-out opens only when the pointer rests on the text for a second, so passing over the page opens nothing. Keyboard focus opens it at once.
  - **A text bubble:** the pop-out hangs under the text, or above it near the foot of the window, and its arrow points at the marker. On its head, quietly, how many cards explain the text: "Explained in 3 learning cards".
  - **An item per card,** each a tile of its own: the book, the number and title of the card, and "See it" on the right, on every item; the gain of the card, what knowing it brings, the same sentence wherever the card appears; and in grey whether and when the User saw it, with an eye for seen and a check for understood. At the foot, "Open the learning centre".
  - It stays open while the pointer is on it, and closes when the pointer leaves, on Escape or on scrolling. On a touch screen a tap on the marker opens it. A button keeps its own action; only its marker opens the pop-out when tapped.
  - **A click on an item** opens the card in a floating window over the page, as a Message opens in context ([DEC-UX-30](#dec-ux-30)); the User stays where they are. The text stays tinted while its card is open.
  - The window has on its head the graduation cap, "Learning card", the number and title, fold and close. Its body holds the same five steps as the page ([DEC-UX-47](#dec-ux-47)): the numbered circles with the name of the current step, and "I understand it" next to them, then the step.
  - At its foot the bar with "Next", and "Open in the learning centre", which opens the full page of the card.
- **A card pushed where the User acts** ([DEC-LR-32](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#dec-lr-32), [FR-LR-42](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#fr-lr-42)): three strengths, from quiet to strong. The marker explains system text; the card under a Message says what is happening; the attention box asks whoever can act to act. *A proposal of 2026-11-11, drawn from what Max decided after trying the learning-card skill; its open points are [OP-UX-75](#op-ux-75) to [OP-UX-77](#op-ux-77).*
  - **The attention box** ([learning-attention](screens/learning-attention.html), [learning-attention-resource](screens/learning-attention-resource.html), [FR-LR-43](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#fr-lr-43), [OP-UX-75](#op-ux-75)): only for whoever can act, on the page where they act. A card edged in the signal colour, with a circled exclamation mark: what to do, in one sentence; why, in the gain of its Learning Card; what it is about, each with the button that does it, such as Publish; and the card as a tile: the book, number and title, its minutes, and "Open the card", which opens it in the floating window. It folds to one line and stays while the situation lasts. Whoever cannot act keeps the quiet line of Needs attention.
  - **Coming soon** ([learning-family](screens/learning-family.html), [FR-LR-41](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#fr-lr-41), [OP-UX-76](#op-ux-76)): a card still to write shows its number and title, muted, with "Coming soon", in the panel, in a family and in a pop-out; it has no page and no "See it". Beside it the quiet button "I want this card"; pressed, it reads "You asked for it" with how many colleagues want it, and sends the request to the Data Team ([MSG-LRN-01](../requirements/functional/message-catalogue.md#msg-lrn-01)).
  - **The family** ([learning-family](screens/learning-family.html), [FR-LR-40](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#fr-lr-40), [OP-UX-77](#op-ux-77)): under the tiles of Where to next, "In the same family", a quiet list of every card of the family in the order of the plan: the open one marked "This card", a written one as a link with its minutes and an eye or a check when the User saw or understood it, one coming soon greyed with "I want this card".
- **The steps stand alone** ([DEC-UX-47](#dec-ux-47), [DEC-LR-08](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#dec-lr-08)): each step is a block that needs nothing around it. In Claude an MCP App shows them with only the number and title of the card above, its five steps, and "Next" at the foot, with "Open in Data Hub" ([FR-LR-26](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#fr-lr-26)).
- **No game** ([DEC-LR-09](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#dec-lr-09)): no score, badge, rank, weekly goal, streak, team progress or note of success, and no red dot; only the silent count of the cards seen. This replaces the game elements of [DEC-UX-43](#dec-ux-43).

## Messages

Screens: [messages](screens/messages.html), the Message Centre; and the Messages on an object in a slide-in, on [a Collection](screens/collection-messages.html), [a Resource](screens/resource-messages.html) and [a Lens](screens/lens-messages.html); and a Message opened in context, [a Question on a Lens](screens/lens-context.html) and [a Request on a Lens to accept](screens/lens-new-context.html). Designed on 2026-10-23, to review ([DEC-UX-29](#dec-ux-29), [DEC-UX-30](#dec-ux-30)).

- **What a Message is:** a Question, Remark, Signal or Request on an object, or a reply, to enrich the data and inform colleagues. Never private, never a chat with a person; it is about a whole object, never a part of it ([DEC-CV-04](../requirements/functional/14-conversations.md#dec-cv-04), [DEC-CV-05](../requirements/functional/14-conversations.md#dec-cv-05)).
- **The Message Centre** is in the rail, under the main items, with the number unread on its icon. Its panel filters: For you; Unread, which it opens on, and which says "Nothing to catch up on" when it is empty ([DEC-UX-136](#dec-ux-136)); Written by you; Everything you may see; then by kind, Questions, Remarks, Signals, Requests and what Data Hub tells you; and by the kind of object, Collections, Resources, Lenses, Queries ([FR-CV-06](../requirements/functional/14-conversations.md#fr-cv-06)).
- **Its list:** each Message with its object (the icon of its kind, the kind, the name and its Collection), its kind and its state (open, answered, closed), who and when, and its first lines; a dot when it is unread, the number of replies. Chips on top: all, open, answered, closed.
- **Closing a Question or a Signal** ([DEC-UX-179](#dec-ux-179), [FR-CV-16](../requirements/functional/14-conversations.md#fr-cv-16)): its writer, the Owner Unit of its object and the Data Team have "Close" on an open Question or Signal, at the top right of the Message, wherever it shows: in the Message Centre, opened in context and in the slide-in. It asks for an optional line why, and says that its writer gets a notification when someone else closes it. Closed, the conversation shows "Closed" with who closed it, when and why, and "Reopen" for the same people, which makes it open again. Replying stays possible and leaves it closed.
- **The Message opened** shows on the right: its object with "Open in context"; the Message with its replies; a field to reply, with who gets a notification. In the prototype the Request and the Question on a Lens open there.
- **Open in context** opens the page of the object as it is, with the Message in a floating window in the lower right ([DEC-UX-30](#dec-ux-30), [FR-CV-09](../requirements/functional/14-conversations.md#fr-cv-09)). The window has the kind of Message and its object on top; the Message with its replies; the field to reply and who gets a notification, or for a Request the reason, "Refuse" and "Accept" for the Data Team; at the bottom "All messages on this …", which opens the slide-in, and the way back to the Message Centre. Closing the window leaves the User on the page of the object, as it is, without the window; the Message Centre is the link at its foot ([DEC-UX-210](#dec-ux-210)). The page behind stays usable: the User reads the result of a Lens or the size check of a Lens to accept while answering. The link in a notification opens the same way.
- **On every object,** a Collection, a Resource or a Lens, "Messages" with their number in the header opens the slide-in: on top a new Message, with its kind and who gets a notification; chips by kind; the Messages on this object with their replies; at the bottom that everyone who may see the object sees them, and the way to the Message Centre ([FR-CV-07](../requirements/functional/14-conversations.md#fr-cv-07)). On a Resource, "Ask a question" opens the same slide-in with Question chosen. The slide-in opens with the cursor in the text of the new Message, for who may write one; from the quick window, Add a Remark and Add a Signal open it with that kind chosen ([DEC-UX-257](#dec-ux-257)).
- **A Collection** takes a Question or a Remark; a Resource and a Lens also a Signal. The Messages on the Resources and Lenses of a Collection stay on those.
- **A changed description in dbt** is told to the Owner by Data Hub, in one Message per Resource and run ([FR-RC-17](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#fr-rc-17)). Opened, it lists every description that changed, the table or a field, with its text before and after, and the Business Description next to it, or that there is none. It asks for nothing: no review, no answer. "Edit business descriptions" opens the Columns tab in editing, with the changed fields marked. Right under its text, "Understand what is happening here" holds the Learning Card K3.4 Where definitions live, which opens inline ([DEC-UX-79](#dec-ux-79)).
- **What Data Hub writes itself** carries a clear link to the Learning Card that explains it, right under its text, under the heading "Understand what is happening here": the card as it is attached to a Message, with its number, title and whether the User has seen it. A click opens it inline, in the Message: its core idea and what the User can do with it, with "Open the whole card" and "I understand it" ([DEC-UX-79](#dec-ux-79), [FR-LR-38](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#fr-lr-38)). A changed description in dbt points to K3.4 Where definitions live, a change since the last Confirmation to 4.3.2 Versions and agreements with users, a Registration to K5.1 What a resource is.
- **A changed field** since the last Confirmation is told to the Owner the same way, by Data Hub, with what changed; the warning on the Resource is where it is marked seen ([FR-MR-12](../requirements/functional/06-maturity-and-release.md#fr-mr-12)).
- **A Request** asks the Data Team for a decision on its object; the first is accepting a very large Lens, sent with "Send to the data team" when it is made ([DEC-CV-06](../requirements/functional/14-conversations.md#dec-cv-06), [FR-CV-08](../requirements/functional/14-conversations.md#fr-cv-08)). Opened, it shows what it asks to decide on, such as what one run reads, its range, the partitions and what a default would read. The Data Team has a field for its reason, "Refuse" and "Accept"; its author sees that it waits, and that only they see the Lens until then. The state shows in the list: waiting, accepted, refused. Refused, the Lens stays unpublished and is no longer frozen: its author changes it from its query and sends it again ([DEC-UX-128](#dec-ux-128)).

## Notifications

Screen: [notifications](screens/notifications.html): an e-mail to an Owner Unit, an e-mail to one person, what Data Hub writes by e-mail, and a direct message in Slack. Designed on 2026-10-29 ([DEC-UX-129](#dec-ux-129)).

- **When:** a Message notifies at once, never as a digest: in Data Hub always, by e-mail, and as a direct message in Slack ([DEC-LN-02](../requirements/functional/07-log-and-notifications.md#dec-ln-02), [DEC-LN-05](../requirements/functional/07-log-and-notifications.md#dec-ln-05)). Who it reaches follows the recipients of its type in the Message Catalogue; whoever wrote it is not notified of it.
- **To whom:** an Owner Unit by e-mail to the address of its Google group, and in Slack each of its members Data Hub knows ([DEC-LN-03](../requirements/functional/07-log-and-notifications.md#dec-ln-03), [DEC-LN-07](../requirements/functional/07-log-and-notifications.md#dec-ln-07)); the Data Team the same way; a person, such as the writer of a Request, by e-mail to their own address and in Slack. A person who also gets it through a group gets one e-mail and one direct message.
- **The e-mail:** from Data Hub, at an address of Immoscoop whose replies are not read ([DEC-PF-31](../requirements/technical/00-platform.md#dec-pf-31)). Its subject is the first line of the plain text of the Message; its body has the mark and "Data Hub" on top, the object with its kind and Collection, the laid-out text of the Message, "Open in context" as its one button, and at the foot why it came, the way to the Message Centre and that replies are not read. The buttons of the Message itself, such as "Accept" or "Edit business descriptions", stay in Data Hub, where "Open in context" leads. It is always light, as e-mail is read, in the language of its receiver: their setting in Data Hub, Dutch for a group.
- **In Slack:** a direct message from the app Data Hub, never in a channel: the laid-out text in the mark-up of Slack, with the plain text as its fallback, "Open in context" as a button, and the object and why it came under it. A User who is not in Slack gets e-mail alone.

## My page

Screen: [my-page](screens/my-page.html). Designed on 2026-10-28, at the request of Max ([DEC-UX-84](#dec-ux-84)).

- **The initials** at the foot of the rail open it; on My page they carry a ring, as the active icon does.
- **Head:** the initials large, the name and the Google account the User signs in with. Under it three tabs, Profile, Excel keys and Background ([DEC-UX-85](#dec-ux-85), [DEC-UX-108](#dec-ux-108)).
- **Profile, Language:** Nederlands or English, for the sentences of Data Hub: its screens, Messages and e-mails; names stay English, and the Learning Cards follow the language too ([NFR-LOC-06](../requirements/non-functional/localisation.md#nfr-loc-06), [NFR-LOC-12](../requirements/non-functional/localisation.md#nfr-loc-12)).
- **Profile, You own:** the Collections of the User's groups, each with its group, what it holds and whether it is public, and one line on being a Reader of all else ([DEC-DH-78](../overview/data-hub.md#dec-dh-78)). Groups come from Google and are not changed here. *Note of 2026-11-10: groups are kept in Data Hub; the tile Your groups lists them, with the way to Groups ([DEC-UX-200](#dec-ux-200)).* The list keeps to about six rows and scrolls within itself, so that a User in many groups does not stretch the page ([DEC-UX-135](#dec-ux-135)).
- **Profile, Your queries:** how many, and when the last ran, with a link to Query.
- **Profile, Slack** ([DEC-UX-209](#dec-ux-209), [FR-LN-07](../requirements/functional/07-log-and-notifications.md#fr-ln-07)): only where the bot token of the Slack app of Data Hub is set; uat gets the same token as prod ([OP-UX-78](#op-ux-78)). It names the Slack account Data Hub finds by the address of the Google account, marked Found, with Another address; or, when it finds none, says so, that notifications come by e-mail alone, and offers a field for the address in Slack with Look it up. Look it up names the Slack account that has the address, and that a code goes there before anything changes; Send the code shows the code field, Confirm and Send another code, and a line that the code holds for 10 minutes ([OP-LN-08](../requirements/functional/07-log-and-notifications.md#op-ln-08)). A confirmed address shows with its date, Confirmed, with Another address and Back to your Google address. No one with that address, or a wrong code, is said in the tile. In the prototype `?slack=found`, `absent`, `look`, `nomatch`, `code`, `wrong`, `other` and `off`.
- **The tab Excel keys** ([DEC-DH-79](../overview/data-hub.md#dec-dh-79)): a sentence on what a key does, "New Excel key", and a table with each key, when it was made, last used and expires; a key that expires within a month shows its date in the warning colour. A revoked key stays in the list, struck through, with when it was revoked ([FR-PL-02](../requirements/functional/12-permissions-and-library-cards.md#fr-pl-02)).
- **New Excel key** is a sheet: a name, then "Make the key", after which the sheet shows the key once in a box with Copy, that it cannot be shown again, and the three steps in Excel, signing in with Basic: the e-mail as user name and the key as password ([FR-PL-01](../requirements/functional/12-permissions-and-library-cards.md#fr-pl-01), [DEC-CH-06](../requirements/functional/13-channels.md#dec-ch-06)). "Done" closes it; the key is in the list.
- **Revoke** asks to confirm in a line that takes the place of the row, with Cancel and a red Revoke.
- **The tab Background** ([DEC-UX-108](#dec-ux-108)): one line saying this is the background by day (or by night), and that switching the theme chooses the other; then a tile per choice of the current theme, Random first, each with a small picture and its name; the chosen one has a ring. In the prototype `?metab=background`.
- The prototype opens on Profile; the tab Excel keys shows the list, making a key, the key shown once, revoking and revoked, with a switch at the bottom (`?metab=keys&keys=made`).

## Admin

Screens: [admin-webhooks](screens/admin-webhooks.html), [admin-keys](screens/admin-keys.html), [admin-users](screens/admin-users.html), [admin-permission-groups](screens/admin-permission-groups.html). Designed on 2026-10-30, at the request of Max ([DEC-UX-130](#dec-ux-130)); its name, place and opening decided the same day ([OP-UX-50](#op-ux-50), [OP-UX-51](#op-ux-51), [OP-UX-52](#op-ux-52)). Excel keys designed on 2026-10-31, the place where the Data Team revokes a key, as Max chose ([FR-PL-03](../requirements/functional/12-permissions-and-library-cards.md#fr-pl-03)). Users and Permission groups proposed on 2026-11-06, at the request of Max; what they take for granted decided the same day ([DEC-UX-186](#dec-ux-186) to [DEC-UX-192](#dec-ux-192)).
