Which Permissions there are is worked out in the requirements ([OP-PL-02](../requirements/functional/12-permissions-and-library-cards.md#op-pl-02)).

- **The section of the Data Team.** Admin sits in the rail after the learning centre, a little apart, and only a Data Admin sees it. Its icon is the toolbox: a gear would read as settings, which the rail does not have ([DEC-UX-53](#dec-ux-53), [OP-UX-50](#op-ux-50), [OP-UX-51](#op-ux-51)). Its panel lists its tools, Webhooks the first, each with the number of things in it to look at. While Webhooks is its only tool, Admin opens on it ([OP-UX-52](#op-ux-52)). Excel keys is its second tool; its start screen is still to be designed, and until then Admin opens on Webhooks ([OP-UX-53](#op-ux-53)).
- **Webhooks** shows what dbt Cloud tells this environment of Data Hub after a run, and what Data Hub does with it; its head names the environment, such as Data Hub uat ([FR-AR-19](../requirements/functional/01-artifact-registry.md#fr-ar-19)).
- **Source Jobs,** on top: a tile per dbt project with its Source Job by name and ID, its kind and environment in dbt Cloud, whether it generates the docs, the last run that delivered an Artifact Set, and who set it and when, or that it was taken over from the configuration ([FR-AR-18](../requirements/functional/01-artifact-registry.md#fr-ar-18)). Under the tiles, the webhook in dbt Cloud that names the same jobs, with its address to copy. The page is the only place where the dbt projects and their Source Jobs are set ([DEC-AR-10](../requirements/functional/01-artifact-registry.md#dec-ar-10)). With no dbt project followed, the place of the tiles says so, and that Data Hub takes in no run until one is.
- **"Change"** opens a sheet with the jobs of that dbt project as dbt Cloud names them, by environment, each with its name, ID, kind, whether it generates the docs and its last run. A job of another environment, a CI job that builds pull requests in schemas of their own, or a job that does not generate the docs, cannot be chosen and says why ([DEC-UX-166](#dec-ux-166)). Its foot says that the change counts from the next run, and that the webhook in dbt Cloud names the job too.
- **"Follow a dbt project"**, in the head of Source Jobs, opens a sheet with the dbt projects of the dbt Cloud account that Data Hub does not follow yet, by name and ID. Once one is chosen, its jobs show under it, by environment, as in "Change", with the same jobs that cannot be chosen, but of every environment; "Follow it" adds its tile, from its next run ([DEC-UX-166](#dec-ux-166)).
- **"Stop following"**, on a tile, asks to confirm in the place of the tile, as revoking an Excel key does: Data Hub ignores the runs of that dbt project from then on, its Resources stay as they are, at its last version, and its job comes out of the webhook in dbt Cloud too. Following it again brings them up to date ([DEC-UX-166](#dec-ux-166)).
- **Deliveries,** under them: those of the last 30 days, the newest first, filtered by what Data Hub did. Each says when it came, the job by name and ID, the dbt project and the run; one opened shows on the right, as a Message does in the Message Centre. The list keeps to the height of the screen and scrolls within itself, with its filter on top, so that a month of deliveries does not stretch the page; the delivery opened stays in view beside it ([DEC-UX-167](#dec-ux-167)).
  - **Recorded:** what Data Hub did, step by step, from recording the run to the Current Version and the Registrations it brought, with the way to the Resources that wait. The run that is the Current Version of its dbt project can be processed again, with one quiet line why: Data Hub reads it with what it does now, such as the column types BigQuery gives for a model the catalog lacks; an older run cannot, as it can no longer become current ([DEC-UX-277](#dec-ux-277), [FR-AR-14](../requirements/functional/01-artifact-registry.md#fr-ar-14)).
  - **Ignored:** why, in one sentence, such as a CI job or a job of another environment, and what the Data Admin can do; an ignored delivery costs nothing. A test from dbt Cloud shows as ignored, with that its signature is right.
  - **Refused,** and **Recorded, failed,** call for action and take the signal colour: what it means and what to do, such as putting the new secret in Secret Manager, or turning on "Generate docs on run". A failed set can be processed again ([FR-AR-14](../requirements/functional/01-artifact-registry.md#fr-ar-14)).
- With no delivery in 30 days, the list says so, and how to send a test from dbt Cloud.
- The prototype opens each kind with the switch at the bottom, or `?opened=ignored` and so on; `?pick=open` opens the sheet that changes a Source Job, `?pick=follow` the one that follows a dbt project, and `?stop=open` asks to stop following dbt-prod.
- **Excel keys** lists every Excel key of the environment with its User, the name they gave it, when it was made, last used and expires, the newest last used on top; its head names the environment, as Webhooks does. A search finds a User or a key by name, and a filter shows the active keys, those expiring within a month, or the revoked ones, each with its count; the panel counts the active keys. A key that expires within a month shows its date in the warning colour, as on My page.
- **Revoke** asks to confirm in the place of the row, as on My page, naming the key and its User, and that they get a Message that the Data Team did ([MSG-KEY-02](../requirements/functional/message-catalogue.md#msg-key-02)). A revoked key stays in its place, struck through, with "revoked today by you", until the filter changes; under Revoked every revoked key shows when and by whom. The prototype shows the list, revoking and revoked with the switch at the bottom (`?akeys=revoking`).
- **Users and Permission groups, a proposal of 2026-11-06.** Two more tools of Admin, after Excel keys. What a User may do no longer comes from a Role Group in Workspace: each User is in one Permission group of Data Hub, given here; a new User gets the default at their first sign-in ([DEC-UX-186](#dec-ux-186), [DEC-UX-187](#dec-ux-187)). Their Owner Units still come from Workspace and still say which Collections they own.
- **Users** lists everyone who signed in to the environment, with their Permission group, their Owner Units, what they use (the web app, Claude, Excel) and when they last signed in; its head names the environment, as Webhooks does. A search finds a User by name or address, and a filter shows one Permission group, each with its count. A User who can no longer sign in with Google stays listed, greyed, with their Permission group; a new one is marked new, with "the default" next to their group. The Data Team gets a Message of every new User, with a button that opens them here ([FR-PL-16](../requirements/functional/12-permissions-and-library-cards.md#fr-pl-16)).
- **A User opened** shows in a sheet over the list: who they are, since when, how they use Data Hub and their Owner Units, read-only and named as coming from Workspace. Under it, their Permission group as a choice of all groups, each with its sentence and number of Users, the current one marked "now" and the default marked "default". Choosing another shows, before saving, what changes for that User: what they gain, what they lose, and that their Owner Units stay. Under it, the history of their Permission group: who changed it and when. The foot says that a change takes effect within 15 minutes, in the web app, in Claude and in Excel ([DEC-UX-189](#dec-ux-189)). Saved, the row shows the new group, marked "changed by you", and a toast says so.
- **Yourself,** opened, cannot change your own Permission group: the choice is greyed, with why, and that another User of the Data Team can ([DEC-UX-188](#dec-ux-188)).
- **Signing in,** on the sheet of a User above their Permission group, sets them Active or Inactive with a segmented control, at once, without Save. Beside it a sentence says that only signing in changes: inactive, they no longer sign in, in the web app, in Claude or with an Excel key, and whoever is signed in is out within 15 minutes; their Collections, Resources, queries, Lenses, Messages, Groups and Permission group stay. Inactive, the sheet says since when and by whom, and their row in the list is greyed and marked inactive. Yourself, opened, cannot be set inactive: a callout says so, and that another User of the Data Team can. *Added on 2026-11-26, at the request of Max, built without a prototype as he asked ([FR-PL-17](../requirements/functional/12-permissions-and-library-cards.md#fr-pl-17)).*
- **Permission groups** lists the groups on the left, each with its sentence, its number of Users and, for one of them, "Default for new Users"; "New Permission group" on top. One opened shows on the right, as a delivery does on Webhooks: its name to rename in place, a click on it as everywhere else in Data Hub, its sentence, "Duplicate", which opens the sheet of a new group as its copy, its Users with the way to them on Users, whether it is the default, with "Make it the default" after No for any group but Admin, and who changed it last.
- **Its Permissions** follow by part of Data Hub, in the order of the rail: Collections and Resources, Query, Lenses, Messages, Claude, Excel, Admin. Each Permission has its name, a sentence of what it allows, and yes or no on the right. While it is yes, its values show under it, in a tinted block, each with its label and a field of the kind it has, the kinds of a Parameter ([DEC-UX-71](#dec-ux-71)): a whole number with its unit, such as 100 MB; a choice, as radio buttons; several choices, as chips; yes or no; a text. A Permission changed is marked "changed" and tinted, and an edit bar on top, which stays in view, counts the changes, says for how many Users they take effect, and saves or discards them all at once.
- **Admin** is the Permission group that can do everything and keeps it: its Permissions show as "Yes" with a lock and cannot be changed; it cannot be deleted and keeps at least one User ([DEC-UX-188](#dec-ux-188)). It can be duplicated.
- **"Delete …",** at the foot of a group, asks to confirm in place, as stopping to follow a dbt project does: its Users go to the group chosen, which becomes the default when the deleted group was; Admin cannot be chosen, as Users join it one by one. Each User moved gets a Message ([DEC-UX-192](#dec-ux-192)).
- **"New Permission group"** opens a sheet with its name, its sentence, and the group it starts as a copy of, or nothing, with every Permission no; it starts without Users.
- **Withdrawn** ([DEC-UX-213](#dec-ux-213), [FR-SR-55](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#fr-sr-55)), a tool of Admin after Permission groups: every Resource that dbt withdrew, the newest first, with its Display Name and Key, its dbt project, its Collection or "Without a Collection", its Level, and when and in which run it was withdrawn. Its head names the environment and says that Data Hub keeps them and brings one back as it was once an exposure depends on it again. A search finds a name or a Key; a filter shows all, those in a Collection, or those without one, each with its count, and the panel counts them. A whole row opens the Resource. With none, it says that nothing is withdrawn. Screen: [admin-withdrawn](screens/admin-withdrawn.html), `?wd=list`, `?wd=empty`.
- The Permissions in the prototype are examples, to show each kind of value. The prototypes open each state with the switch at the bottom: `admin-users.html?us=open`, `?us=changed`, `?us=saved`, `?us=self`; `admin-permission-groups.html?pg=changed`, `?pg=delete`, `?pg=datateam`, `?pg=new`, and `?kinds=on` names the kind of each value.

## Groups

Screens: [groups](screens/groups.html), with the tile Your groups on [my-page](screens/my-page.html), the part Groups of [admin-permission-groups](screens/admin-permission-groups.html), the groups of a User on [admin-users](screens/admin-users.html) and the Owner Unit of [collection-edit](screens/collection-edit.html). Proposed on 2026-11-10, at the request of Max, to review: groups are kept in Data Hub, no longer in Google Workspace ([DEC-UX-200](#dec-ux-200), [DEC-UX-201](#dec-ux-201), [DEC-UX-202](#dec-ux-202), [DEC-UM-10](../requirements/functional/02-user-management.md#dec-um-10)).

- **Kept in Data Hub.** A group is a set of Users, kept in Data Hub, so that it does not depend on the directory, which moves from Google to Entra. A group can be the Owner Unit of Collections; another group, or the group itself, manages it. Sign-in still goes through Google until then ([DEC-UM-11](../requirements/functional/02-user-management.md#dec-um-11)).
- **Reached from My page.** The tile Your groups on Profile lists the groups the User is in, each with its number of members and who manages it, with Groups and New group under it. Groups has no place in the rail; its breadcrumb is My page, and the groups of a User on Users and the Owner Unit of a Collection link to it.
- **The list** shows the groups the User is in or manages, each with what it owns or manages, its number of members, and a tag for how the User stands: "You are in it", "It manages itself", "You manage it, through Sales management". A search finds a group, or a colleague in one. With Manage every group, a choice Yours or All shows every group, the others under "Other groups".
- **A group opened** shows on the right, as a Permission group does: its name, to rename in place, and its sentence; then its facts: its members, the group that manages it and whether the User is in that group, the Collections it is the Owner Unit of, the groups it manages, and who made it and when. Under them its members, the group that manages it as a choice, its history, and at its foot what deleting it takes.
- **Members.** Each with their name, address and since when. Whoever manages the group adds a colleague by name or address from a field above the list, which suggests while typing; the colleague is in it at once, marked "added by you", and the history says so. A cross takes someone out. A colleague who left Immoscoop stays listed, greyed, with "cannot sign in", until someone takes them out ([OP-UX-70](#op-ux-70)).
- **Managed by.** The group whose members change this one: add and remove its members, rename it, choose who manages it, and delete it. The choice lists the groups the User is in, and the group itself; with Manage every group, any group. Choosing a group the User is not in tells them they will no longer manage it ([OP-UX-71](#op-ux-71)).
- **Only in it.** A member who does not manage the group sees it read-only, with a line that names the group that manages it and two of its members to ask.
- **Deleting** is at the foot. A group that is the Owner Unit of a Collection, or manages another group, cannot be deleted until those move: the foot says which, and Delete is greyed. Otherwise it asks to confirm in place, as stopping to follow a dbt project does.
- **New group** is a sheet: a name, a sentence on who is in it, and the group that manages it, chosen from the groups the User is in, or the new group itself. The User is its first member and adds colleagues once it is made. Only a User whose Permission group allows Make groups sees New group.
- **Two Permissions,** in a part Groups of a Permission group, after Collections and Resources: Make groups (`groups.manage`), and Manage every group (`groups.all.manage`), the work of a Data Admin, who then changes every group, also one they are not in, with a line on the group that says so ([DEC-UX-201](#dec-ux-201)).
- **The Owner Unit of a Collection** is chosen, when it is made or edited, from the groups the User is in, as before ([DEC-CO-09](../requirements/functional/03-collections.md#dec-co-09)); they are now groups of Data Hub, named without an address.
- The prototype shows each state with the switch at the bottom: `groups.html?gr=manager` (Broker team, managed by a group the User is in), `?gr=adding`, `?gr=member` (Marketing, only in it), `?gr=new` and `?gr=all` (every group, Finance opened by a Data Admin).

## The environment

Screen: [environment](screens/environment.html). Designed on 2026-11-10, at the request of Max, and approved the same day ([DEC-UX-203](#dec-ux-203)).

- **uat wears a ribbon, prod none.** uat looks like prod, with real colleagues and real data, so it is easy to forget where you are. Every environment but prod wears a ribbon with its name across the upper right corner of the window: uat, and local on a laptop.
- **On every screen,** the sign-in and the page for what does not exist included, above every frame, sheet and floating window. It never takes a click: what lies under it works as ever, and it stays clear of the actions in the head of a page.
- **Teal, with its name in capitals** (`--env-ribbon`, `--text-on-env`): light teal with dark letters by night, dark teal with white letters by day, both legible at the size of a meta text. Not printed.
- **The title of the tab begins with the name,** "uat · Data Hub — Collections", so that a tab among many says it, and so does a screen reader; the ribbon itself is a note with "This is Data Hub uat, to test in: not prod".
- Admin and Groups keep naming the environment in their head, "Data Hub uat", as before ([DEC-UX-130](#dec-ux-130)). Claude does not get the ribbon: there the name of the connector says it.

## A link to something you may not see

Screen: [not-found](screens/not-found.html). Designed on 2026-10-24, to review ([DEC-UX-32](#dec-ux-32)).

- **One page** for a link to something that does not exist and to something the User may not see: a private Collection of another Owner Unit, a Resource that is not published, a Lens on a Resource they may not use, a query of someone else ([FR-CO-32](../requirements/functional/03-collections.md#fr-co-32)). It does not say which of the two it is: a private Collection does not exist for whoever is not in its Owner Unit ([FR-CO-17](../requirements/functional/03-collections.md#fr-co-17)).
- **It says what to do:** ask whoever sent the link, as its owner decides who sees it; or search what the User may see. Then the way to Collections and Lenses.
- **No "Ask for access":** it would tell that the object exists. Whether a User can ask for access, and how, is open ([OP-CO-13](../requirements/functional/03-collections.md#op-co-13)).

## Sign in

Screen: [sign-in](screens/sign-in.html). Designed on 2026-10-24, to review ([DEC-UX-35](#dec-ux-35)).

- **A background of the set of the theme,** picked at random each time the sign-in opens, fills the screen, as in the app ([DEC-UX-173](#dec-ux-173)). It moves as slowly as there.
- **The sign-in sits on a glass card** that blurs what lies behind it: the mark and "Data Hub", "Sign in", one sentence on what Data Hub is, and "Sign in with Google", the only way in ([FR-UM-01](../requirements/functional/02-user-management.md#fr-um-01)). Under the button: it takes the Immoscoop account, and Data Hub keeps no password of its own ([FR-UM-03](../requirements/functional/02-user-management.md#fr-um-03)).
- **An account of another domain is refused** ([FR-UM-02](../requirements/functional/02-user-management.md#fr-um-02)): the card says which account was refused and to sign in with the account of work, and the button reads "Sign in with another account".
- It follows the theme of the system, as Data Hub always does ([DEC-UX-172](#dec-ux-172)), and the set of that theme; the choice of the User is not known yet, so it is always Random.
- Where a browser cannot blur, the card is nearly opaque.

## Sheets

- A **sheet** slides in from the right over the whole window, for a task that belongs to the page but is not the page, such as adding Resources to a Collection. The rail, the panel and the page stay visible behind it, deeply dimmed, and nothing behind it can be clicked or scrolled while it is open; a click beside it, "Close" or Escape closes it ([DEC-UX-174](#dec-ux-174)).
- It takes half the width of the window, so that a task has room; a sheet with a table, such as the Grain and relations, is wider where half is too narrow ([DEC-UX-174](#dec-ux-174)).
- A sheet that opens to find something, such as "Add a resource" on a query and "Find a table", has the focus in its search at once, so that the User types straight away ([DEC-UX-245](#dec-ux-245)); any other sheet takes the focus itself, and gives it back where it came from when it closes.

## Floating window

- A **floating window** sits over a page for something done while the page stays in view, such as answering a Message in context ([DEC-UX-30](#dec-ux-30)). Unlike a sheet, it does not dim the page, which stays usable behind it.
- It opens in the lower right. Its head moves it; "Fold" shrinks it to its head, and closing it leaves the page as it is, without the window ([DEC-UX-210](#dec-ux-210)).

## Flow Box

Screen: [flow-box](screens/flow-box.html). Designed on 2026-11-20, a proposal, from Max's description of a "flow box" and his answers to how Claude read it ([DEC-UX-299](#dec-ux-299) to [DEC-UX-304](#dec-ux-304)).

- A **[Flow Box](../definitions.md#flow-box)** takes a User through the steps of one flow, in a frame of its own docked on the right of the main area: the page narrows to make room, and nothing is covered or dimmed ([DEC-UX-299](#dec-ux-299)). It is one general component for every flow. Its first flow is describing and publishing a Resource: the steps required to publish, the same list as its Maturity shows ([DEC-UX-304](#dec-ux-304), [FR-MR-20](../requirements/functional/06-maturity-and-release.md#fr-mr-20)). Its second is setting up the EDA of a Resource ([DEC-UX-327](#dec-ux-327), [FR-ED-14](../requirements/functional/15-eda.md#fr-ed-14)). Focus follows the mouse into it, as into the panel ([DEC-UX-146](#dec-ux-146)).
- **Its head:** what the flow does, such as "Describe and publish", the object it is about as a link to its page, Minimize and Close. Under it the progress: one segment per step, and how many are done.
- **Its steps:** each done or open, with its number and name, and folded what it holds, such as "1 Jan 2025, on `week_start`", or what it misses, with the warning colour. Key Fields and Fields are two steps, as on Maturity: choosing them on Querying, and describing each on Columns. The step to do now is outlined and unfolds: **Why** it matters first, then **What** it asks and **How** it is done ([DEC-UX-334](#dec-ux-334)), its Learning Card, and one button to the place where it is done. A click on another step unfolds that one instead. A step whose place is open on the page says so.
- **Purely a guide** ([DEC-UX-303](#dec-ux-303)): the Flow Box writes nothing itself. Its button goes to the place of the step, a part of the Overview or Columns, and opens it for editing, with its field in focus and marked for a moment; "Nothing to add" for the pitfalls is said there too. The Known Gaps are advised, so not a step of it ([DEC-UX-333](#dec-ux-333)). A step ticks as soon as the page saves what it asks, and the next open step unfolds.
- **It stays open on every page of Data Hub** ([DEC-UX-300](#dec-ux-300)): in Query, on a Lens, on another Resource or Collection, in Messages, the Learning Centre, My page and Admin, until it is closed, so that a User who looks at the data in Query keeps the steps in view. A sheet dims it with the rest of the window, as it dims everything behind it. Its steps and the name of its object lead back to the page of its object.
- **Minimize** folds it to a strip on the right edge, with its progress as a count and a dot per step; the strip, or Maximize on it, opens it again ([DEC-UX-301](#dec-ux-301)).
- **Close** takes it away at any moment; Publish brings it back ([DEC-UX-302](#dec-ux-302)). While a step required to publish is open, Publish opens a pop-out under the button: "Not ready to publish yet", each open step with what it misses and a link to its place, "Guide me", which opens the Flow Box at the first open step, and "I'll do it myself"; under them, that Claude can take the Owner through the steps too. Once every step is done, Publish opens the Publish sheet, and the Flow Box ends on its last step, Read back and publish ([DEC-MR-20](../requirements/functional/06-maturity-and-release.md#dec-mr-20), [FR-MR-21](../requirements/functional/06-maturity-and-release.md#fr-mr-21)). After publishing, it says so once, "Published and confirmed", and is closed. On the page of such a Resource, the quick window offers "Guide me through publishing" ([DEC-UX-240](#dec-ux-240)).
- **Who sees it:** whoever may describe the Resource, its Owner Unit and the Data Team, while it is not published. There is one Flow Box at a time: "Guide me" on another Resource takes the place of the one open ([DEC-UX-308](#dec-ux-308)).
- **It keeps nothing of its own** ([DEC-UX-306](#dec-ux-306)): it reads the state of its Resource each time it shows, and opens from the beginning, the first step still open unfolded, a tick beside every step done. Whether it is open, and minimized, lives in the open app only, and goes with a reload.
- **A narrow window:** below 1360 px wide, opening it folds the panel, which the User opens again as always ([DEC-UX-307](#dec-ux-307)). Its look and the texts of its steps are as drawn ([DEC-UX-305](#dec-ux-305)).

## Quick window

*Designed on 2026-11-13, at the request of Max, after the window of Raycast he shared; drawn in `screens/app.js` for every screen, with the commands of a query in [query](screens/query.html), where `?quick=open` opens it.*

- **Cmd+Shift+P or Cmd+K** (Ctrl+Shift+P or Ctrl+K on Windows and Linux) opens the quick window anywhere in Data Hub, also while typing in the SQL editor or a field; Cmd+K is there because Firefox keeps Cmd+Shift+P for itself. The same keys, Escape or a click beside it close it ([DEC-UX-237](#dec-ux-237), [OP-UX-87](#op-ux-87)).
- **It floats** over the upper part of the window, 720 pixels wide at most, as glass, the page lightly dimmed behind it. Unlike a floating window, the page cannot be used while it is open; it is a moment, not a place.
- **On top, the search,** with on its right where it opened, such as "Query · Leads per source name", as the commands it shows first belong there.
- **Empty, it lists** first what the User did from it in this session, under Recently used, most recent first, five at most, those that can be done here and now ([DEC-UX-242](#dec-ux-242)); then the commands of the screen it opened on, then the queries of the row on top with their keys, then Go to: every section of the rail, My page and Groups, and for the Data Team the tools of Admin. What shows under Recently used is not listed again below it. Typed, a row used more lately comes before one that matches as well.
- **Typed, it searches** those and everything the User may see, as the search on the start screen of Collections does: Resources, Collections, Lenses, their Projects, their own queries and Learning Cards ([OP-UX-88](#op-ux-88), [DEC-UX-288](#dec-ux-288)). Grouped by kind, four at most a kind, the commands of the screen first; a row has its icon, its name with what was typed in bold, what tells it apart, such as its Collection and Level or when a query last ran, its key if it has one, and on the right its kind. When nothing is found, it says so, and that Data Hub finds only what the User may see.
- **Going somewhere from it puts the cursor in the search of the screen that opens,** once it is there, such as that of Groups or the question on the start screen of Collections; going to a section whose search lives in its panel, such as Messages or Query, the search of the panel. Not when the screen put the cursor somewhere itself ([DEC-UX-256](#dec-ux-256)).
- **Up and Down** move through it, **Enter** does the row: a command does what its button does, a place opens. Its foot says what Enter does: "Do it", "Open" or "Go there". **⌘1 to ⌘9** (Ctrl on Windows and Linux) highlight the first nine rows that can be done, each marked with its key at the right end, also after typing; they never do the row, Enter does ([DEC-UX-242](#dec-ux-242)). While the window is open, these keys are the window's and open no flagged query: a flagged query shows its own key in its line instead.
- **What the User may never do is not listed,** such as Add a table for who may not read tables outside Data Hub. What they cannot do right now is greyed, with why under it, such as Run while the query runs ([DEC-UX-207](#dec-ux-207)).
- **Each screen brings its own commands, Query first.** On a query, every button of its page that does something to it has its command, in this order ([DEC-UX-239](#dec-ux-239)). First, when the cursor in the SQL is on a word a double-click explains, a field, a Parameter or the name of a Resource: Show info, which opens what a double-click on it opens, with that word under its name ([DEC-UX-238](#dec-ux-238), [DEC-UX-221](#dec-ux-221)). Then: **Run**, with what it reads, which does what ⌘↵ does ([DEC-UX-142](#dec-ux-142)): up to 100 MB it runs; above it, as "Approve and run", it puts the focus on the red button, where Enter approves it, so that an approval stays a press on that button; above 1 GB, as "Send to the Data Team", it opens the Request on its button, to add a word and send it; greyed, with why, while it runs, when the Pre-flight stops it, when it is Broken or when a Request waits. **Add the partition filter,** only when the Pre-flight says it misses one, as "Add the filter" does. **Make it a Lens,** or **Update the Lens** on a query used as one, greyed with why as its button is ([DEC-UX-232](#dec-ux-232)). Add to project, or Change project and Remove from project, as its Project button, and the other commands of [Projects](#projects) ([DEC-UX-288](#dec-ux-288), [DEC-UX-318](#dec-ux-318)). **Rename this query,** which opens its name and question in place ([DEC-UX-119](#dec-ux-119)). Fold all; Format the SQL, both without a button on the page ([DEC-UX-248](#dec-ux-248)); **Add a Parameter;** Add a Resource; **Remove a Resource,** one for each of its Resources when it has more than one, which starts a new query without it, as the × on its chip does ([DEC-UX-73](#dec-ux-73)); Add a table, for a power user, as "Find a table" does; New query from this one, the sheet of another set of Resources with the same set, its SQL, Parameters and question along, and the name suggested from its Resources and the date ([DEC-UX-73](#dec-ux-73)); **Lenses on these Resources,** with how many there are ([DEC-UX-73](#dec-ux-73)); **Show the history** or **Show the result;** **Copy the Query ID** of the result shown ([DEC-UX-72](#dec-ux-72)); **Copy the result as JSON** and **Copy the result as CSV**, its first 100 rows, as its two buttons do, while a result shows ([DEC-UX-292](#dec-ux-292)); Hide this query or Show this query again, which has no button on the page ([DEC-UX-246](#dec-ux-246)); and the view: Put the query next to the result, or above it ([DEC-UX-143](#dec-ux-143)). Of each pair only the one that applies shows; what is for its maker only, as on its page, shows only to its maker. Each is found by its words and by others, such as "copy" for New query from this one, "landscape" for the view or "execute" for Run. Folding a box, turning a page of the result and Preview or Apply on one run of the history stay on the page: too small a step for a command.
- **Every other screen brings its commands too** ([DEC-UX-240](#dec-ux-240)): a command for every button of the screen that does something to what it shows, and for its tabs and parts, shown as on the screen, only to whoever sees the button there, greyed with why where the button is. They come first, under the name of what the screen shows, such as This Resource, with where it opened on the right, such as "Resource · Weekly lead volume". Leaving the screen takes them away. What runs, approves, publishes a large Lens or decides a Request puts the focus on its button, where Enter does it, as Run on a query does ([DEC-UX-239](#dec-ux-239)). Actions on one row of a list, such as removing one Resource of a Collection or revoking one Excel key, stay on the row.
  - **Collections:** New Collection; Search by a question, which puts the cursor in the search; Show or Hide the intro.
  - **A Collection:** Edit this Collection; Add Resources; New folder; Add a Lens; Edit the intro; its Messages; its Owner Unit.
  - **A folder:** New folder in it; Delete this folder, once it is empty; its Collection.
  - **A Resource:** Query this Resource; Publish this Resource, for its Owner while it is not published; Ask a question; **Add a Remark** and **Add a Signal**, for who may write them there, which open its Messages on that kind with the cursor in the text ([DEC-UX-257](#dec-ux-257)); Add to a Collection, while it waits for one; Use the Resource to use instead, when it is deprecated; Mark the changes as seen; Edit relations, on its Columns; Show each other tab; its Collection. On its Overview also: Edit the part on screen, for who may, and Show each other part, the Danger zone among them; and, from whichever part is on screen, straight to what its Owner Unit fills in, each for who may edit it, opening its part to edit with the cursor in the field ([DEC-UX-258](#dec-ux-258)): **Add the Business Description**, or Update it once there is one, greyed with why below 2 · Described; **Update the intended use**; **Update the pitfalls**; **Add a key field**; **Add a Parameter**; and **Add a Known Gap**, which opens Known Gaps on a new gap.
  - **A Lens:** Run, as its button, above 100 MB putting the focus on it; Open in Excel; its Messages; Open in Query and Edit more, for its Owner Unit; its Collection.
  - **Make it a Lens, or edit one:** each other step; Publish Lens, Publish anyway or Send to the Data Team, as its size check offers them, the last two putting the focus on their button or the word to the Data Team; Save, Delete this Lens and Cancel while editing one.
  - **A new query:** Add a Resource; Start from SQL, for a power user; Start your own.
  - **A Project:** Share it; New query in it, an empty one; Rename it; Change the order of its queries; Leave it; Delete it, which puts the focus on its button ([Projects](#projects), [DEC-UX-288](#dec-ux-288)).
  - **The Learning Centre:** Go on where you left off; Start with the Compass; the path of each Level; Show or Hide the intro.
  - **A Learning Card:** I understand it, or Not understood after all; the next step and each other; Quick or Full; its Messages. **See it:** Back to where you were; Open the whole card; I understand it.
  - **Messages:** each other view of the panel, For you, Unread, Written by you and Everything you may see, with how many it holds; the next and the previous Message; where the opened one was said; each other filter; and **Mark all as read**, every unread Message of the list shown, a command without a button ([DEC-UX-255](#dec-ux-255)). **An opened Message:** Reply, which puts the cursor in the reply; Close this Message, which opens the field for why with the cursor in it, as its button does, or Reopen this Message ([DEC-UX-255](#dec-ux-255)); on a Request that waits, Accept, which puts the focus on its button, and Refuse, which puts the cursor in the reason it needs.
  - **My page:** each other tab; Groups; New group, for who may make one. **Groups:** New group; on an opened Group, the Group that manages it and Delete it, for who may change it.
  - **Admin:** Permission groups: New Permission group, and for the one opened Duplicate it, Make it the default and Delete it. Webhooks: Follow a dbt project. Users, Excel keys and Removed Resources act on one row at a time: no command of their own.
- **The commands of Data Hub** follow those of the screen, on every screen ([OP-UX-88](#op-ux-88)): New query, for who may make one, which opens the screen of a new query, to choose its Resources and see the Lenses on them first ([FR-SR-47](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#fr-sr-47)); **New blank query,** only for a power user, who may write SQL on the tables Data Hub knows without Resources ([DEC-SR-46](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#dec-sr-46)): an empty query in no Project, named "New query" and the date, opened at once, as New query on the page of a Project makes one ([DEC-UX-290](#dec-ux-290)); **New query in** each Project the User is in, "New query in Credit leads Q4", the one they work in first: an empty query at the end of its row, opened at once, and the User then works in that Project ([DEC-UX-293](#dec-ux-293)); Switch light or dark; **Hide the panel** or **Show the panel**, as the « in the head of the panel and a click on the main item of the rail the page is in do, on every screen that has a panel; and on the screens of Query, **Refresh the queries**, as the ↻ in the head of its panel does, which reads them again, such as those Claude made meanwhile ([DEC-UX-254](#dec-ux-254), [DEC-UX-163](#dec-ux-163)), and **New project**, as the + over the Projects in its panel does ([DEC-UX-288](#dec-ux-288)).

## Background and motion

- Motion in the background is **very slow** (`--background-cycle`), felt rather than seen.
- Only in the background: reading surfaces never move.
- **"Reduce motion"** in the system turns all animation off. A switch of the User can do so too; where it lives is open ([OP-UX-13](#op-ux-13)).
- **Which background:** each theme has a set, by day the clouds, the flowing gradient, five flowing lights and six painted scenes, by night the dark gradient, the flowing gradient, four flowing lights and four painted scenes ([DEC-UX-109](#dec-ux-109), [DEC-UX-173](#dec-ux-173), [DEC-UX-198](#dec-ux-198), [DEC-UX-199](#dec-ux-199), [DEC-UX-208](#dec-ux-208)). The User chooses two on My page, one for day and one for night, each from the set of its theme, or Random, the default: one picked at random when Data Hub starts, and again when the User switches between day and night ([DEC-UX-108](#dec-ux-108)). No User adds a picture of their own. The sign-in picks one of the set at random ([DEC-UX-173](#dec-ux-173)).
- Light for the computer: CSS, no video. The flowing gradient is the one exception, drawn with WebGL, and kept light: at half resolution, 30 frames a second, paused while its tab is hidden ([DEC-UX-80](#dec-ux-80)).
- **In the light theme,** soft clouds of colour on white: warm yellow and peach in the upper left, a soft rose, lilac in the middle and sky blue in the lower right, from a picture of Max turned a quarter and mirrored ([DEC-UX-78](#dec-ux-78)). They drift as slowly. The dark theme keeps its three-colour gradient.
- **A picture** can take the place of the gradient ([DEC-UX-34](#dec-ux-34)), drifting as slowly; a photo is shown as it is, covering the window ([DEC-UX-110](#dec-ux-110)). The frames then blur it lightly, so that it never competes with the text. Neither set holds a picture now: Concrete, the last, left ([DEC-UX-208](#dec-ux-208)). A picture that comes back lives in `backgrounds/`.
- **Each theme has pictures of its own** ([DEC-UX-36](#dec-ux-36)): a photo is never darkened for the dark theme. The dark set has no picture since Lights on left it ([DEC-UX-173](#dec-ux-173)); by night a picture of the day is never shown.
- **A flowing gradient** can take the place of the background of either theme, or of a picture ([DEC-UX-80](#dec-ux-80)): drawn live with WebGL on a canvas fixed behind the app, which the frames blur as they blur a picture. Any screen shows it with `?bg=flow` in its address.
  - **By day,** sky blue, a warm orange band rising to the right, and cream. **By night,** in the dark theme, a dark sky with a band of teal, violet and magenta along the same rising line, and a warm coral glow in the bottom left: the dark theme has a flow of its own, as it has pictures of its own ([DEC-UX-36](#dec-ux-36)). A still Grain lies over both. Their colours live in their shaders, as the colours of a picture live in the picture.
  - **It moves slowly,** in a loop of 15 seconds that meets its start without a seam (60 until 2026-10-28, when Max asked for it faster). *Since 2026-10-28, at the request of Max, there is no loop, by day and by night: its four movements each turn at a speed of their own, between three quarters and one and a third of the usual one, from a random start, so that the field never repeats and differs each time Data Hub starts ([DEC-UX-109](#dec-ux-109)).* **It follows the mouse:** the field leans towards it and the band bends towards it, eased over seconds, so that a passing mouse barely stirs it; it settles back when the mouse leaves. Touch does not move it.
  - With "Reduce motion" it is one still frame, and the mouse does nothing. Without WebGL the usual background stays.
- **The flowing lights** ([DEC-UX-109](#dec-ux-109)): soft spots and arcs of colour, drawn live with WebGL as the flowing gradient is, after pictures Max chose. By night Borealis, Embers, Dusk and Horizon, light adding up on black; by day Periwinkle, Sunrise, Apricot, Blush and Sky, colour painted over a pale ground. Each light drifts, breathes and its arc swells at a random speed of its own, in periods of 8 to 18 seconds, and leans a little towards the mouse; nothing repeats. With "Reduce motion" they stand still. Any screen shows one with `?bg=borealis` and so on in its address.
- **Painted shapes** ([DEC-UX-198](#dec-ux-198), [DEC-UX-199](#dec-ux-199)): many kinds of shapes and many colours painted over each other, as watercolour is, drawn live with WebGL as the flowing lights are. **Prism**, by day, after a picture Max shared: a ground of lilac turning to sky blue along the top and blush to peach along the bottom; a tilted eye of violet and periwinkle; a field of pink from the left whose edge is an S that cuts into the eye and sweeps round under it; a ribbon of rose that passes behind the eye; sky blue standing on the right; butter, peach and coral low down; blush in the bottom left corner; a thin arc of mint and one of mauve. A slow warp keeps every edge from being straight. Each shape drifts, turns, sways or breathes at a speed of its own, in periods of 20 to 60 seconds, and the whole field leans a little towards the mouse; nothing repeats, and it starts at a random moment. With "Reduce motion" it stands still.
  - **Two sides,** by day, after pictures Max shared: broad, soft sides of colour, a light band between them and soft spheres, with a Grain as the pictures have. Cinnamon: terracotta and, on top, dark brown, over cream and tan, a golden glow between; the dark side on top, as Max likes it. Evening: a dusk sky, deep indigo on top, through blue, lilac, blush and sand to orange; not night blue, so that the rail and the grey text above stay readable. Citrus: blue on the left, a band of cream, gold and coral red on the right. Orchid: almost white with blush, orchid on the right, ice blue in the bottom left. Tide: deep blue in the top left, a band of cream on the diagonal, an orange sphere in the bottom right.
  - **By night,** after pictures Max shared: Afterglow, black above a great hill of red and orange whose edge is a red line, teal peeping in between on the right and pale peach in the corner; Lumen, a very light yellow streak in the middle in orange, waving out to magenta and purple on the left, blue below, dark orange in the top right; Cobalt, the night above tints of blue, royal, azure, cyan and aqua, that mix into one form, violet and magenta low down; Midnight, subtle colours of one family, night blue, indigo, violet, a purple leaning to magenta and royal blue.
  - Each is its own shader; a scene of two sides is only its colours and shapes, from which its shader is written. Any screen shows one with `?bg=prism`, `?bg=cinnamon` and so on in its address.

## Claude

Screens: [a Resource](screens/mcp-resource.html), [its Preview](screens/mcp-resource-preview.html), [its publication](screens/mcp-resource-publish.html) and [a query with its result](screens/mcp-query.html), as MCP Apps in a conversation in Claude; [a Learning Card](screens/learning-mcp.html); and [the Owner Journey](screens/owner-journey.html), as a storyboard (a proposal, 2026-11-15). Designed on 2026-10-30, the query on 2026-11-01, to review ([OP-CL-01](../requirements/functional/17-claude.md#op-cl-01)). Claude comes in version 2 ([DEC-CH-02](../requirements/functional/13-channels.md#dec-ch-02)).

- **Text or a window** ([DEC-CL-02](../requirements/functional/17-claude.md#dec-cl-02), [FR-CL-01](../requirements/functional/17-claude.md#fr-cl-01)): most answers are Claude's own words, from what a tool gave it. An MCP App opens only where the User should see the object or must decide: a Resource asked to be shown, a Preview, a publication.
- **The same components, without the app around them** ([DEC-DH-15](../overview/data-hub.md#dec-dh-15)): an MCP App puts together the components of the page, with no rail, panel, breadcrumb or edit links. On its head the icon, the kind and Collection, the Collection with its own icon before its name, as in the panel, when the window is of one Collection ([DEC-UX-164](#dec-ux-164)), and the name; at its foot what it is (as you see it, kept in your Query Log) and "Open in Data Hub", which opens the page at the matching tab ([FR-CL-06](../requirements/functional/17-claude.md#fr-cl-06)).
- **The Level, always:** on the head of the frame or in its status row, and in Claude's own answer, with a deprecation and what to use instead ([FR-CL-05](../requirements/functional/17-claude.md#fr-cl-05)).
- **The User decides in the window, Claude never** ([FR-CL-09](../requirements/functional/17-claude.md#fr-cl-09), [FR-CL-12](../requirements/functional/17-claude.md#fr-cl-12)): "Approve and run" above 100 MB, the Request above 1 GB, and the declaration with "Publish and confirm" are buttons of the User in the MCP App, each saying that Claude cannot do it; Claude has no way to use them ([DEC-CL-07](../requirements/functional/17-claude.md#dec-cl-07)).
- **A Lens first** ([DEC-CL-10](../requirements/functional/17-claude.md#dec-cl-10)): before Claude writes a query, it offers the Lens that answers the question, in its own words with a link, and opens no window for it.
- **The result of a query** ([DEC-CL-13](../requirements/functional/17-claude.md#dec-cl-13)): the grid of Query in the window, with the Resources it reads and their Level, its Query ID and the SQL folded, but no editor: "Open in Query" is where the User goes on. The query is the User's, labelled Claude in their panel ([DEC-CL-11](../requirements/functional/17-claude.md#dec-cl-11)).
- **A Resource opens on its Summary** ([DEC-CL-16](../requirements/functional/17-claude.md#dec-cl-16), [FR-CL-26](../requirements/functional/17-claude.md#fr-cl-26)): the same Summary as the Overview in Data Hub, every part in the order to read it, numbered, with its weight ([DEC-UX-169](#dec-ux-169)), and Columns beside it. Its parts lead nowhere inside the frame, but "Its fields" opens Columns. Claude reads the same Summary first.
- **On a phone** ([DEC-UX-180](#dec-ux-180), [FR-CL-27](../requirements/functional/17-claude.md#fr-cl-27)): Claude on a phone shows an MCP App at the width of the screen, 360 px or less. Every frame lays out its own width, whatever the device: one column; the number and weight of a part above its text; the fields of Columns as a list of cards, each with its labels written out; Parameters one under another, each control the width of the frame; a table of data scrolling sideways in its own box, never the conversation; tabs, buttons and "Open in Data Hub" at least 44 px high, buttons the width of the frame; long names such as a Key wrap; and nothing told only on hover.
- **It folds** ([DEC-UX-181](#dec-ux-181)): a button at the top right of every MCP App folds it to its head, the icon, its kind and its name, and unfolds it again, so that a long answer takes little room in the conversation.
- **A conversation, drawn plainly:** the prototypes show the question of the User in a bubble, the tool that opened the window above it in grey, and Claude's answer under it, so that the frame is seen in its place. Only the frame comes from Data Hub.
- **A flow through Claude, as a storyboard** ([OP-CL-12](../requirements/functional/17-claude.md#op-cl-12)): a flow of many steps, such as [the Owner Journey](screens/owner-journey.html), is drawn as a storyboard: the steps on the left, as a design aid, and the conversation of the step chosen on the right, in a window of its own. Everything in it is tagged with what draws it: **CUI** for what Claude draws itself, in Markdown (the User's words, Claude's words with the source of each proposal, and the tools it calls), and **MCP App** for a frame of Data Hub, with an edge of teal. A switch shows the conversation without the tags, as its User sees it.
- **The start of a journey says where it goes** ([DEC-UX-271](#dec-ux-271)): the window of the first step of a flow through Claude says what the User is about to do and why, what they get on the way (the Learning Cards, with their icon, and Data Hub one click away), the steps ahead, and that everything they write can be changed later, here or in Data Hub; only what cannot be undone, such as a publication, is said to be for good.
- **The overview at hand** ([DEC-UX-272](#dec-ux-272)): every other step of the flow keeps the overview folded on top of its window: the bar of the steps, the step it is, how many are done and how many required ones are still open. Unfolded, it lists every step with what the Resource holds for it, done, still missing, and its mark: required, for the EDA, or optional for an advised step.
- **"How can we help with this step?"** ([DEC-UX-273](#dec-ux-273)): the Learning Cards of a step come folded, under that heading, one short line each: the icon of a Learning Card, its number and its title. A click unfolds it to its core idea with the term in bold, what knowing it brings, and "Read the card", which opens the whole card as its own MCP App, in its five steps ([learning-mcp](screens/learning-mcp.html)). It replaces the strip of [DEC-UX-261](#dec-ux-261).
- **Every link to Data Hub, one form** ([DEC-UX-274](#dec-ux-274)): the mark of Data Hub, its two rings, then "Open … in Data Hub" with what it opens, such as "Open the Known Gaps in Data Hub", then the arrow of a link that leaves. In a step it opens the part of the Overview, or the tab, that the step writes; elsewhere the matching tab ([FR-CL-06](../requirements/functional/17-claude.md#fr-cl-06)). The same form in every MCP App, on a Learning Card and in Claude's words.
- **What a term means** ([DEC-UX-279](#dec-ux-279)): the title of the step carries the ⓘ, and a tap unfolds under it the definitions of its terms, each with its name: at the Business Description and the fields, what the Business Description is beside the description from dbt; at the pitfalls and the Known Gaps, the definition of each, which holds the test that tells them apart. The quiet line that said what a text is for is gone.
- **Fields suggested by Claude, in one card** ([DEC-UX-281](#dec-ux-281), [FR-CL-38](../requirements/functional/17-claude.md#fr-cl-38)): when the User describes the fields of a Resource with Claude, Claude suggests and the card is where the User decides: a row per field with a box "Key Field", the name, its type and its description from dbt, and its Business Description in a box to edit, marked "Suggested", "On the card" or "Edited". On top what the suggestions are based on and a filter: all fields, the suggested ones, the Key Fields. At the foot what "Save to Data Hub" saves; only that click writes. Drawn in [mcp-fields](screens/mcp-fields.html).

## Decisions

All decided on 2026-10-22, in the design session.

- <a id="dec-ux-01"></a>**DEC-UX-01** · No Figma for Data Hub: an internal tool, the design lives in code next to the requirements.
- <a id="dec-ux-02"></a>**DEC-UX-02** · Navigation on the left with a slide-out panel, instead of navigation at the top.
- <a id="dec-ux-03"></a>**DEC-UX-03** · Each section opens on a start screen; the panel is the index. No separate overview screen that repeats the panel.
- <a id="dec-ux-04"></a>**DEC-UX-04** · The "Promoted" feature on the start screen is not built yet.
- <a id="dec-ux-05"></a>**DEC-UX-05** · Background: a gradient of three colours instead of blobs of colour. *Note of 2026-10-24: a picture can take its place ([DEC-UX-34](#dec-ux-34)).*

Decided on 2026-10-22, in the review of the detail page of a Resource against its requirements:

- <a id="dec-ux-06"></a>**DEC-UX-06** · A Resource has no icon of its own. Icons are for the interface and for Collections. *Note of 2026-10-22: the icon of its kind in a list is interface ([DEC-UX-11](#dec-ux-11)).*
- <a id="dec-ux-07"></a>**DEC-UX-07** · The detail page of a Resource does not show its freshness.
- <a id="dec-ux-08"></a>**DEC-UX-08** · "Ask a question" on a Resource starts a Question in its Conversations; it does not open a chat with Claude. *Note of 2026-10-23: it opens the Messages of the Resource in a slide-in, with Question chosen ([DEC-UX-29](#dec-ux-29)).*
- <a id="dec-ux-09"></a>**DEC-UX-09** · The Owner and the Data Team edit what Data Hub keeps about a Resource in place, on its Overview. What comes from dbt is shown next to it, read-only. *Note of 2026-11-04: in place, part by part: each page of the Overview has its own Edit ([DEC-UX-168](#dec-ux-168)).*

Decided on 2026-10-22, after a tryout of the panel:

- <a id="dec-ux-10"></a>**DEC-UX-10** · One panel for Collections and what they hold: a Collection unfolds in place in the list of Collections, so switching Collection is one click. This replaces the second panel with the Resources of one Collection and an arrow back.
- <a id="dec-ux-11"></a>**DEC-UX-11** · In a list, every item has the icon of its kind, the same for every item of that kind: a table for a Resource. It tells the kinds apart once Lenses and Reports join; it is not an icon of the item itself ([DEC-UX-06](#dec-ux-06)).
- <a id="dec-ux-12"></a>**DEC-UX-12** · A Resource that is not published is shown in a lighter grey, without the struck-through eye.

Decided on 2026-10-23, with the page of a Collection and the icon of a Collection:

- <a id="dec-ux-13"></a>**DEC-UX-13** · Clicking a Collection in the panel opens its page and unfolds it; the arrow next to it only folds and unfolds. This answers [OP-UX-14](#op-ux-14).
- <a id="dec-ux-14"></a>**DEC-UX-14** · From the page of a Collection, a User gets an overview of its Resources and its Lenses, creates a Lens, and edits the details of the Collection; a Data Admin also adds Resources. *Note of 2026-10-23: only who owns the Collection creates Lenses in it and edits it ([DEC-CO-15](../requirements/functional/03-collections.md#dec-co-15)).* *Approved on 2026-10-23, as designed in [collection-overview](screens/collection-overview.html).*
- <a id="dec-ux-15"></a>**DEC-UX-15** · The icon of a Collection is grey, without a circle; the colour of the Collection is a thin bar on the left edge of its card, and of the head of its page. Variant E of the exploration; this answers [OP-UX-16](#op-ux-16). *Note of 2026-10-26: on a card, the thin bar becomes a stripe of 5 pixels ([DEC-UX-68](#dec-ux-68)).* *Replaced on 2026-10-28: a Collection has no colour ([DEC-UX-105](#dec-ux-105)).*
- <a id="dec-ux-16"></a>**DEC-UX-16** · "Without a collection" is not a Collection, so it never gets the colour bar of one. It is shown in an urgent red, because it waits for the Data Team and asks to be solved quickly. This answers [OP-UX-17](#op-ux-17). *Note of 2026-11-21: the number waiting stands before its name, and it says it is for Data Admins only ([DEC-UX-335](#dec-ux-335)).*

Decided on 2026-10-23, with the preview and the query of a Resource:

- <a id="dec-ux-17"></a>**DEC-UX-17** · Query is a main section of its own, next to Collections, and not a tab of a Resource: a query goes over Resources, and later Lenses. This answers [OP-SR-11](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#op-sr-11).
- <a id="dec-ux-18"></a>**DEC-UX-18** · Your own queries are not on the page: they slide in as a sheet. *Note of 2026-10-23: replaced by [DEC-UX-20](#dec-ux-20): they are the panel of Query.*
- <a id="dec-ux-19"></a>**DEC-UX-19** · The page of a Resource has a history of the queries that ran on it, in a tab "Queries".
- <a id="dec-ux-20"></a>**DEC-UX-20** · The panel of Query lists the queries that ran, not the Collections: your own by default, everyone's on request. This replaces [DEC-UX-18](#dec-ux-18). *Decided on 2026-10-23: everyone's, under "All", is for the Data Team only, as the Query Log has it ([DEC-LN-01](../requirements/functional/07-log-and-notifications.md#dec-ln-01)).* *Note of 2026-11-19: above them, the Projects of the User ([DEC-UX-285](#dec-ux-285)).*
- <a id="dec-ux-21"></a>**DEC-UX-21** · Every query has a name, given as the first step of a new query and suggested from where it starts; a query on a Lens bears the name of the Lens. In the panel, the icon of a query shows whether it ran on Resources or on a Lens. The page of a Resource has a button "Query" that starts a new query on it, its name already filled in.
- <a id="dec-ux-22"></a>**DEC-UX-22** · A new query shows, next to its name and Resources, the Lenses that already use these Resources: first those on exactly these, then those on these and more. Reusing a Lens is the first choice, "Start your own" the alternative, to prevent double work. The SQL is not on this screen.
- <a id="dec-ux-23"></a>**DEC-UX-23** · The Lenses suggested to a new query match on more than the Resources: on the words its question shares with their name, description and tags, common words left out, and the shared words are marked. No other names are suggested for the query than the one from its Resources and the date.
- <a id="dec-ux-24"></a>**DEC-UX-24** · A Lens shows no weight, light or heavy: whoever asks for data wants the answer, and a label does not stop them. What a query reads is shown in its Pre-flight. [FR-LE-02](../requirements/functional/09-lenses.md#fr-le-02) is withdrawn. Its size is checked once, for its author, when it is made ([FR-LE-08](../requirements/functional/09-lenses.md#fr-le-08)).
- <a id="dec-ux-25"></a>**DEC-UX-25** · "Make it a lens" is a button on a query in Query. It opens a screen with the Lens on the left, filled in from the query, and its size check on the right, which decides how it is published: at once, after a warning, or once the Data Team accepts it.
- <a id="dec-ux-26"></a>**DEC-UX-26** · A Lens runs on a page of its own in the section Lenses, with two tabs. Run: the request on top, its Parameters in a panel that folds open and shut, the Pre-flight and "Run"; below it the result, a table. About: what it answers, what it does, what it is built on, its Parameters and its history. This is the basic flavour, a query with Parameters and a table. *Note of 2026-10-26: the page of a Lens sits under Collections; there is no section Lenses ([DEC-UX-66](#dec-ux-66)).*
- <a id="dec-ux-27"></a>**DEC-UX-27** · The page of a Lens shows no SQL and adds no Parameters. A colleague never sees the SQL of a Lens; its Owner Unit sees it, and adds Parameters, when editing. "Open in Query" is for the Owner Unit only.
- <a id="dec-ux-28"></a>**DEC-UX-28** · On the page of a Lens the Pre-flight is in the "Run" button, in the lower right corner of the Parameters, with what the run reads; it takes no room of its own. Above the approval threshold the button turns red and asks to approve, and when the table is partitioned a pop-out on the button says how to read less.
- <a id="dec-ux-29"></a>**DEC-UX-29** · Messages have a Message Centre of their own, in the rail with the number unread, and every object with Messages, a Collection, a Resource or a Lens, opens its own Messages in a slide-in, which is the Message Centre filtered on that object, with a field to start one.
- <a id="dec-ux-30"></a>**DEC-UX-30** · "Open in context" on a Message opens the page of its object with the Message in a floating window over it, which does not dim the page, so that the User answers with the object in view. It replaces "Open the …" in the Message Centre. The floating window shows one Message; the slide-in of [DEC-UX-29](#dec-ux-29) all Messages on an object.
- <a id="dec-ux-31"></a>**DEC-UX-31** · Publishing and confirming a Resource happen in a sheet over its page. "Publish" is the first time, from Described, and is also the first confirmation; "Reconfirm" after a drop opens the same sheet as "Confirm again". The sheet says who will see the Resource and what Data Hub checked, and asks for the confirmation: the figures it was checked with, since when the data is reliable, and the declaration. *Note of 2026-10-26: the control figures and since when the data is reliable are left out of the sheet ([DEC-MR-07](../requirements/functional/06-maturity-and-release.md#dec-mr-07)), and the declaration moves next to its button ([DEC-UX-63](#dec-ux-63)).* *Note of 2026-10-26: "Reconfirm" and confirming again after a drop are withdrawn ([DEC-UX-64](#dec-ux-64)); only "Publish" opens the sheet.*
- <a id="dec-ux-32"></a>**DEC-UX-32** · A link to something the User may not see opens the same page as a link to something that does not exist, without saying which of the two it is, and with what to do instead. It has no "Ask for access".
- <a id="dec-ux-33"></a>**DEC-UX-33** · The section Lenses opens on a start screen: an intro, a search that finds Lenses by the words of a question, the Lenses of the User and their Owner Unit, and Lenses to discover, popular or new. Its panel holds the Lenses by Collection, as [OP-UX-18](#op-ux-18) proposes. *Note of 2026-10-26: withdrawn with the section Lenses; its parts moved to the start screen of Collections ([DEC-UX-66](#dec-ux-66)).*
- <a id="dec-ux-34"></a>**DEC-UX-34** · A picture can be the background, in place of the gradient: the painted counterpart of a photo ([DEC-UX-37](#dec-ux-37)), drifting as slowly. With a picture, the frames blur what is behind them lightly. The dark theme takes a picture of its own ([DEC-UX-36](#dec-ux-36)). The first pictures are ski slopes. *Note of 2026-11-04: the paintings left the set; Concrete is the one picture left ([DEC-UX-173](#dec-ux-173)).* *Note of 2026-11-11: Concrete left too, so the sets hold no picture ([DEC-UX-208](#dec-ux-208)).*
- <a id="dec-ux-35"></a>**DEC-UX-35** · Signing in shows a photo of the set as it is, with the sign-in on a glass card that blurs what lies behind it: the mark and the name, "Sign in", one sentence and "Sign in with Google". In a corner, on glass too, the story of the photo. A refused account is named on the card, with the button to try another. *Note of 2026-11-04: the sign-in shows a background of the set picked at random, without a story ([DEC-UX-173](#dec-ux-173)).*
- <a id="dec-ux-36"></a>**DEC-UX-36** · The pictures come as sets, each photo with its story: a title, a few lines and who took it. Max provides the photos and writes the stories. The light theme and the dark theme each have a set of their own: a photo is never darkened for the dark theme. Until the dark set has a photo, the dark theme keeps its gradient. *Note of 2026-10-24: the dark set has its first photo, a blue-hour photo from Max made into a night picture with its lights on, at the request of Max.* *Note of 2026-11-04: the photos with their stories left the sets ([DEC-UX-173](#dec-ux-173)).*
- <a id="dec-ux-37"></a>**DEC-UX-37** · Each photo of a set has a painted counterpart, a watercolour made from the photo itself: the photo as it is for signing in, the painting for the background of the app. Both carry the same story. The dark set works the same way: its own photos, painted, never darkened ones. *Note of 2026-11-04: no painted photo is left in the sets ([DEC-UX-173](#dec-ux-173)).*
- <a id="dec-ux-38"></a>**DEC-UX-38** · Learning is a main section, under Reports in the rail, with a start screen and a panel that is the index of every Learning Card: the Compass first, then the four Learning Levels from easy to advanced, each unfolding to its sections and their cards. The panel shows how many cards the User understood, per Learning Level and per section, and per card whether they did. *Designed on 2026-10-24, at the request of Max, to review.* *Note of 2026-10-24: the section is called the learning centre ([DEC-LR-04](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#dec-lr-04)).* *Note of 2026-10-25: the panel counts the cards seen, not understood, and its progress card makes way for one silent count ([DEC-UX-51](#dec-ux-51)).* *Note of 2026-10-25: the learning centre leaves the main items, and sits at the bottom of the rail next to the account ([DEC-UX-52](#dec-ux-52)).*
- <a id="dec-ux-39"></a>**DEC-UX-39** · In the interface a Learning Level is named by its audience with its step, "1 · User" to "4 · Data owner and expert", with a mark of four short bars from easy to advanced. The word "Level" alone stays for the maturity of a Resource, Lens or Report, and a Learning Level gets no colour of its own. *Designed on 2026-10-24, to review.* *Note of 2026-10-26: the mark of four bars is replaced by an icon per Learning Level, as it looked like the maturity icon ([DEC-UX-62](#dec-ux-62)).*
- <a id="dec-ux-40"></a>**DEC-UX-40** · The page of a Learning Card reads as one column: what you will be able to do, the article with its graphics, the common mistake, the examples, the video, then the questions to check yourself. What comes before and after is beside it. "Mark as understood" is the main button of its header. *Designed on 2026-10-24, to review.* *Note of 2026-10-25: replaced by [DEC-UX-45](#dec-ux-45): the card is worked through in steps, and what comes before and after moved to Congrats.*
- <a id="dec-ux-41"></a>**DEC-UX-41** · A Message can carry a Learning Card, which shows in it as a small card with whether the reader understood it. A Learning Card has Messages of its own, like every object. *Designed on 2026-10-24, to review.*
- <a id="dec-ux-42"></a>**DEC-UX-42** · A link to a Learning Card stands inline in a text, underlined in teal with a small graduation cap. A click opens the card in a floating window over the page, as a Message in context ([DEC-UX-30](#dec-ux-30)), with "Mark as understood" and "Open in the learning centre". *Designed on 2026-10-24, at the request of Max, to review.* *Note of 2026-10-25: the window shows the steps of the card ([DEC-UX-47](#dec-ux-47)), and the button is "I understand it" ([DEC-UX-48](#dec-ux-48)).* *Note of 2026-10-25: the teal underline and the graduation cap make way for a marker and a pop-out ([DEC-UX-54](#dec-ux-54), [DEC-UX-55](#dec-ux-55)); the floating window stays.*
- <a id="dec-ux-43"></a>**DEC-UX-43** · The game elements of the learning centre are calm. They are progress, a Rank per Learning Level, a Weekly Goal as a ring, a Streak in weeks, Badges as round line marks in teal, Quests and team progress without names. A success is a quiet note in the lower left. There are no points, no ranking of colleagues and no confetti. *Designed on 2026-10-24, at the request of Max, to review.* *Note of 2026-10-25: withdrawn: no scores, badges, rankings or deadlines, only a silent count of the cards seen ([DEC-LR-09](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#dec-lr-09), [DEC-UX-51](#dec-ux-51)).*
- <a id="dec-ux-44"></a>**DEC-UX-44** · Every User has a Learning Profile, opened from the progress card at the top of the panel of the learning centre and from its start screen. *Designed on 2026-10-24, to review.* *Note of 2026-10-25: withdrawn with the Learning Profile ([FR-LR-18](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#fr-lr-18)).*
- <a id="dec-ux-45"></a>**DEC-UX-45** · The page of a Learning Card shows one step at a time, with the four steps in a row above it and an arrow between each. Its header holds only the title and "Mark as understood"; there are no labels. Every step ends with "Next" in a bar held to the foot of the window, always in the same place, saying what comes next and how this step helps there. *Designed on 2026-10-25, at the request of Max, to review.* *Note of 2026-10-25: five steps, with a quick and a full path; the button is "I understand it"; "Next" never waits for an answer, and the last step offers choices instead ([DEC-UX-48](#dec-ux-48), [DEC-UX-49](#dec-ux-49)).*
- <a id="dec-ux-46"></a>**DEC-UX-46** · Congrats is where a card celebrates: a drawing of two overlapping circles with a check, the Knowledge Matrix with the card in it, related subjects and next topics. The drawing moves once, only when motion is allowed, and there is no confetti. *Designed on 2026-10-25, at the request of Max, to review.* *Note of 2026-10-25: replaced by [DEC-UX-49](#dec-ux-49): the last step is You can now, without the Knowledge Matrix. Whether the drawing stays is open ([OP-LR-21](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#op-lr-21)).*
- <a id="dec-ux-47"></a>**DEC-UX-47** · Each step of a Learning Card is a block that stands alone, so the page, the floating window and the MCP App in Claude show the same steps; only what is around them differs. *Designed on 2026-10-25, at the request of Max, to review.*
- <a id="dec-ux-48"></a>**DEC-UX-48** · A Learning Card has its five steps in the row above it, Why now to You can now, with a switch between the quick and the full path next to them; on the quick path the two middle steps are muted, not hidden. The page uses its whole width. "I understand it" is the only button of the header. "Next" never waits for an answer. *Designed on 2026-10-25, from the principles page, to review.*
- <a id="dec-ux-49"></a>**DEC-UX-49** · You can now has three parts: what the User can now do, large; what it brings them; and two or three choices as tiles: deeper, a neighbouring concept, and "Just apply it". It has no score, no badge and no single next card. *Designed on 2026-10-25, from the principles page, to review.*
- <a id="dec-ux-50"></a>**DEC-UX-50** · See it is a page of its own: the third step of a card, and reachable from a context link, such as "See it" with an eye next to a pitfall on a Resource. On its own it shows the example, "Look at …" and "You see …", the way back and the whole card. *Designed on 2026-10-25, from the principles page, to review.* *Note of 2026-10-25: from context, it opens from a row of the pop-out ([DEC-UX-55](#dec-ux-55)).*
- <a id="dec-ux-51"></a>**DEC-UX-51** · The learning centre shows one silent count: the cards a User has seen, on top of the panel and per Learning Level and section. No ring, no streak, no badge, no red dot. *Designed on 2026-10-25, from the principles page, to review.*
- <a id="dec-ux-52"></a>**DEC-UX-52** · The learning centre sits at the bottom of the rail, just above the account, not among the main items. *Decided on 2026-10-25, at the request of Max; this answers [OP-UX-21](#op-ux-21).* *Note of 2026-10-29: replaced by [DEC-UX-89](#dec-ux-89): the learning centre is a main item on top ([OP-UX-37](#op-ux-37)).*
- <a id="dec-ux-53"></a>**DEC-UX-53** · The rail has no button for settings. *Decided on 2026-10-25, at the request of Max.*
- <a id="dec-ux-54"></a>**DEC-UX-54** · What the learning centre explains carries a small marker right after it, ⓘ in the colour of its text, on a phrase or on a button. It is the only sign: no underline, no colour. *Designed on 2026-10-25, at the request of Max, to review; the character is open ([OP-UX-22](#op-ux-22)).* *Note of 2026-10-25: only on system text ([DEC-UX-56](#dec-ux-56)).* *Note of 2026-10-25: the character is decided, ⓘ ([OP-UX-22](#op-ux-22)).*
- <a id="dec-ux-55"></a>**DEC-UX-55** · Hovering or focusing a marked phrase or button opens a pop-out with one or more cards: for each, why or how it helps the User here, its number and title, and whether and when the User saw it. A card opens in the floating window, See it on its own. *Designed on 2026-10-25, at the request of Max, to review.* *Note of 2026-10-25: replaced by [DEC-UX-57](#dec-ux-57), after the review by Max.*
- <a id="dec-ux-56"></a>**DEC-UX-56** · Only system text carries the marker: a button, a label, a chip or a status. Text that Users provide never does. *Decided on 2026-10-25, at the request of Max.*
- <a id="dec-ux-57"></a>**DEC-UX-57** · The pop-out is a text bubble whose arrow points at its marker, and opens after the pointer rests on the text for a second. It says how many cards explain the text, and shows each as a tile: number and title, "See it" on every item, the gain of the card, and whether and when the User saw it. A click on an item opens the card in the floating window over the page. This replaces [DEC-UX-55](#dec-ux-55). *Designed on 2026-10-25, at the request of Max, to review.*
- <a id="dec-ux-58"></a>**DEC-UX-58** · Adding Resources is a sheet in two steps: choose the Resources, then the relation of every field. The second step lists the fields of each Resource with a choice of "No relation", "Identifies each row" or "Refers to", and the Entity identifier, filled in from the docs of a Colleciónes model or suggested from the name of the field, and says how many Resources each reference links to. *Designed on 2026-10-25, at the request of Max, to review.* *Note of 2026-10-25: the second step also shows how each Resource is stored, its partition column, cluster columns and whether a partition filter is required, derived from dbt and set by the Data Admin ([FR-CO-34](../requirements/functional/03-collections.md#fr-co-34)).* *Note of 2026-10-26: the second step is now "Grain and relations": per Resource its Grain next to its storage, and per field a mapping, nothing, `is` or a relation type of the Domain Model, with the identity and the cardinality shown, never chosen ([FR-CO-33](../requirements/functional/03-collections.md#fr-co-33), [DEC-RC-05](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#dec-rc-05) to [DEC-RC-08](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#dec-rc-08)).*
- <a id="dec-ux-59"></a>**DEC-UX-59** · A name on a screen, such as `creditLead` or `visitorId`, carries the marker ⓘ like other system text, and its pop-out lists at least its own Learning Card, a minimal one if need be ([FR-LR-37](../requirements/functional/16-learning.md#fr-lr-37)). *Decided on 2026-10-25, by Max; not designed in the prototypes yet.*
- <a id="dec-ux-60"></a>**DEC-UX-60** · The Collection palette lives in one configuration file, to which colours are added over time, each with its name and its value for the light and the dark theme. It starts with the colours there are now: the six of this design, and the nine of the muted palette that existing Collections use. It is tuned after testing on uat. *Decided on 2026-10-25, by Max ([OP-UX-05](#op-ux-05), [OP-UX-08](#op-ux-08)).* *Replaced on 2026-10-28: a Collection has no colour ([DEC-UX-105](#dec-ux-105)).*
- <a id="dec-ux-61"></a>**DEC-UX-61** · The two descriptions of a Resource and of each field, the Business Description and the description from dbt, are shown side by side, each with where it comes from: the Business Description on the left, from the Owner; the description from dbt on the right. Neither is ever shown as replacing the other. On a narrow screen the Business Description comes first ([DEC-RC-01](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#dec-rc-01), [DEC-RC-02](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#dec-rc-02)). *Note of 2026-11-04: on Description, the Business Description, in Markdown, stands above the description from dbt, as it needs the width; while it is edited, the two stand side by side ([DEC-UX-168](#dec-ux-168)).*
- <a id="dec-ux-62"></a>**DEC-UX-62** · Each Learning Level has an icon of its own, for its audience: `user`, `user-focus`, `chart-scatter` and `user-gear`. The bars of the maturity icon belong to the maturity of a Resource, Lens or Report alone, and no other mark looks like them or is filled up to a step. *Decided on 2026-10-26, by Max: the maturity icon is not reused.*
- <a id="dec-ux-63"></a>**DEC-UX-63** · A declaration that a button acts on, such as confirming a Resource, stands in one bar with that button: the checkbox and its sentence on the left, the button at the right end. The button is off until the box is ticked, so it is clear that they are one. *Decided on 2026-10-26, by Max.*
- <a id="dec-ux-64"></a>**DEC-UX-64** · A change to the schema of a Resource since its last Confirmation shows as a warning on its page, for everyone, in the signal colour: what changed, when, and that the Level stays. "Mark as seen", for the Owner and the Data Team, takes it away. There is no Reconfirm and no sheet to confirm again ([DEC-MR-08](../requirements/functional/06-maturity-and-release.md#dec-mr-08)). *Decided on 2026-10-26, by Max.*
- <a id="dec-ux-65"></a>**DEC-UX-65** · An unfolded Collection in the panel shows what it holds in a group per kind: first its Resources, then its Lenses, each with the icon of its kind and its Level, without a label for the group: the icon is clear. Reports join later as a third group. Not one mixed list, which is hard to scan. *Decided on 2026-10-26, by Max ([OP-UX-15](#op-ux-15)).*
- <a id="dec-ux-66"></a>**DEC-UX-66** · Lenses have no section of their own, and no item in the rail: everything is reached through its Collection. A Lens is listed in its Collection, in the panel and on the page of the Collection; its page sits under Collections. The start screen of Collections finds Lenses too, by the words of a question, with the User's own Lenses and Lenses to discover ([FR-CO-37](../requirements/functional/03-collections.md#fr-co-37)). *Decided on 2026-10-26, by Max.*
- <a id="dec-ux-67"></a>**DEC-UX-67** · A Resource has no tab Conversations. Its Messages open in the slide-in from "Messages" in its header, as on every object, and "Ask a question" opens the same slide-in with Question chosen. *Decided on 2026-10-26, by Max ([OP-UX-19](#op-ux-19)).*
- <a id="dec-ux-68"></a>**DEC-UX-68** · The colour of a Collection is a stripe of 5 pixels on the left edge of its card, rounded only at its top-left and bottom-left corners, as the card is, with square corners on its right side. It stays the same whether the Collection is open, unfolded or not; a card is never filled with the colour. *Decided on 2026-10-26, by Max, after a test of a 10-pixel stripe and of a card filled with its colour when selected, and 7 pixels first.* *Replaced on 2026-10-28: a Collection has no colour ([DEC-UX-105](#dec-ux-105)).*
- <a id="dec-ux-69"></a>**DEC-UX-69** · Publishing a Resource that is not Described is possible once a Business Description covers every column dbt does not describe: the sheet names those columns and whether each is covered, and "Publish and confirm" comes on when all are. Such a Resource shows Confirmed with a small mark next to its Level until dbt has the descriptions ([DEC-MR-09](../requirements/functional/06-maturity-and-release.md#dec-mr-09)). *Decided on 2026-10-26, by Max.* *Note of 2026-10-28: withdrawn by Max: a Resource without its descriptions from dbt cannot be published ([DEC-MR-10](../requirements/functional/06-maturity-and-release.md#dec-mr-10)).*
- <a id="dec-ux-70"></a>**DEC-UX-70** · The editor of Query has the full width of the page, in a box of its own, and folds every CTE to its first line, as a code editor does. The Pre-flight is no card next to it: it is in a bar at the foot of the query, with "Run" and what the query reads in the button, as on the page of a Lens ([DEC-UX-28](#dec-ux-28)); the bar stays in view while the SQL scrolls. *Decided on 2026-10-27, by Max, after a test of a query built in steps, each CTE a block with a name and a description, which he found too much.*
