- [x] <a id="op-ux-80"></a>**OP-UX-80** · **The rules of Format** ([Query](#query)). Max asked where they are written, and to review them. Today they are only in the code (`SqlEditor.svelte`): sql-formatter for BigQuery, keywords in lower case, functions and types as written, an indent of 2 spaces, a function call longer than 50 characters split over a line per argument, `@name` as a Parameter. Proposal: the house layout of the Data Team for dbt (`docs/sql-formatting.md` in immoscoop-dataplatform-data), as far as a formatter goes: keywords, functions and types in lower case, an indent of 4 spaces, each clause keyword on a line of its own with its operands under it, one per line, and a function call kept on one line up to 100 characters. sql-formatter cannot do four rules of that layout by itself: `with` on a line of its own and the CTEs not indented, `where` and `qualify` starting with `true`, a date part such as `DAY` in lower case, and the space in `interval (vork + 1)`, which it drops. A small step after it does the first, third and fourth; `where true` stays for the templates ([DEC-UX-141](#dec-ux-141)), as adding it changes what the User wrote. *Raised on 2026-11-13, while answering Max.* *Decided on 2026-11-13, by Max: "agree, lowercase" ([DEC-UX-228](#dec-ux-228)).*
- [x] <a id="op-ux-81"></a>**OP-UX-81** · **What "Find a table" searches** ([DEC-UX-224](#dec-ux-224)). Max asked for "tables in the bigquery projects". Proposal: the tables Data Hub knows from the dbt projects it follows, as registry keeps them ([DEC-SR-46](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#dec-sr-46)): those are the tables a power user's query may read, so the sheet offers nothing the Pre-flight then refuses, and each comes with its layer, description and columns from dbt. Every table of the BigQuery projects would also list tables of no dbt project, such as raw exports and scratch tables, which the query cannot read ([FR-SR-56](../requirements/functional/04-stacks-and-resources.md#fr-sr-56)) and which have no description. *Raised on 2026-11-13, while designing it.* *Decided on 2026-11-13, by Max: as proposed, "agree".*

Later, what the User opened last could top the list when nothing is typed. *Raised on 2026-11-13, in the design of the [quick window](#quick-window).* *Decided on 2026-11-13, by Max: "agree on all open points, build it".*
- [x] <a id="op-ux-89"></a>**OP-UX-89** · **What the window of a table outside Data Hub shows, and from where** ([Query](#query), [DEC-UX-221](#dec-ux-221)). Max asked for an info window for such a table and its fields, with "what is in the description, metadata such as table or view, partitions, and the field descriptions and field types", if BigQuery can give it. Both can: registry already keeps from dbt its description, its layer, its partition column and the type and description of each column as the last run of its Source Job had them, and query already reads from BigQuery what a table is, its size and partitions, for the storage of a Resource. Proposal: both, each for what it knows best. From dbt, by registry: its description, layer, project and how dbt makes it. From BigQuery, by query at the moment the window opens: its kind, partitioning (column, unit, required filter), cluster columns, size, rows, partitions and last change, and the type of each column, nested fields included; a read of its metadata, which BigQuery does not bill, made with the account of Data Hub, only for a table Data Hub knows and only for a User who may query tables outside Data Hub. The description of a column comes from dbt, or from BigQuery where dbt has none (dbt writes its descriptions into BigQuery, so they are mostly the same). When BigQuery does not answer, what dbt knew, said as such. The alternative: dbt only, without a call to BigQuery, so without size, rows, partitions or last change, and its kind guessed from how dbt makes it. Drawn in [query](screens/query.html), the switch Double-click. *Raised on 2026-11-14, in the design of this window.* *Decided on 2026-11-14, by Max: "dbt + BigQuery", the proposal, of the two options Claude put ([DEC-UX-247](#dec-ux-247)).*

The alternative: also "Open in BigQuery", a link to the table in the BigQuery console, for a power user who has access there. *Raised on 2026-11-14, in the design of this window.* *Decided on 2026-11-14, by Max: "Copy the full name", the proposal ([DEC-UX-247](#dec-ux-247)).*

- [x] <a id="op-ux-107"></a>**OP-UX-107** · **The copy of a result as a menu button** ([DEC-UX-292](#dec-ux-292)). Max asked to put Copy as JSON and Copy as CSV "under a 'sub menu' … a button with an icon, when clicked opens a menu with these 2 options", the design first, as a new component. Proposal: a **menu button**, a new component of its own ([components](components.md#4--containers-and-layers)): an icon button with the icon of what its choices do, here the copy icon, and a small caret that says it opens a menu; its name, "Copy the result", on hover and for a screen reader. A click, Enter, Space or the down arrow opens its menu under it, aligned to its right edge, with the first choice focused; the arrows move through the choices, Home and End jump, Enter does one and closes the menu; Escape, Tab or a click beside it closes it, and the focus goes back to the button, pressed while its menu is open. The menu has a line on top that says what the choices act on, then each choice with its icon and a hint: Copy as JSON, "an object a row"; Copy as CSV, "a comma, a header row". The toast after a copy and the commands of the quick window stay. *Raised on 2026-11-20, while designing it.* *Decided on 2026-11-20, by Max: "ok voor beide, bouwen maar" ("ok for both, go ahead and build") ([DEC-UX-294](#dec-ux-294)).*
- [x] <a id="op-ux-108"></a>**OP-UX-108** · **Stop working in a Project from the panel** ([DEC-UX-282](#dec-ux-282)). Max: "we should be able to select much more simply that you are no longer working in a project … in the left menu, somewhere 'close project' or so", and a new query not made from the Project in no Project. Proposal: in the panel, the Project the User works in comes first among the Projects, with a line under it: the flag, "You work in it", and a quiet button **Close project**, with on hover what it does: the row on top shows the flagged queries again, a new query is in no Project, and the Project stays as it is. "Close" stops working in it, as closing a project in a code editor does; it deletes nothing and nobody else is touched. A new query is in no Project already today unless it is made in one (New query on its page, New query in it in the quick window): the rules say so now, and putting it in one stays the User's own act: "Add to …" at the end of the row, Add to a project, or New project with it ticked. *Raised on 2026-11-20, while designing it.* *Decided on 2026-11-20, by Max: "ok voor beide, bouwen maar" ("ok for both, go ahead and build") ([DEC-UX-295](#dec-ux-295)).* *Changed on 2026-11-20: there is no Close project; the active Project follows what is open ([DEC-UX-317](#dec-ux-317)).*
- [x] <a id="op-ux-109"></a>**OP-UX-109** · **The tile of Reliable since, its line and its form** ([DEC-UX-296](#dec-ux-296)). Proposal, as drawn in [resource-overview](screens/resource-overview.html) (`?ov=gaps`, `&rs=start`, `&rs=empty`, `&rs=edit`, `&rs=odd`): the title carries the ⓘ with its definition and K5.17, rather than a line under it that says what it means, as [DEC-UX-279](#dec-ux-279) chose for every term; the day large, "on `week_start`, its period" beside it, "Why not earlier" and its text, who set it and when. The line runs from its first row to today: before Reliable since shaded with fine stripes, the day marked with a stroke and its date; it draws no Known Gaps. "Change" opens the form in place, with From its first row and From a later day as two choices; the Date Field is a list of its date fields, its table type's one first with what it is; another shows the warning in the signal colour; Why not earlier is required for a later day. Not set: "Not set yet", required to publish, and the question with its two choices, neither chosen, and Save off until one is. *Note of 2026-11-20, from Max's first look: Not set showed From its first row as a button with a check, which read as already chosen ("I want a difference between null and one of the options, where we explicitly ask for one of the options"), and the numbers 1 and 3 on the line, two Known Gaps, were unclear; Not set is drawn again as said here, and the line draws no gaps ([DEC-UX-296](#dec-ux-296)).* The Summary moves the line of Reliable since from Watch out to the part of the Known Gaps, renamed "Reliable since, Known Gaps". In Claude, the Owner Journey gives it a step of its own, with the same terms under the ⓘ and what the data says, its first row and the rows per day of its first weeks. *Raised on 2026-11-20, while designing Reliable Since.* *Decided on 2026-11-20, by Max: as proposed ([DEC-UX-297](#dec-ux-297)).*
- [x] <a id="op-ux-110"></a>**OP-UX-110** · **One day or a period, for the period of a Known Gap** ([DEC-RC-30](../requirements/functional/05-resource-card.md#dec-rc-30)). Max proposed a check mark "end date same as start date", or saying "it's a period or it's a given date". Proposal, as drawn in [resource-overview](screens/resource-overview.html) (`?ov=gaps&gapform=open&gapwhen=day`): the second, as two choices on top of the block Period, **One day** and **A period**, A period first chosen, as before, with its dates right behind the choice: one date for One day, From until for A period, a side left empty for from the start or until now. A choice says what is meant before a date is typed, where a check mark would come after typing two. *Note of 2026-11-20, from Max on the drawing: the date right behind One day, From until behind A period, and no "Reads as" line; drawn so.* *Raised on 2026-11-20, by Max.* *Decided on 2026-11-20, by Max: as proposed ([DEC-UX-298](#dec-ux-298)).*
- [x] <a id="op-ux-111"></a>**OP-UX-111** · **The look of the Flow Box, its strip and the warning of Publish, and the texts of its steps** ([DEC-UX-299](#dec-ux-299)). The strip 56 px wide, with the count and a dot per step. The pop-out of Publish under its button. The What, Why and How of the seven steps as written there, in English; the Dutch follows in the build. *Raised on 2026-11-20, while designing the Flow Box.* *Decided on 2026-11-20, by Max: "agree" ([DEC-UX-305](#dec-ux-305)).*

- [x] <a id="op-ux-119"></a>**OP-UX-119** · **The detail of an EDA-Probe, and the list to add one** ([EDA](#eda)). The empty tile of an EDA-Step opens the list of EDA-Probes. To confirm: a field without a rule is not covered; a field mapped to an Entity is left to Values without a match; Data Hub never invents a highest number. The fourth, semgraph, is decided: its charts are illustrations, not semgraph ([DEC-UX-325](#dec-ux-325)). The list to add an EDA-Probe remains.* *Note of 2026-11-21: adding a rule is drawn in the same screen, on request of Max: each field ends with Add a rule, which opens the floating window of a rule, the same as the window of a Parameter of a query ([Floating window](#floating-window)): the field, the rules its kind of field can have as a grid, those already in its list marked, a blank where the rule has one, an optional condition and how it reads; an existing rule opens in it to change or remove; a rule can get unless or only when another field has a value; what no sentence says, the Data Team writes in SQL, kept in the EDA and read as a sentence, the Owner asking for it with a Message. A rule added runs with Run again, and the EDA-Probe stays complete (`?rule=menu`, `?rule=added`).* *Decided on 2026-11-21, by Max: the list is a sheet with a search ([DEC-UX-328](#dec-ux-328)). Of the three points of Impossible values to confirm, Data Hub never inventing a highest number is decided ([DEC-ED-28](../requirements/functional/15-eda.md#dec-ed-28)); the other two moved to [OP-ED-10](../requirements/functional/15-eda.md#op-ed-10).*
- [ ] <a id="op-ux-120"></a>**OP-UX-120** · **How a Key or a table name fits** ([Long names](#long-names)). Proposal: cut the start (3). Otherwise "Sleutelveld" and "Valkuil" stay as Dutch names, written as terms, with their capital and the ⓘ. *Raised on 2026-11-25, with [DEC-UX-340](#dec-ux-340), after Max: "Sleutelveld also a term".* *Decided on 2026-11-25, by Max: "Go", every open point as proposed ([DEC-UX-344](#dec-ux-344)).*
- [x] <a id="op-ux-127"></a>**OP-UX-127** · **A term in a row that is a link or a button** ([DEC-UX-340](#dec-ux-340)). Some rows are one link or one button as a whole: a row of the list of EDA-Probes, a step of the Flow Box, a tab of the Overview. An ⓘ cannot sit inside them without nesting one control in another, and right after them it lands at the far end of the row. Proposal: such a row writes its term with its capital but carries no ⓘ; the page or the step it opens explains it, where the term stands as a heading with its ⓘ, as "The Grain ⓘ holds" on the page of the EDA-Probe. *Raised on 2026-11-25, with [DEC-UX-340](#dec-ux-340), after Max asked whether it works on fields that are links.* *Decided on 2026-11-25, by Max: "Go", every open point as proposed ([DEC-UX-345](#dec-ux-345)).*

Proposal: it leaves the page; the head ends with two quiet buttons with only an icon, Archive (the box) and the bin, after Share and New query, and the bin opens the Danger zone in a sheet, as every task beside a page opens ([DEC-UX-174](#dec-ux-174)): Leave it, and Delete it with what follows, its reason and "Delete for good", in the sheet itself rather than a second sheet; on top a line points to Archive for who only wants it out of the way. Leave next to your name, in "In it" and in Share, opens the same sheet. The Danger zone of a Resource stays a part of its Overview ([DEC-UX-176](#dec-ux-176)): a Resource has a left-hand navigation where it belongs, a Project does not. *Raised on 2026-11-26, by Max, in Dutch: "the danger zone is still text under the project info; I don't find that graphically ok and would move it behind a button and then a slide-in, for example, a very simple button, only a delete icon or something like that".* *Look at: [query-project](screens/query-project.html), with ?danger=open for the sheet.* *Decided on 2026-11-26, by Max, in Dutch: "build everything first and then you may merge", as proposed ([DEC-UX-357](#dec-ux-357)).*
- [x] <a id="op-ux-129"></a>**OP-UX-129** · **Archive a Project, and All projects** ([Projects](#projects)). Max asks to "deactivate" a Project so that it leaves the panel, and to find it again through a quieter item. Proposal: the word **Archive** ("Archiveren", "Gearchiveerd" in Dutch), as in mail: out of the way, not gone. Not "inactive", as the *active* Project already means the one open, whose row is on top ([DEC-UX-317](#dec-ux-317)); an "inactive" Project would read as merely not open. Other words: "Put away" ("Opbergen"), or "Hide", as the eye on a query ([DEC-UX-137](#dec-ux-137)). Archive at once and without asking, as Back in your panel undoes it, from the box in its head, on hover in the panel, or the quick window. Under the Projects in the panel, a quiet line "All projects · 2 archived", which opens **All projects**: every Project the User is in, with a search and the chips In your panel, Archived and All, opening on Archived from that line; an archived one quieter, with when it was archived and "Back in your panel". *Raised on 2026-11-26, by Max, in Dutch: "I would like to be able to 'deactivate' projects so that I no longer see them in the bar on the left, but can bring them up in a more subtle item on the left 'inactive' (you may propose the name), so a button comes under 'all projects' where you get an overview and can find your project again".* *Look at: [query-projects](screens/query-projects.html), the panel of [query](screens/query.html).* *Decided on 2026-11-26, by Max: Archive, as proposed ([DEC-UX-354](#dec-ux-354)).*
- [x] <a id="op-ux-130"></a>**OP-UX-130** · **Archived for whom** ([OP-UX-129](#op-ux-129)). A Project is shared: everyone in it has every right on it ([DEC-UX-286](#dec-ux-286)). Proposal: archiving is for the User alone, as hiding a query is: it leaves their panel and stays in that of the others, who are not told, and All projects says who still has it in theirs. The other way: archived for everyone in it, which the others would then have to be told about, with a Message, as when it is deleted ([OP-UX-100](#op-ux-100)). *Raised on 2026-11-26, with [OP-UX-129](#op-ux-129).* *Decided on 2026-11-26, by Max: only your own panel, as proposed ([DEC-UX-355](#dec-ux-355)).*
- [x] <a id="op-ux-131"></a>**OP-UX-131** · **An archived Project opened again** ([OP-UX-129](#op-ux-129), [DEC-UX-317](#dec-ux-317)). It can still be opened: from All projects, a link, or a query of it in the history, which makes its Project active ([DEC-UX-317](#dec-ux-317)). Proposal: it stays archived; while it is open it is active, its row on top and in the panel, with on its page "You archived it" and "Back in your panel", and it leaves the panel again once no query of it is open.
